# Changelog

All notable changes to **minimaCore Desktop** — the cross-platform (macOS / Windows / Linux) Electron app that runs
a full minimaCore node locally with a native-styled wallet + module suite. Newest first. Each release is tagged
`vX.Y.Z`; since 0.13.0 the installers (`.dmg` / `.exe` / `.AppImage`) are built on GitHub Actions and attached to the
matching [GitHub Release](../../releases).

---

## 0.20.00 · Keets peers on the shared server port

Pandamonium now validates incoming Keets peers when the Minima node and Parlons relay share the public port. Classic P2P, direct Parlons clients and Keets continue to use their existing connection paths.

## 0.19.456 · Incoming Keets greetings

The Keets bridge now keeps every part of an incoming greeting while it opens the connection to the local node. This lets Minima validate peers whose greeting arrives in several chunks.

## 0.19.455 · Live incoming connections

The Node tab shows open incoming classic P2P peers and Keets Minima tunnels separately. The Keets counter drops when either end of the tunnel closes. Router guidance is labeled Classic P2P.

## 0.19.454 · Host resync progress and StateNFT artwork

Resync node shows the remote connection check, coin-proof count, live node log and final result. NFTwallet recognizes embedded SVG edition artwork.

## 0.19.453 · Snapshot import on first run

The automatic MegaMMR memory setting is 7168 MB. Setup distinguishes the wallet sync host from the selected snapshot provider, displays the downloaded filename, and gives the key-use field a visible border.

## 0.19.452 · Restore with recovery words alone

Onboarding derives and checks all 64 classic and 32 block-based addresses for key reuse. The results show the number checked in each family. Users can start the audit with their recovery words alone.

## 0.19.451 · Guided Pandamonium setup

First-run setup checks restored addresses and signing counters, guides classic wallets into minimaWallet for a reviewed sweep, and imports a checked MegaMMR snapshot. New setups offer classic P2P server mode with Keets Chat, router guidance and Keets transport fallback.

## 0.19.450 · Wallet balances, sweep and NFT Wallet navigation

minimaWallet opens on combined Minima and token balances across its derived addresses. Payments can use coins held under multiple keys. Settings can sweep spendable assets to a verified main-node address. Each independent signing key requires a typed next-use count.

NFT Wallet uses the Android wallet layouts for its navigation, Quick Send, Coins, balances, gallery, mint hub and receiving addresses. Selected coins stay in Coins and Coin Control. Display precision applies to lists. Payment confirmations retain exact amounts.

## 0.19.449 · minimaWallet and NFTwallet

Pandamonium Desktop adds both wallets to the app tray. minimaWallet starts with the node seed and classic keys. Reset it to import an independent seed with classic or block-based keys. Local payments require a typed key-use count. When the node owns the key, the Wallet tab handles the payment.

NFTwallet follows the current node wallet. It supports the gallery, favourites, transfers, irreversible burial, token and NFT creation, and locked-edition collections. Coin control includes split sends, consolidation and distributions in batches of up to 14 recipients. Quick send allows an optional Minima burn with a separate confirmation of the exact amount.

## 0.19.448 · Web Wallet key-use verification

Opening Send checks the archive estimate and shows the next use saved on this computer. Each payment requires a typed count at least as high as both figures. The local signer checks again before reserving that use. If the archive is unavailable or the key is exhausted, sending stays disabled.

## 0.19.447 · Pandamonium Desktop Web Wallet

The Web Wallet derives the classic address and signs payments locally in both key modes. The MegaMMR node supplies public coin proofs, validates the signed transaction and broadcasts it. If the native wallet already owns the classic key, the Web Wallet shows its address and balance and opens the Wallet tab for spending.

## [0.19.446] — KeetChat follows the Keets transport setting

- With Keets off, Chat disappears and old notifications cannot open it. Enabling Keets at the next node start brings Chat back with its existing history.

## [0.19.445] — The Parlons Node answers eight commands at once (was two)

- The cause under the stall of 24 Nov 2026 (0.19.443, 0.19.444): the Parlons Node's admin RPC, through which this app sends every command, had two worker threads. Any two slow commands held both, and every other command (a balance, a chat send, a trading engine's read, `quit`) waited until its own time ran out. It has eight now. The node's own RPC server gives every request its own thread, so commands are safe side by side; this app still sends its signing commands one at a time.
- Pandamonium flavour only: `parlons-node.jar` is the 0.19.441 jar with ONE class replaced (com/eurobuddha/maxima/node/AdminRpc.class, from maxima security/cmd-inject-2026-11-21-block @ 491af6c3024887c35b7adf820a2c544001e0dfcb); every other entry byte-identical. sha256 85440d40169a30a4cf55ccb2546c63b07de28fa083fc962242f01bcec62e0692 (was 30db5baafa48695a6d9769160ee2d2c8a97fbd84a0d4f075af712d8c8ef7ae5a). minima.jar is unchanged. The released flavour's jar still has two.
- Test, in the node: AdminRpcTest.twoSlowRequestsDoNotHoldUpAThird (fails with two workers). scripts/flavor-test.cjs pins the new jar.

## [0.19.444] — Chat photos: one wait per offline sender, photos on disk at once, a click really retries; receipts one at a time

- Independent review of 0.19.443 ("safe to install; it closes the measured stall, but only down to half capacity"). With one fetch at a time, a thread of N unfetchable photos still held one of the Parlons Node's two places for N × 30 s, and two things got worse: a photo already on disk waited in that queue, and "click to retry" did nothing for 2 to 30 minutes.
- main/chat.js: a photo already on disk is returned at once, with no queue and no node command. When a sender's photo cannot be fetched, that sender's drive is taken as unreachable for 2 minutes and the other photos of that sender are refused in their turn without asking the node: one 30 s wait, not one per photo. Only a failure of the peer is remembered ("media unavailable", an answer that took too long), never a stopped node, a file too large or a disk problem. Stopping the node forgets all of it. A click on the photo, or on Play, asks again at once.
- "Delivered" and "read" receipts go out one at a time: a mailbox drained of twenty messages sent twenty receipts at once, each dialling its sender for up to 15 s.
- Tests: scripts/chatfiles-test.cjs (three; two fail on 0.19.443).
- Not changed: the Parlons Node still answers two commands at a time. A contact who sends many photos from many different invented drives still costs 30 s each on one place.

## [0.19.443] — Chat: photos that cannot be fetched no longer hold up every other command ("Send failed: RPC timeout")

- Owner, 24 Nov 2026, on 0.19.442: in a chat thread whose photos could not be fetched (their sender's device was off), sending a message failed with "RPC timeout", and the node's `status` did not answer either. Measured: each such photo holds one node command for 30 s (`keets action:fetch` waiting for a peer), the thread screen asked for every photo again at each redraw, and the Parlons Node answers only two commands at a time (its admin RPC has two worker threads). Two fetches in flight held both, and every other command of the app, the chat send, a balance, a trading engine's read, waited behind them until its own 30 s ran out.
- main/chat.js `fetchMedia`: one photo is fetched at the node at a time, so one of the two places always stays free; a photo is asked for once however often the screen wants it; and a photo that could not be fetched is not asked for again for 2 minutes, then 4, up to 30.
- Not a fault of Chat only: the same would have happened in any Keets setting with the sender offline. Not changed: the Parlons Node's two worker threads (the node jar is as in 0.19.441).
- Test: scripts/chatfiles-test.cjs (fails on 0.19.442: two fetches in flight).

## [0.19.442] — Keets setting: an old stored flag no longer stops the node, and the three options stay after a save

- Independent review of 0.19.441 (no critical; the jars and the sidecar verified to the byte; two major, in the app).
- An install whose saved extra arguments held `-keets`, `-keetschatonly` or `-keetssidecarargs` (allowed until 0.19.440) was refused at node START ("… is in the extra arguments … Remove it in Startup Params"): the start gate ran before the code that drops those flags. A stored value is now dropped at the start as 0.19.441 said; typing one in Startup Params is still refused.
- The three Keets options turned back into the on/off switch after any save (a new wallet, the theme, plain Save) until the app was started again: the page keeps the config a save answers with, and that one did not carry the capability. Every config the page gets carries it now.
- A file given with -conf can no longer set the three flags the setting owns. Dropping a stored `-keetssidecarargs` no longer takes the parameter after it.
- Tests: scripts/keets-test.cjs (the start gate through `keyModeProblem`, the strip, the page's config), all failing on 0.19.441.

## [0.19.441] — Keets is one setting of three: chat and network, chat only, classic only (Pandamonium Desktop)

- Owner, 24 Nov 2026: the phone's three-way Keets setting, in Pandamonium Desktop. Startup Params shows **Chat and network** (as before: `-keets`), **Chat only** (`-keets -keetschatonly`: KeetChat, files and the mailbox stay, blocks and transactions use the classic Minima network only) and **Classic only** (no keets), in the phone's wording and translations. The released flavour keeps its on/off switch: its node jars do not know the flag, and a node given a flag it does not know refuses to start.
- Node jars of the Pandamonium flavour: the same two jars as 0.19.440 (minima 1.1.2.31-euro14, parlons-node 0.2.160) with the classes of seven sources recompiled from minima-core `feat/keets-chat-only-block-euro14` @ 2d8aad6981a48cae5d9fd937a01b0caabb765443, which is the change reviewed for the phone (Pandamonium 2.2.124–2.2.131), applied to the commit these jars were built from. Every other entry is byte-identical. minima.jar sha256 dea0a6c7b4568fcd15576dafd421bad28da79310b76d2265d2ebadf707f350dd, parlons-node.jar sha256 30db5baafa48695a6d9769160ee2d2c8a97fbd84a0d4f075af712d8c8ef7ae5a. The node starts the Keets sidecar itself and hands it `--chat-only`.
- What else that node change does, as on the phone: Keets peers no longer count when the node gives up on a classic peer, and classic peers are seeded from the published lists `https://spartacusrex.com/minimapeers.txt` and `https://eurobuddha.com/minimapeers.txt` at start and whenever the node has no peers (bounded: exact host:port entries, 64 KB, 50 checks, time limits; nothing in a list can stop the node. This computer and private networks are left out only when the node is not started with -allowallip, which this app's Mainnet preset sets: with it, a listed local address is sent one greeting and becomes a peer only if a real Minima node of the right chain answers there).
- Keets sidecar 1.3.5 (keets c3ac90351c7150de0c41c9e064fe453cceabb1a4): `--chat-only`. Its dependencies are unchanged.
- The setting owns `-keets`, `-keetschatonly` and `-keetssidecarargs`: typed in the extra arguments they are refused, and a value stored earlier is dropped together with its value. Chat only is sent only with keets, and only by a flavour whose jars know it.
- Tests: scripts/keets-test.cjs (four new: the owned flags, the sidecar, the real argument lists of both node kinds for the three settings, the pinned jars), scripts/core-screens-test.cjs, scripts/flavor-test.cjs; scripts/fixtures/KeetsFlagsCheck.java asks a jar whether it accepts the three settings without starting a node (both new jars do; the 0.19.440 jar does not compile against it).
- Not changed: the Chat tab is shown whatever the setting (with Classic only it says the node is starting); the sidecar's sender-id fix (1.3.6) is not in this copy.

## [0.19.440] — The stop path after a resync: only a real shutdown answer counts, and a lost answer is not recorded as a cut

- Independent review of 0.19.439 ("safe to install, strictly safer than 0.19.438"; two Required findings, both reproduced), main/node-manager.js.
- Core compares a command's action exactly: `megammrsync action:RESYNC` is answered with status true at once and shuts nothing down. That answer was taken as "the node has saved", and a peer's "saved" line could then get the node SIGTERM before its save. Only an answer whose message says the node must be restarted counts now (megammrsync, a MegaMMR import, archive resync, restore all say so).
- A resync or import whose answer was lost: core has closed its RPC port by then, so `quit` can never answer (0.19.439's comment was wrong). The stop waits out the 10-minute silence rule, as 0.19.439 did, but no longer records a cut for a node that had said it saved: the next start does not say "that file may be incomplete".
- Tests: the stand-in refuses `quit` after a resync, as core does; three new or corrected tests (two fail on 0.19.439). Stop-path tests 20 of 20.
- Not done: the 10-minute wait itself in the lost-answer case; `megammrfile.js` reading "saved" from the log during an import; the app's exit signal to a node mid-resync-save.

## [0.19.439] — A "saved" line in the log is never believed by itself: the stop needs the node's own answer too

- Independent review of 0.19.429–0.19.438 (MAJOR 1–3, both reproduced by the reviewer), main/node-manager.js. After `quit` is sent the node is still on the network for seconds (core shuts the network down first, writing nothing), and a peer can make it print a whole "Main Instance Cleared.." line: the stop believed it and sent SIGTERM 5 s later, before the node had saved anything (recorded as clean). A resync the node REFUSED also left its mark set for the rest of the run, so every later such line was believed.
- "Saved" now also needs what no peer can produce: the `status:true` answer of the command that shut the node's insides down (megammrsync, restore, archive, a MegaMMR import: it answers only after its save), or, when that answer was lost, the answer of `quit`. A plain Stop or Quit uses neither: the node exits by itself. A refused command clears its mark. The record of a stop still reads the log after the app's own signal.
- A node that said its shutdown began and then closed its RPC port is no longer sent SIGTERM after 60 s of quiet (MINOR 1): the 10-minute silence rule applies.
- Tests: the stand-in node now has core's silent phase between "Shut down started.." and "Saving all db" and can forge inside it; two new tests, both failing on 0.19.438. Stop-path tests 18 of 18.
- Not done from that review (minors): `-noshutdownhook` in Startup Params, callers of a stop that gave up at 30 minutes, Future Cash read-gap retries, the Dexicon script list's 5 s age, Windows `wmic`.

## [0.19.438] — The "older copy" refusal compares within one app only, and names its way back

- Independent review of 0.19.428 (MINOR 12), main/writermark.js. The mark in `<data folder>/minimacore-writer.txt` (0.19.423) carried only a version. Two apps of this family pointed at one custom data folder have unrelated version numbers (0.18.x and 0.19.x): the one with the lower number was refused for good, and nothing said how to get out of a refusal.
- The file now holds one line an app: `app <version> <the app's bundle id>`. A copy is compared only with the line of its own app. The line builds 0.19.423 to 0.19.437 wrote and read (`app <version>`, no app named) is still written, and only raised, so those older copies go on refusing; here it is read only in a file that names no app at all (a mark one of them left), which cannot be told apart and is still compared with every copy.
- **The way back, for a deliberate return to an older version**: quit the app, delete the file `<data folder>/minimacore-writer.txt`, open the older copy. The refusal now ends "To go back to this older version on purpose, quit the app, delete the file {its full path} and open this copy again.", and README.md has it ("Going back to an older version"). Nothing else reads that file; the next start writes it again.
- The refusal is said in zh, ru and uk.
- Tests: scripts/writermark-test.cjs (fails on 0.19.437: the other app's higher number refused this one), scripts/mainsaid-test.cjs.

## [0.19.437] — A take's page newscripts read the wallet's scripts once, not once each

- Independent review of 0.19.428 (MINOR 14), main/dexicon.js. The guard of 0.19.428 read the node's `scripts` for every page newscript (a take sends up to 32), though the signing check already reads the same lists.
- One short-lived read of `scripts` and `keys` (5 s) now serves the newscript guard and the signing check. It is read again as soon as the page itself reads `scripts` or `getaddress` (the only ways a page can learn a key this wallet has just made), and whenever a simple row names a key the kept list does not hold. A read that fails falls back on the last whole one for up to 60 s, as the key list did; with none, nothing is registered, as before.
- Test: scripts/dexicon-host.test.cjs (fails on 0.19.436: 32 reads).

## [0.19.436] — The Dexicon page's Activity read is let through; a forged fill record no longer keeps the Quit warning on

- Independent review of 0.19.428 (MINOR 10), main/dexicon.js.
- `coins … depth:` from the page took at most 6 digits (0.19.424). The page's Activity reads `depth:` tip + 6 for a trade that has no post block yet (renderer/dexicon/ui/activity.js), and the tip has 7 digits: that read was refused, so such a trade's proceeds coin was not found. 7 digits are allowed.
- "Fills with an outcome not known yet" (the Quit and Shutdown warnings, the Freeze blocker) counted ANY `fill_%` record saying status unknown. Since 0.19.425 the engine settles only its own unanswered record, so a record the page wrote was never settled and kept the warning and the blocker on for good. Only the records the engine settles are counted: role maker, a `fill_unanswered_` key, its unanswered id, a plain positive fill.
- Test: scripts/dexicon-host.test.cjs (two fail on 0.19.435: "not available to Dexicon: coins with depth"; the forged record counted 1).

## [0.19.435] — Future Cash Next: no false theft alarm, a landed collect ends, one coin proves one collect

- Independent review of 0.19.428 (MINOR 8 and 9), main/futurecash.js. The theft alarm ("A collected stake … did NOT reach your safe address. Your old key may be compromised", status TAKEN) is final. It was raised on one read, and two harmless things look the same to that read: our own sweep landed and its coin at the same-node safe was spent onward before any pass saw it; or a reorg dropped the collect, so the coin being swept no longer exists.
- Before the alarm, now: (1) the sweep's own transaction is looked for on chain (`txpow address:<the safe>`: a mined transaction that spends this coin and pays the safe this amount). It is found by the coin it spends, never by an id: the txpowid `txnpost` answers is the id before mining and matches no mined txpow (it is kept in the Activity log line of the post, whole). Found: the sweep ends as Secured. (2) If the collect that made the coin was undone (its stake is listed, unspent, in the contract again), the sweep is dropped and the stake is collected again. (3) Otherwise "gone and not ours" must be read twice, 3 blocks apart. (4) When the search cannot be read, or the sweep was posted more than 600 blocks ago (past what the node's chain tree shows), the end is said as "arrival unverified: please check it arrived", never as a theft.
- A collect this app posted whose stake is no longer listed and whose coin was never seen at the payout ends 120 blocks after the collect as COLLECTED_UNFOUND, logged "Collected, output not seen…". It sat in DETECTED for good under "Collect retry scheduled", with nothing left to retry; that line is no longer written for a stake the node no longer lists.
- One coin at a payout proves one collect: the coin is recorded on its collect (fc_collect.note, an existing column), and a coin recorded for another collect, or queued for a sweep, proves no second one (two stakes of the same amount to the same address were both ended by one coin).
- Tests: scripts/futurecash-test.cjs, five new and one changed (the alarm now needs the second read); its stand-in node answers `txpow address:` as core does (all six fail on 0.19.434).

## [0.19.434] — On Windows the app's exit no longer ends the node unsaved

- Independent review of 0.19.428 (MINOR 7). When the app closes with its node still running and not yet told to quit, it sent the node SIGTERM and logged "sent SIGTERM, so it saves and stops by itself". On Windows that call is TerminateProcess: the node ends at once, with no shutdown hook and nothing saved.
- main/node-manager.js killNow: on Windows nothing is sent. The node runs on as it is, and the next launch stops it with `quit` and waits for it (as it already does for a node left by a crash). The log line says what was done on each platform. macOS and Linux are unchanged.
- Test: scripts/lifecycle-test.cjs (fails on 0.19.433: SIGTERM was sent).

## [0.19.433] — A stop sees the node writing its H2 stores, not only the files beside them

- Independent review of 0.19.428 (MINOR 6). A stop takes a write in the node's databases folder as a sign that the node is saving. Only the files directly in `<data>/<version>/databases` were looked at (megammr.mmr and the like); the H2 stores live one folder down (walletsql, archivesql, txpowsql, coindb, txblockdb), and H2 rewrites its file in place, so the folder's own time does not move: a node writing only those counted as silent.
- main/node-manager.js dbTouchedAt: each folder under databases/ is looked into, one level and no further, at most 64 files a folder.
- Test: scripts/lifecycle-test.cjs (fails on 0.19.432: the hour-old time is answered).

## [0.19.432] — "Your MegaMMR looks incomplete" no longer stays for good after a deliberate change

- Independent review of 0.19.428 (MINOR 5). The note compares the MegaMMR with the figures of the last clean stop (its coins, its file's size), and those figures were kept for as long as the MegaMMR was found short. After a deliberate resync, an import or "Keep MegaMMR on, empty" the MegaMMR is smaller on purpose, so "Your MegaMMR looks incomplete… import the MegaMMR file again" came back at every start with no way to end it.
- main/node-manager.js acceptMegaNow: the figures (and the notes made from them, the "cut short" one included) are dropped when a resync, a restore or a MegaMMR import has run, when a banner's Keep is chosen ("Keep MegaMMR on, empty", "Keep the imported chain", "My balance is correct"), and when the note's OK is pressed. The next clean stop records the MegaMMR as it is then.
- The note's OK now means that: it used to hide the note until the next start only.
- Test: scripts/lifecycle-test.cjs (fails on 0.19.431: no such function, and its own older assertion shows the figures frozen at 663414).

## [0.19.431] — AtomiX: the background keep-alive never publishes a market as zero on a failed balance read (engine 0.1.71)

- Independent review of 0.19.428 (MAJOR). **A correction to 0.19.422**: that entry said "a market is never published as zero because a balance read failed" and named "the desk's republish". It fixed only Save and Publish, the two calls this app makes. The engine's own keep-alive (main/atomix/service.js `getBalances`, every block and every minute) still read with `Number(...) || 0` and `usdt: e ? 0 : …`, and `AX.maker.keepAlive` published that zero: the market left every taker's book and a key use was spent, with no error, on versions 0.19.422 to 0.19.430 too.
- The AtomiX engine is re-copied from its donor, atomix-mds **0.1.71** (commit fa5efa7e55fa679b066e231e5a451ad9d59d423c, branch security/2026-11-07; it was 0.1.61, f33c4afd495f84451d459a46b0445f6b7d7490e9), with scripts/vendored-manifest.cjs. What that brings: `getBalances` answers null on a refused, pending or unreadable read and `keepAlive(null)` republishes nothing (0.1.64; a dead price feed still withdraws the market, and reads failing for over 30 minutes stop new takes and say so, 0.1.69); `wallet.shortAddr` and `fmt.shorten` are gone (0.1.65; nothing in main/ or renderer/ called them); the Minima dollar token reads MxUSD in the engine's own sentences (0.1.66); an Ethereum gas price above 8000 gwei is refused before signing with `ETH_FEE_REFUSED`, decided on the median of three endpoints, logged every time and notified once per swap (0.1.67, 0.1.70); the wallet keeper's split asks coinage:3 and goes only to an address the node is proven to own, `scripts address:` and a complete `keys` list, checked right before the send (0.1.62, 0.1.68); a password-locked plain node is said at boot from `status` (0.1.63).
- The host needed nothing new for it: the engine's `scripts`, `keys`, `status` and `checkmode` go to the node as every command does, its re-quote uses the same nine endpoints (main/atomix.js NET_HOSTS is unchanged and still equal to the engine's list), and the most this app's own Send can sign (5000 gwei) is under the engine's ceiling.
- Review MINOR 11, main/atomix.js makerSave and main/makeravail.js: the balances are read BEFORE the new order is stored, so a Save refused for an unread balance leaves the saved order as it was (it was stored first, for the keep-alive to publish later). And only the side the ladder uses must be readable: an ask-only market is no longer blocked by a failed USDT read, nor a bid-only one by a failed Minima-side read. A pending `balance` reply counts as not read.
- To know: the engine's own notifications (the new ones included) are English, as all of the engine's are.
- Tests: scripts/atomix-unit.js (the old engine answers `{minima: 0, usdt: 0}` for a failed read), scripts/makeravail-test.cjs (two new; the functions do not exist on 0.19.430).

## [0.19.430] — A stop never waits without bound, and says what it is really waiting for

- Independent review of 0.19.428 (MAJOR, the reviewer ran it; MINOR 4 folded in). When `quit` failed with anything but "nothing listens" (a 401, a timeout on a wedged RPC thread, a reset) it was never sent again, and a node that keeps logging or touching its databases never reached the 10-minute silence bound: stop() never returned, Quit could not finish (the window cannot be closed while the node is being stopped), and the note said "The node is saving… Do not force-quit", which was not true. The next launch waited in the same loop with no note at all.
- main/node-manager.js waitGone, new: 60 s into a stop, when `quit` was never answered: (1) the node's output is read, `quit` failed or is still pending, and the node has not printed `Shut down started..` (core Main.shutdown's first line): SIGTERM. That node is not shutting down, so its own shutdown hook runs the whole save, and the wait goes on under the same rules as after `quit`. (2) nothing ever listened on its RPC port and there was no output and no database write for 60 s (it was 10 minutes): SIGTERM. Never on Windows, where "SIGTERM" ends the process at once. A node of an earlier launch (nothing it prints can be read) whose RPC took `quit` and did not answer may be saving: it gets neither.
- A ceiling: 30 minutes after a stop began, the wait is given up and nothing more is signalled. The window says "The node (pid N) has not stopped after 30 minutes and is still running. You can close the app: the next launch waits for this node before it starts one."; a Quit shows that and then closes; the next launch waits for that node (and gives up the same way, with "could not stop this app's node left running by an earlier launch (pid N)").
- The note is true per case: "The node has been told to stop and has not answered yet…" until the node has answered `quit`, said its shutdown began or said it saved; "The node is saving…" from then on; at a start that waits for a node of an earlier launch: "This app's node from an earlier launch is still running (pid N). It is waited for while it saves its data and stops…".
- Tests: scripts/lifecycle-test.cjs, three new and one extended (on 0.19.429 two never return and are cut at 30 s, two fail). New words in zh, ru and uk.

## [0.19.429] — A "saved" line from earlier in the run never makes a stop signal the node

- Independent review of 0.19.428 (MAJOR, the reviewer ran it). Core logs a peer's failing script as it came, line ends included (TxPoWChecker "Script FAIL input:…"), so a peer can make the node print a whole `Minima @ … : Main Instance Cleared..` line. The app believed such a line from any point of the run: the NEXT stop then sent SIGTERM 5 s in, while megammr.mmr was being written (probe: 10000 of 40000 bytes), and recorded the stop as clean.
- main/node-manager.js: a "saved" line counts for a stop only when it came AFTER that stop first sent `quit` (or a signal), or after a command that shuts the node's insides down was sent (runResync: megammrsync, restore, archive, a MegaMMR import). And the signal after "saved" also waits for 5 s with no output and no database write (a megammr.mmr being written keeps its own mtime fresh). A stop is recorded as clean only when its signal went on that quiet path, or the node said it had saved after the signal (its own shutdown hook).
- Limit to know: a node that has saved and sits there (after megammrsync) but keeps printing more often than every 5 s is not signalled by this rule.
- Tests: scripts/lifecycle-test.cjs, three new (all fail on 0.19.428: megammr.mmr 1000 of 12000 bytes; SIGTERM during the 20 s write; SIGTERM at 306 ms).

## [0.19.428] — A page newscript never touches one of this wallet's own addresses

- Full review 23 Nov 2026 (MAJOR), the part 0.19.424 left owed; as DexiconPro 0.2.183. The node's `newscript` REPLACES its row for a script's address (simple:false, key 0x00, the caller's trackall). A page that had been taken over could register `RETURN SIGNEDBY(<one of our keys>)` untracked: our own address stopped being ours, and its coins left the balance until a seed restore.
- main/dexicon.js: a page newscript is refused when its script names one of this wallet's own keys (any 32-byte value in it) or is one of our tracked scripts (a pool covenant); when the wallet's keys or scripts cannot be read, nothing is registered. A maker's script registers as before.
- Effect to know: a swap from the Dexicon page against one of YOUR OWN pools is refused with "that script is one of this wallet's own" (registering it untracked would hide the pool's coins from this wallet).
- Test: scripts/dexicon-host.test.cjs (fails on 0.19.427).

## [0.19.427] — A read that walks the MegaMMR is given 150 seconds, not 30

- Stand-in audit 23 Nov 2026 (MINOR). `balance … megammr:true` and `coins … megammr:true` walk the node's whole MegaMMR coin table. The app cut every read at 30 s, while the PandaPools engine allows its own reserve lookup 120 s: on a full mainnet MegaMMR the lookup ended as "Reserves unresolved".
- main/rpc.js timeoutFor: a command carrying `megammr:true` gets 150 s. Writes (180 s) and plain reads (30 s) are unchanged.
- Test: scripts/rpc-test.cjs (fails on 0.19.426: 30000).

## [0.19.426] — The AtomiX room's Ethereum Send takes the ETH Wallet's guarded path; no fee tier passes the ceiling

- Full review 23 Nov 2026 (MAJOR), as minimaCore Desktop 0.18.323. The AtomiX room's Send signed at whatever gas price the Ethereum endpoint returned (no ceiling: one hostile or broken endpoint among the fallbacks set the fee; in the reviewer's run a 21,000-gas send was signed with a 0.945 ETH fee), with no address checksum check and no one-send-at-a-time guard.
- main/atomix.js: sendMax / sendReview / sendExecute now go through ethSendMax / ethSendReview / ethSendExecute at the Medium tier (EIP-55 check, one send at a time, capped gas).
- Fee tiers: the price handed to the engine is pre-scaled ×8/9 for the ×9/8 the engine adds (it was ÷1.2, for a ×1.2 the engine no longer applies: every tier was broadcast about 6% under its preview), and the 5000 gwei ceiling is applied AFTER the tier multiplier (High could reach 1.6× the ceiling). The Max reserve covers the High tier's real cost.
- Test: scripts/ethwallet-unit.js (fails on 0.19.425), its model of the engine's fee arithmetic corrected to the vendored engine's.

## [0.19.425] — A record the page wrote can never put an amount back on a live order

- Full review 23 Nov 2026 (MAJOR). The page may write TAKER fill records. One saying `status:"unknown"` and naming a live order's applied fill id was settled as the engine's own unanswered fill: found "lost", and its `filled` (any number) was added back to the order. In the reviewer's run a live order went from 40 to 1000040, and the maker would then co-sign takes far past what was ordered.
- main/dexicon/service.desktop.js settleFills: only the engine's own unanswered record is settled: role maker, a `fill_unanswered_` key, its unanswered id, a plain positive fill.
- Test: scripts/dexicon-host.test.cjs (fails on 0.19.424: the order's remaining became 1000040).

## [0.19.424] — The Dexicon page's reads carry only the engine's own parameters

- Full review 23 Nov 2026 (MAJOR). The page's bridge allowed a list of VERBS with any parameters. A page that had been taken over could send `history action:customsize where:<text>` (the node pastes it into a query on its H2 database, which can write and read files on this Mac), `coins megammr:true` with no coin id (the node copies its whole unspent-coin table under the database lock: AtomiX settlement stalls), and a `newscript` with any parameters (`clean:true`, `trackall:true`).
- main/dexicon.js `readShape`: status, block, getaddress, checkmode and scripts take no parameter; random, balance, history, txpow, txnview and coins take only the parameters the engine files really send, each once and in shape (`history relevant: max: offset:` only; `coins megammr:` only with one `coinid:`); `newscript` is only `script:"…" trackall:false` (either order), with no quote or backslash inside the script.
- Still owed (not in this version): refusing a `newscript` that names one of this wallet's own keys or tracked scripts (DexiconPro 0.2.183 has it).
- Test: scripts/dexicon-host.test.cjs (fails on 0.19.423: `history action:customsize where:1=1` passed).

## [0.19.423] — An older copy of the app refuses a data folder a newer copy wrote

- Every build since 0.19.105 uses the same data folder, and nothing recorded which version last wrote it. On 23 Nov 2026 a stray 0.19.105 was opened on the folder 0.19.416 had written: it knows nothing of the MegaMMR import marker, the frozen apps or the stop guard.
- main/writermark.js (new): each node start records the app's version in `<data folder>/minimacore-writer.txt` (only ever raised). A copy older than the recorded version does not start the node: "This data folder was last used by Pandamonium Desktop {v}. This copy is {mine}, which is older and can damage what the newer one wrote. The node was not started and nothing was changed. Open Pandamonium Desktop {v} or newer. This copy is at {its own path}".
- Limit: builds older than 0.19.423 do not read the mark, so they are not stopped by it; keep old copies out of reach.
- Test: scripts/writermark-test.cjs (in test:flavor; the module does not exist on 0.19.422).

## [0.19.422] — AtomiX: a market is never published as zero because a balance read failed

- Review of minimaCore Desktop 0.18.328 (MAJOR 2), the same code in all three desktop apps. `makerAvail` turned a failed read into 0 (`Number(b.minima) || 0`: `balances()` gives "0" when the `balance` RPC fails and "—" when the USDT read fails), and Save, Publish and the desk's republish then published a zero order: the market left every book and a key use was spent, with no error.
- main/makeravail.js (new): Save and Publish refuse on a failed read ("Your … balance could not be read just now …: nothing was published"); a real zero is still zero; Withdraw and the currency switch (a tombstone carries no size) go as before. `balances()` now says when the Minima-side read failed (`minimaErr`).
- Test: scripts/makeravail-test.cjs (in test:ethwallet) (the module does not exist on 0.19.421).

## [0.19.421] — Future Cash Next: a spent coin is read as the real node shows it

- The node never lists a spent coin (`coins coinid:` of one answers `[]`, core TxPoWSearcher.searchCoins), and `[]` is also
  what a reorg or a coin outside the node's window looks like. Future Cash Next read `[]` as "rolled back by a reorg" and
  only a row with `spent:true` (which the node never sends) as spent: a posted collect never became COLLECTED, a stake
  collected elsewhere was never finished, a landed rescue sweep was DELETED with "rolled back by a chain reorg", and the
  theft alarm could never fire.
- Now `[]` is unknown, never a conclusion and never a deleted row. A collect is done when its output is at the payout (this
  token and amount, created at or after the collect or the stake's maturity); a sweep when its output is at the safe. As the
  Pandamonium 2.2.113 Guardian (collectDone / sweepVerdict), plus one thing it does not do: with a same-node safe that can be
  read, a swept coin that is gone from its address and not at the safe after 30 blocks raises the theft alarm; an external
  safe that cannot be read ends as "spent — arrival unverified" after 30 blocks.
- The tests' stand-in node now answers as the real one: a spent coin is never listed.

## [0.19.420] — A stop waits while the node saves: no Quit, Restart or Stop cuts a MegaMMR again

- main/node-manager.js stop(): the old stop armed SIGTERM at 12 s and SIGKILL at 25 s BEFORE it sent `quit`, and core's `quit` writes every database before it answers: on a MegaMMR node that is megammr.mmr (1.5 GB), deleted and written again (MiniFile.saveObjectDirect), and a cut file loads without a word. On 23 Nov 2026 a Quit sent SIGTERM 12 s after "Saving all db" and the 1.5 GB MegaMMR (663,414 coins) came back as 49.1 MB (61 coins); a SIGKILL cut another save at 19:23. Now `quit` is sent first (again every 2 s while the node is still booting and nothing listens), and the node is waited for while it shows any sign of life: a line on its output or a write in its databases folder. It is signalled only when it has said it saved ("Shut down completed OK.." or "Main Instance Cleared..") and then does not exit by itself (megammrsync, restore and archive leave it that way: SIGTERM after 5 s, not a cut), or after 10 minutes with no output and no database write (SIGTERM; SIGKILL 2 minutes after that with no sign of life). Every stop comes through it: Stop, Restart, the node kind, the import's and the resync's restarts, a chat backup restore, Quit. A second stop waits for the first.
- The start-up reclaim of the app's own node from an earlier launch (reclaimStaleNode) waits the same way: `quit` when it is the one on the RPC port, its writes in the databases folder as its sign of life (its output went to the launch that is gone). The old one signalled it after 8 s.
- The app's own exit (will-quit, exit) never kills the node (killNow): one told to quit is left to finish saving and exit; any other is sent SIGTERM, which makes the node run its own shutdown (Minima.java's shutdown hook saves everything). The pidfile stays, so the next launch waits for it.
- A Quit (the system's included) waits for the save; after 2 s the window comes up and says "The node is saving its data: this can take a minute or two with a MegaMMR. Do not force-quit." (a bar under the toolbar, renderer/app.js paintNoteBar), and it cannot be closed until the node has stopped.
- How each stop ended is kept in the data folder (minimacore-shutdown.json). A stop that needed a signal before the node said it had saved is recorded as cut, and the next start says "The node's last shutdown was cut short (SIGKILL) before it had saved everything. If it keeps a MegaMMR, that file may be incomplete." A clean stop records the MegaMMR's coins (`megammr`) and its file size; at the next start a file under half that size, and once the node runs a coin count under half, is said: "Your MegaMMR looks incomplete (61 coins, was 663414): import the MegaMMR file again." Informational only; those figures are kept until the MegaMMR is whole again. zh / ru / uk.
- main/keymode.js: the app's own files in the data folder (minimacore-*) are not node data for the key-mode reader and the first-run check.
- Tests: scripts/lifecycle-test.cjs (in test:flavor) runs the real stop, reclaim and exit paths on a stand-in node PROCESS (scripts/fixtures/saving-node.cjs) that saves megammr.mmr in pieces and answers signals as the JVM does. All 8 fail on 0.19.419 and on 0.19.417 (a 14 s save cut at 12 s: 23000 of 28000 bytes written; the reclaim signalled a saving node after 8.6 s; the app's exit sent SIGKILL).

## [0.19.419] — P2P Chance (Zero Edge Casino) removed from the app

- 23 Nov 2026: the casino is removed pending an audit and a rebuild, after the coin-state-to-command injection fixed in
  0.19.418 (a taker's covenant state reached node commands through auto-reveal). The game itself left in 0.18.68; what was
  left (main/casino.js and the vendored main/casino/: the settle-only loop that revealed, resolved, withdrew this wallet's
  open offers and claimed timeouts on each new block) is now deleted, with its IPC (`mcd:casino*`), its Settings card,
  its lines in the restore / Quit / Shutdown guards and the coin guard, its words in en/zh/ru/uk, its npm scripts, its CI
  steps and its vendoring manifest entry. `main/casino*` is also excluded from the packaged files.
- Nothing is deleted from an install: the casino's keychain accounts (`casino-<sha256>`: commit preimages, history, cached
  identity) and the `casinoEnabled` config flag stay exactly as they are. Nothing reads or writes them any more.
- On the chain: the app no longer settles a bet or withdraws an offer at the casino covenant
  `0xD65ADBBB7AB5032D794B02CF5E8814C720BE3C9562CC6C07081DE41CCA665A6F`. An open offer stays takeable by any other casino
  client; once taken, a house that does not reveal within the offer's timeout (1500 blocks) loses the whole pot to the
  player, and a player that does not resolve within it after the reveal loses the whole pot to the house.
- To add it back: revert this commit. Its parent, `3bbb586f45d930b6a74581a509e2308ac45b4b2e` (0.19.418), holds the module,
  its tests and its manifest as they were; the donor is `~/Projects/archive/Ideas/universal-casino`.

## [0.19.418] — SECURITY: a stranger's coin state can no longer become a node command

- The node splits every command text on ";" (even inside quotes) and runs each piece. A PandaPools beacon at the public sentinel whose state port 2 held `0x00;send …;` turned ONE discovery `runscript` into FOUR admin `send`s (the token appears 4 times in the covenant), and a P2P Chance taker's state 9 `0;SEND …` ran through auto-reveal (the shim refused only a lower-case `send`).
- main/cmdverb.js `refuse(source, text)`: a command holding ";", CR, LF or NUL is refused, nothing is sent. It runs under every caller in main/rpc.js (`rpcCall` and the raw call under `rpcRead`) and first in each engine shim (main/pandapools.js, main/atomix.js, main/dexicon.js, main/casino.js). An app or engine refused so is said in a notification naming it (main/main.js, once a minute per source); the Terminal refuses a chain before any gate or question: "send the commands one at a time" (renderer/mainsaid.js RPC_UNSAFE, zh / ru / uk).
- Re-vendored: PandaPools engine 0.6.46 (beacon and tracked-covenant fields strict hex / number where read; `PoolRuntimeGuard` refuses ";" / CR / LF / NUL and logs it), P2P Chance service.js 2.9.4 (state read only as 0x hex or a number; reveal / resolve skip a bet whose state is anything else; txnbasics and txnpost as two commands), AtomiX 0.1.61 (refusals logged; HTLC validators no longer trim).
- main/casino/engine.js (desktop glue): the same clean state reads and checks, and its five `txnbasics;txnpost` chains are two commands. main/casino.js reads the verb as the node does (cmdverb: `SEND`, `"send"`, padded).
- main/pandapools.js migrate: refused for a pool whose token is not MxUSD (as createPool).
- Tests: scripts/engine-inject.test.cjs (in test:inject) drives the real host shims over the real engines against scripts/node-standin.cjs, a stand-in node that splits commands like the real one; it fails on 0.19.417. Chain expectations in rpc-test, inflight-test and seedgate-test now expect the refusal; casino-timeout-test's service / engine fixtures use hex keys.

## [0.19.417] — The MegaMMR file import no longer refuses itself ("Dexicon has not finished starting")

- main/megammrfile.js: since 0.19.395 the import held the trading engines (and Future Cash Next was already stopped by the import's own start) and only then asked them what was open; a held engine does not answer, so every import was refused with "Dexicon has not finished starting" and "Future Cash Next has not started (or is frozen)" although both were running. Now what is open is read while the engines run, refused if anything is; then the stop guard is asked, still with the engines running; then the hold; then the same read again, of the engines as held, so work that started between the first read and the hold refuses too and the engines are let go (the stop guard's hold is ended with it, so a retry is not refused as a stop already under way).
- main/dexicon.js and main/futurecash.js keep what they had open when they were stopped (Dexicon: its on-chain orders, placements waiting for a block, takes awaiting an answer and fills of unknown outcome, read whole from its engine and store as it is dropped; Future Cash Next: its posted collects, queued and posted sweeps, read from the store as it is closed). A stopped engine changes nothing, so that is what it has open. main/restore.js heldRead: an engine the app's OWN flow stopped (Dexicon held by a MegaMMR import or its marker, Future Cash Next paused while an import, a restore or a MegaMMR switch runs) is read from that, never said as "has not started". Used by the restore and import gate (walletWork), the stop and quit guards (inFlightWork: the guard after the import's own restart into MegaMMR mode, Turn MegaMMR off, Restore from file (which could refuse with "Future Cash Next has not started" too: the restore's own start had stopped it) and the banner's Resync node no longer ask "could not be checked" of an engine the app itself stopped; after the app was restarted with the import's marker, Dexicon never ran in this session and is still said as not started) and Freeze of Future Cash Next. An engine that never ran, was not read whole, or was stopped for another reason (frozen, the node stopped) is said as before. An engine that really has not finished starting still refuses with "Wait a moment", and after two minutes the import offers "Import without these checks?" as before.

## [0.19.416] — History says when older on-chain trades are not listed

- renderer/dexicon-ui.js History: the on-chain engine hands over only its newest 200 trades (resting/pd_service.js loadTape, vendored and not changed here). When that list is full, the footer under Show more says "Older on-chain trades are not listed: only your newest 200 are kept here." In zh / ru / uk.

## [0.19.415] — Back from an older build, a fill that build applied itself is never deducted again

- main/dexicon/service.desktop.js DOWNGRADE GUARD: an app before 0.19.406 (engine 0.0.91) running on this store applies its fills itself and records them without `verified` and without listing them on the order. Back on this build, the refresh deducted them a second time (an order of 100 with a fill of 30 here and 20 there was left at 30, not 50). At boot, before the engine is ready, such a record of a live order (from its fillsFrom on, not listed) is listed as applied; this build's own unapplied records (which always carry `verified`) are still deducted. Going back and forth between builds stays discouraged: cancel your FAST orders first.

## [0.19.414] — An unanswered fill not yet applied survives a restart; a "lost" one never adds back what was never taken off

- main/dexicon/service.desktop.js: a fill whose txnpost the node never answered, and whose afterFill did not save, was pending only in memory: after a restart its order stood at full size and took takes against it (its record has no txpowid, so no refresh deducts it). Once per engine, before the first take, every fill_unanswered_ record of a live order that does not list its `applied` id (status unknown or done) is pending again: the order refuses takes and the retry applies it once. An unanswered fill counts as applied only by that id (never by fillsFrom's time). When the chain says it was never posted, the amount goes back on the order only if it was taken off; before, a fill that was never applied was added on top (100 became 130).

## [0.19.413] — The FAST ladder never says "checking…" for ever

- renderer/dexicon-ui.js: when the page's check of the FAST book is still not ready after 60 seconds, the ticker line says why instead of "checking…": the page's own error ("the FAST book could not be checked: …"), else "no relay has answered yet", else that it is still not checked and only your own FAST orders are shown. With no page view at all, your own FAST orders (from the service's book) stay on the ladder; a stranger's unchecked order never shows. In zh / ru / uk.

## [0.19.412] — A FAST click waits for the page's check of the book, as SWEEP does

- renderer/dexicon-ui.js submit(): while the page has not yet checked which FAST orders this node can take (its takeable view is not ready), a FAST click is refused with "Still checking which FAST orders this node can take: try again in a moment. Nothing was signed or sent." Before, a crossing order missing from the unchecked view let the click rest as a FAST order instead of taking. In zh / ru / uk.

## [0.19.411] — SWEEP's on-chain part waits for the engine's answer to ITS order, not the next answer to anything

- The page's take-only LIMIT carries a reference (renderer/dexicon/mds.js sweepChain); main/dexicon/service.desktop.js ACTION REF puts it on the first POSTED or ERROR told while that LIMIT is handled — at once, after the pool refresh, or from the callback of the transaction it started (the vendored engine's limitWithPools and PandaTxn fill / fillComposite / create are wrapped, not edited). The Trade screen takes only the packet with its own reference; another action's answer within the 180 s no longer stands in for it.

## [0.19.410] — SWEEP: the FAST part never fills past the worst price its confirmation showed

- renderer/dexicon-ui.js sweep(): the FAST part's limit (sent to the page's take run and to the signing check) is the worst FAST price the confirmation named, not the typed price. With a typed limit looser than the FAST orders in the plan, the run could have taken a FAST order that appeared in between at a price the dialog never showed. What the FAST part cannot take at that price falls to the on-chain part, inside the typed limit as before.

## [0.19.409] — A FAST order is never backed by a coin a posted on-chain trade is spending

- main/dexicon.js: the page may read exactly `SELECT coinid FROM pp_posted_inputs` (lib/maker.js pageHolds, on every FAST create and edit: the resting engine's durable input claims). It was refused, so the page's coin choice ignored them and could name a coin an on-chain trade had just spent.

## [0.19.408] — Dexicon engine 0.0.156: a fill is never dropped by a racing save, a cancelled order cannot stay live on a relay, junk fill records are ignored

- Vendored mds/dexicon 1993840dd774dda5734ac19f3e22d94f418a8a6f (0.0.156; was 0.0.153 cefb6caf9fc6ac15e87ef39bb8597096d39d556f) byte for byte via a detached worktree: only lib/maker.js changed. A stored `remaining` or `fills` that changed since a writer loaded the list counts as moved (a refresh's reconciled fill is no longer dropped by a background save); a refresh whose re-sign is refused keeps the raised seq of an order it just deducted a fill from, so the relays get the deducted size; afterFill saves before it publishes; a cancel or relay sync that meets a newer LIVE copy on a relay re-sends the cancel above it (no cancelled order left live there); the refresh ignores a fill record whose `filled` is not a plain decimal above 0 or whose `verified` is junk. The donor's service.js is unchanged (nothing to port); both manifests updated; the donor parity check passes (39 files).

## [0.19.407] — The page can no longer plant a maker fill record under a key the engine reads as one

- main/dexicon.js dexSql: a fill record is every key the engine's `k LIKE 'fill_%'` reads — "fill" in any case, then any one character or more (`_` is any character, SQLite's LIKE ignores ASCII case). Through the page bridge `fillZ0x…` and `FILL_0x…` were written as maker records of -50 and the service's refresh raised a live order from 60 to 160. Now the page writes only taker records under any such key, and deletes none.

## [0.19.404] — Dexicon History is one list by time: fast-book fills, on-chain trades, finished AtomiX swaps and pool operations

- History mixes every kind with DexCore.activityMix and draws them as Activity does (day headers, kind marks). The NET card stays on top and says it counts settled fast-book fills only.
- On-chain trades are in History now (the engine's my_trades from the service's STATE), with txpow, order and spent coin whole.
- One depth rule: 60 rows of the mixed list, then "Show more" pages the whole list by date; PandaPools' own 60 cap in History is gone (no older pool row dropped).

## [0.19.403] — A light / dark button in the bar of every screen

- The bar (home, wallet, every tab and tray app) has a ☀ / ☾ button left of the menu: one click saves the opposite theme as config `theme`, the same setting as Settings › Appearance; Dexicon's sun now saves that setting too, so all three always agree. Follow the system is chosen again in Settings › Appearance. The Pandamonium Android home screen has no such toggle to copy; the look is Dexicon's.

## [0.19.402] — The MegaMMR import's gate-review minors

- A verify or Keep that awaited the node across a newly started import releases nothing; Keep is refused while one runs; the trading engines are held for the whole import (holdsEngines) but for its own release.
- Plain words when none of the node's earlier blocks can be compared with the imported chain (Keep offered at once), and "Waiting for the node to answer" when a read is not answered.
- Quit during the final restart has its own truthful words; the import's row stays open while it runs; Keep, "My balance is correct" and "Turn MegaMMR off and resync" work with the node stopped; the interrupted switch gets "Keep MegaMMR on, empty".
- NITs: an old marker claims no "less"; a refused check keeps an earlier switching marker; an unwritable clear still releases, with a warning; token names beside whole ids in the dismiss.

## [0.19.406] — Dexicon engine 0.0.153: proofs answered, fills counted once, the page's store lock, only takeable orders on the book, SWEEP sends

- Vendored mds/dexicon cefb6caf9fc6ac15e87ef39bb8597096d39d556f (0.0.153; was 0.0.91 9a95eb114ad35d3e18692a60c248529762fd72d5) byte for byte: lib/ (fill, ladder, maker, new proofs), resting/ (its pandadex 0.4.34 copy: book, pd_service), the page (index.html, ui/app.js, ui/pd_app.js, ui/dexicon.css, new lib/takeable.js and doors/sha3.js, the hash takeable needs: the only door served). Both manifests updated; the donor parity check passes (39 files).
- main/dexicon/service.desktop.js, the donor's service.js hunks: coin proof requests answered after the takes (64 senders, 32 coins each), ONE maker refresh at a time with its relock errors logged and said, the NEWBLOCK re-back (under the tick's guard here), mirrorCoinLock, afterFill with the fill's txpowid and the remaining the take was checked against (kept on the fill_ record as `verified`). Attestation signing stays OFF on this wallet (no signing key passed; kv attest_sign '0' at every boot).
- From DexiconPro's desk: a fill whose save failed keeps its order's coins locked and refuses further takes of it until applied (retried every tick); every take is checked against the true remaining (unapplied fill records deducted; a take is refused as busy while one is not applied); a save that did not land, a refused take and a failed coin refresh are said on the Exchange screen. An unanswered fill (no txpowid) is applied under its own id, never counted twice.
- main/dexicon.js: the page may send exactly `coinexport coinid:`, `coinimport data:0x… track:false` (146 to 16000 characters) and `verify publickey: data: signature:`; never `sign` or `cointrack`. Its SQL gains exactly the store lock's statements (ids myorders / coinimport, the engine's token charset) and the fenced orders save; it may not write the orders store any other way, a maker fill record, the attestation settings, the coin reservations or the backing-coin settings.
- The native Exchange screen: the ladder shows only the FAST orders the page's takeable view shows (an order this node cannot take is no market), with the page's hidden-order line; coinless orders say "waiting for its coins"; Settings has the FAST backing-coin settings (refresh old coins, ON by default; coin max age 1 to 900); SWEEP sends (FAST take-only first, then the on-chain remainder as a take-only LIMIT, one confirmation with both parts and the worst price, results with every transaction id whole).

## [0.19.405] — Pandamonium Desktop has the Pandamonium app's icon (the panda), not Minima Core's

- The owner: "Pandamonium desktop needs the Pandamonium logo/icon as the apk". The icon is drawn from the Pandamonium APK's own launcher vectors (apks/base app/src/main/res/drawable/ic_launcher_background.xml + ic_launcher_foreground.xml, the Pandamonium flavour's orange pupils `launcher_pupil` #F7931A), as Android shows it: the black background, the panda over it, the launcher's visible 72 dp of the 108 dp canvas. On the Mac that square fills the standard icon body (824 px rounded square, corner 22.5 %, 100 px margin in 1024), the same shape and margin as Minima Core's icon.
- build-pandamonium/icon.icns (16 to 1024, @2x: the Dock, Finder, and the DMG's volume icon), icon.ico (16 to 256: Windows), icon.png (1024: Linux), named in package.json flavors.pandamonium.icons and applied by scripts/build-flavor.cjs; a flavour without icons is refused. The released app (minimaCore) keeps build/icon.*.
- scripts/pandamonium-icon.py redraws the three from the APK (Pillow + iconutil; vector geometry at 4096 px, reduced to every size: nothing is upscaled).
- Unchanged: the menu-bar tray icon is the Minima M, which is also the APK's monochrome (themed) icon layer; the in-app logos are the APK's own (it does not show the launcher panda inside the app).
- Tests: scripts/flavor-test.cjs (the flavour's three icon files exist with every size; the released build's icon paths are not touched).

## [0.19.401] — The real-node proof script follows 0.19.400 (released only on a matched block), with --cases and --offer-minutes

- scripts/megammr-import-private-chain.cjs: `--cases older,crash,behind,mismatch` runs a subset; `--offer-minutes` shortens the app's 10 minutes for the Keep offer and the "not receiving new blocks" line, and the transcript says so. The older-file case waits for the release or the stuck line (then Resync node); the other-genesis case checks that nothing is released, then presses Keep.
- Run 2026-11-23 (`--cases older,mismatch --offer-minutes 2`): the older file was held at "block 50 of at least 57", then released when "block 57 is the same block as before the import and the node's tip is fresh (block 62, 8 s old)"; the other genesis' file was NOT released ("catchup" from block 40 to 47 of at least 60, Keep offered after 2 minutes), and Keep released it.
- Not reproduced on this run: the stuck-on-orphan case (it came about by chance in an earlier run: the node stayed on its file's last block, "Invalid TxPoW Super Parents"); a lone -test node mines its own blocks, so a stall cannot be forced here. The stalled banner is covered by scripts/importfile-test.cjs.

## [0.19.400] — After a MegaMMR file import the trading engines start only when one of the node's own earlier blocks is found again; freshness alone never releases

- The owner's decision on the gap the 0.19.399 run found (a file from another genesis, ending at a block under 30 minutes old, was released before any block was compared): in "catchup" the engines are released only when BOTH hold: the node has reached a block height recorded before the import and holds the SAME block id there, and its tip block is at most 30 minutes old.
- Until then the banner says "Catching up after the MegaMMR import: block X of at least Y" (Y: the lowest recorded height), with "Keep the imported chain" after 10 minutes (the owner decides). A different id at a recorded height is "mismatch", as before.
- The stuck case (a node left on its file's last block after the network orphaned it: "Invalid TxPoW Super Parents" for every next block): it stays held, and after 10 minutes without the block number growing the banner adds "The node is not receiving new blocks. Resync node gets it moving again." with what Resync does to a MegaMMR, and offers Resync node now. Information, not a verdict; the line goes when the number grows.
- Tests: scripts/importfile-test.cjs (fails on 0.19.399); zh / ru / uk.

## [0.19.399] — The real-node proof script follows the simplified marker (third review's cases)

- scripts/megammr-import-private-chain.cjs (manual, not in the gate; same refusals as before): an older file with its RPC cut at 300 ms; a crash with the balance intact (cleared by itself, no banner); a crash after a payment newer than the file (banner "less", then My balance is correct); a crash with a payment arriving while the app is down (banner, then Resync node); a node cut off from G that mined its own fork, importing a newer file from G (allowed; the check says block N differs, Keep the imported chain releases); a file from another genesis. Payments are sent again when a -test chain drops one; L is put back on G's tip (Resync node) when it cannot catch up by itself.
- Found on the run (2026-11-23): a file from ANOTHER chain whose last block is under 30 minutes old is released at once by the freshness rule, before any recorded height can be compared ("the node's tip is fresh (block 40, 1139 s old); none of its earlier blocks could be compared"). Not changed here: it is what the third review's direction asks for; left for the owner to decide (a one-line alternative: release only once the tip has reached the tip recorded before the import, so a recorded block is always compared; until then "catching up", with Keep after 10 minutes).
- Found too: after importing a file whose last block G then orphaned, L stayed at that block ("Invalid TxPoW Super Parents" for every next block) until a Resync node; the freshness rule had already released it.

## [0.19.398] — Third review's minors: the Terminal waits too, Quit says what a quit leaves, refusals are said

- While an import, a restore, a MegaMMR switch or a resync runs, the Terminal refuses megammr*, archive, backup and restore with the same plain reason (main/seedgate.js checkCommand asks main's busyRefusal).
- Quit asks during a Resync node, a restore and Turn MegaMMR off too, and its words fit: only the import's own command can leave the chain half-replaced ("…the balance will be incomplete until Resync node"); before it, "The import has not replaced anything yet…"; otherwise "Quitting now interrupts it. Your funds are safe…".
- The tray's Restart node and the stopped bar's Start node say a refusal (an import running) instead of swallowing it; the user's own "Keep it running" stays silent.
- The dev preview's stub of the removed wwEnableMegammr is gone.
- Tests: scripts/megafile-guard-test.cjs (1 new, 1 updated; both fail on 0.19.397); zh / ru / uk.

## [0.19.397] — The MegaMMR import's marker, simplified (third review): verify first, no permanent verdicts, an exit that destroys nothing on every banner

- **What is left** (main/megammrfile.js, top of file): one marker, four stages. `switching` (written before the node is first restarted into MegaMMR mode; holds nothing; next launch offers Finish the import or Turn MegaMMR off, and clears itself if MegaMMR is off). `importing` (written right before the import command with the wallet's balance, confirmed per token, and coin count, plus the anchors): a crash, a failure after the reset or a broken MegaMMR leave it, and it means VERIFY FIRST: once the node is up, the same balance and coin count clear it silently and release the engines; only a different one shows "Your balance shows less than before an unfinished MegaMMR import. Your funds are safe." `catchup` (the import's reply was good, or the node printed "All saved.."): released as soon as the node's tip is fresh (its tip block at most 30 minutes old); meanwhile "Catching up after the MegaMMR import: block X", and Keep after 10 minutes. `mismatch`: a recorded height holds ANOTHER block id on the new chain, said as exactly that ("Block N on this node before the import was <id>; after the import it is <id>. One of the two is a fork."), never "the file is from another chain".
- **Exits**: every banner has one that destroys nothing: "My balance is correct: dismiss" (the balance read again and shown whole, then cleared), "Keep the imported chain", "Keep the chain as it is". Resync node from the banner says first that on a MegaMMR node it EMPTIES the MegaMMR (megammrsync.java clears it) and offers "Turn MegaMMR off and resync" or "Resync and keep MegaMMR mode (empty until re-imported)". No "Check against the host": megammrsync speaks Minima's P2P protocol to it and the app has no sanctioned read of another node's blocks, so none was invented.
- **Removed**: the "no growth for 30 minutes = another chain" verdict, the "foreign" and "failed" stages (an old marker with them is verify-first and clearable, like any odd marker), and the refusals of a file whose tip is more than 100 blocks above this node's or whose block list starts at or above it (a stuck or lagging node is when an import is needed). A file whose MegaMMR ends more than 30 days of blocks behind this node is said in the import's question, never refused.
- The reset signal is "MegaMMR loaded.." (megammr.java). A marker that cannot be written, or a status listener that throws, no longer turns a good import into a failed one.
- The trading engines: held by every stage but `switching` (main.js onEnginesRelease and at launch), and never released by the import while a seed or backup restore holds them (its own settle does). Mail, miniMall, Vestr, Future Cash Next and P2P Chance pause only while an import actually runs, not for the life of a marker. The banner says what is held.
- Tests: scripts/importfile-test.cjs (3 new, the rest moved to the new stages; 9 fail on 0.19.396); mainsaid; zh / ru / uk.

## [0.19.396] — A manual real-node proof of the MegaMMR file import (scripts/megammr-import-private-chain.cjs), not in the gate

- `node scripts/megammr-import-private-chain.cjs --jar <minima jar> --out <empty dir> [--base 29900]` starts THROWAWAY private -test -nop2p nodes of its own (a genesis MegaMMR node G, a second genesis node G2 = another chain, and the app's real NodeManager as a light wallet node on G), drives the app's own main/megammrfile.js over them, and kills everything it started at the end, on error and on SIGINT / SIGTERM / SIGHUP. It refuses to run unless --out is empty, base >= 29900, every port it would use is free (it talks only to nodes it started), each node says -TEST, L's block 1 is G's genesis and G2's genesis differs. 127.0.0.1 only. The config is a JSON file under --out, so the import's marker really is on disk.
- Cases: **timeout** (the import's RPC cut at 300 ms while the node imports: followed from its log, not restarted, finishes, the chain check releases the engines); **kill** (the app dies when the node prints "IBD blocks processed..": the marker is on disk, a new launch holds the engines and refuses to release them, Resync node clears it); **foreign** (a file exported by G2: imported, then found out by the chain check, the engines never released; Turn MegaMMR off + Resync node is the way out; its no-growth bound is shortened from 30 to 2 minutes for the proof, and the transcript says so).
- Run on 2026-11-23 with minima-1.1.2.31-euro14-block.jar: all three as described (transcript in the commit message of this version).

## [0.19.395] — The MegaMMR file import, second review's minors: engines held before open work is read, the file fingerprinted, truthful messages, the other apps' loops held, the Web Wallet points to the import; a broken MegaMMR said once per node start

- **Order**: the trading engines are held first, then open work is read (nothing new can start in between), then the stop guard; a refusal lets them go.
- **The file's size, modification time and inode** are taken before the node checks it and compared before the import: "This file was not imported: it was changed while the node checked it (choose it again)."
- **Messages say what happened**: a check that ended without an answer says the node did not finish checking (not that it refused the file); an out-of-memory error (in the reply, or the node's own uncaught line) says so, with the memory it had and what to raise ("set Parlons Node memory higher and press Apply and restart the node"); a coin-proof failure is cut at " @ " (the reason, not the coin's JSON); FAILED on a node that already kept a MegaMMR adds that Resync node also empties that MegaMMR (megammrsync clears it), so a file must be imported again afterwards.
- **The import's skip question is its own** ("Import without these checks?": the engines stay stopped until the import has finished; the wallet is not replaced), never "Replace the wallet…"; Freeze says "A MegaMMR file import is running, and Dexicon's engines are stopped for it", not "The wallet is being replaced"; main's log line for the skip no longer says the wallet was replaced.
- **Mail, miniMall, Vestr, Future Cash Next and P2P Chance** are stopped when the import holds the engines and their start sites refuse during the import and while its marker is set; they start again at the first node status after.
- **The Web Wallet's "Enable MegaMMR & restart node"** (which switched on an EMPTY MegaMMR) is gone: its gate says to import a MegaMMR file and its button opens Resync from a MegaMMR file. `mcd:wwEnableMegammr` and its preload entry are removed.
- NITs: "Your MEGAMMR/Cascade is BROKEN" is believed only as MinimaLogger's own line; its notice is said once per node start (bootTs, kept after a halt), not once per window.
- Not done, on purpose: the reviewer's "about twice the room" for a re-import on a node that keeps its own MegaMMR. The owner's measured import (0.19.393) was that case (the node had a 663,414-coin MegaMMR loaded) and peaked at 5.5 GB for 1.5 GB: four times the file covers it, twice that would refuse a file the owner has just imported on 6 GB.
- Tests: scripts/importfile-test.cjs (3 new, 3 updated; fail on 0.19.394); scripts/megafile-guard-test.cjs (main.js hands the flow every dependency it reads: written after a mid-line comment hid fs, totalmem and platform in this very change before the gate caught it elsewhere); scripts/mainsaid-test.cjs; apps-screens, inflight, dexicon-host, casino-glue pins; zh / ru / uk.

## [0.19.394] — A MegaMMR file from another chain is refused by its tip, or found out after the import; the trading engines wait until the node's own blocks are on the new chain and it is at the network's tip

- **Before anything is touched** the node's own chain is recorded: its tip, the block 3 back (a tip can be orphaned) and the block 300 back (final), with their ids (`block`, `txpow block:n`). Not readable: refused ("status could not be read"), nothing changed.
- **The integrity reply's tip** (`chaintip` = the file's cascade tip + its blocks; `cascadetip`) is compared with this node's tip before the import. Refused, the chain untouched: more than **100 blocks above** (this node may trail the network a little: about 83 minutes at Minima's 50 s target), a block list that **starts at or above this node's tip** (none of this node's blocks could be found in it), or more than **51,840 blocks below** (30 days at 50 s: an export is made to be used within days; an older one is a stalled chain or slower to catch up from than a fresh file is to fetch). A check followed from the log after a timeout has no reply: then the blocks found after the import decide alone.
- **After the import** the marker stays as "catchup" and the engines stay held until, at a node status, one of the recorded blocks (deepest first) is found on the new chain with the same id AND the node's tip block is no older than 30 minutes (at the network's tip). A recorded height holding another id: **"foreign"** at once. No growth towards the old tip for 30 minutes, or past it at the tip with none of its blocks found: "foreign" too. "foreign" releases nothing; the banner says the file is from a different chain, the funds are safe, and offers Turn MegaMMR off and Resync node. While it only catches up, the banner says so, without a button.
- Tests: scripts/importfile-test.cjs (2 new, the stand-in node now has a chain; fail on 0.19.393); scripts/mainsaid-test.cjs; zh / ru / uk.

## [0.19.393] — The MegaMMR file import shows every step live, with the node's last log lines; the room check is refitted to the owner's mainnet import (four times the file)

- **The owner's complaint** (mainnet, 2026-11-23: a 1.5 GB file, 663,588 coins, about 4.5 minutes with nothing on screen): the import's screen now shows, for the whole flow, what the node is doing, said in the app's language from its own log lines: restarting in MegaMMR mode, loading the node's MegaMMR (n of total coins), reading the file, checking its blocks, checking coins (n of total), reading the file's MegaMMR, replacing the chain, importing blocks (n of total), reading your coins, saved and restarting. Under it, the node's last 8 log lines, whole, redacted as the Logs view is (node-manager emits each fresh line through `scrub`). Live from the first step, not only after an RPC timeout; said at most every 400 ms, the last line of a burst included; opening the screen again shows it too.
- **Room for the import, refitted**: the owner's import peaked at 5.5 GB of its 6144 MB heap during the integrity check, on a node that had its own 663,414-coin MegaMMR loaded. The node's memory must now be at least **four times the file** (1531 MB → 6124 MB: that import passes; 5120 MB is refused). The refusal says which setting and what to press: "In Settings, set Parlons Node memory to {need} or more and press Apply and restart the node" (the plain node: choose the Parlons Node first). The earlier rule (three times plus 1 GB) let a 1.5 GB file start on 5.6 GB, below its measured peak.
- **"java.lang.Exception: Invalid SyncBlock as NO PARENT! syncblock:N"**, seen once after the owner's final restart, read in core (TxPoWProcessor `processSyncBlock`, called for a peer's sync IBD): a peer sent blocks whose first new block does not hang off this node's tree (its sync window began past this node's tip, which is where a node stands after importing a file a few minutes old). The node drops the rest of that IBD, disconnects that peer to reconnect, and asks for the blocks as full TxPoW; it catches up from there. Harmless once; a sign of a gap only if the node then does not reach the network's tip, which the import's catch-up check is to say (next).
- Tests: scripts/importfile-test.cjs (the stages, the live lines, the window's wording, the room rule; 4 fail on 0.19.392); zh / ru / uk.

## [0.19.392] — An unfinished MegaMMR file import leaves a trace that survives a quit or a crash; the engines stay held and a banner offers Resync node; a slow import is followed, never killed

- **The marker** (config `megammrImport`, written only by main/config.js `setMegammrImport`, never from the window): written right before the node is given `megammr action:import`, cleared only on success or on a failure from before the node reset its chain. While it is set (at launch too) the trading engines stay held (`onEnginesRelease` refuses, whoever asks; at launch they are held before the node starts), and every screen shows a banner: "Balance incomplete after an unfinished MegaMMR import. Your funds are safe. Resync node rebuilds the chain from {host}." with **Resync node now** (config megammrHost; one press, never by itself; no usable host opens the Resync node screen). A Resync node that ends well (any screen or the Terminal) clears it and releases the engines.
- **Pre-reset and post-reset failures are told apart** from the node's own log: the import prints "Streaming IBD.. blocks:n" right before `archiveResetReady`. A failure before it says that nothing was imported or reset (no Resync); after it, the marker stays and the message says Resync node. Only lines with MinimaLogger's prefix are believed.
- **A success the node itself calls broken** (its boot says "Your MEGAMMR is BROKEN") releases nothing: the marker stays.
- **No answer is not an end (M3).** When the integrity check or the import ends without an answer (the RPC's one-hour bound) while the same node process is still there, it is not restarted: its log is followed (the step is shown on the import's screen: "The node is working: IBD blocks processed.. 2560/34000"), and it is given up only after 30 minutes with no new step or when the node exits. A followed import that finishes ("All saved..", then the node exits by itself) is started again by the flow.
- node-manager emits each fresh node line (`line`) and carries `megafileStep` and the marker's stage in its status.
- Tests: scripts/importfile-test.cjs (3 new, 2 updated; 5 fail on 0.19.391); scripts/mainsaid-test.cjs (the two new messages); zh / ru / uk.

## [0.19.391] — Nothing stops, restarts or resets the node while a MegaMMR file import (or a restore, a MegaMMR switch, a resync) runs; Quit asks first

- **Refused, with the plain reason** ("A resync, an import, a restore or a MegaMMR switch is already running. Wait for it to finish. Nothing was changed."), while `megafile.busy() || node.resyncing`: Stop, Restart (Node tab, tray, Save & Restart), Start, Start again, the node kind switch, Contribute, Startup Params' Save (any change to how the node starts: params, extra arguments, data folder, ports, memory, kind, network), Resync, Rebuild, the Web Wallet's MegaMMR switch and the Terminal's commands that reset or stop the node. All of them already went through main's `mayStop`; the check is there (and in `saveConfigPatch` / `mcd:nodeStart`). The import and the restore ask the stop guard alone from inside their own exclusive (`guardStop`).
