A restart in the middle of an import used to cut it after the node had reset its chain; a Startup Params save of MegaMMR off made the import's own restart delete the MegaMMR it was writing.
- **Quit during an import asks**: "Quitting now leaves the chain half-replaced. Your funds are safe, but the balance will be incomplete until Resync node (Backup & Recovery) has run." Stay open is the default. A quit the system asked for (logout, power off) is not held behind it.
- **The window disables** those buttons meanwhile: the node's status carries `megafileBusy` (what main/megammrfile.js is running).
- Tests: scripts/megafile-guard-test.cjs (new, in test:import; fails on 0.19.390); zh / ru / uk for the two new texts.

## [0.19.390] — The MegaMMR questions say what is true: a trust warning on the import, no memory claim, what catches the node up; Startup Params' MegaMMR switch asks; Restore says the +256 key uses

- **Resync from a MegaMMR file's question**: adds "Import only a file from a source you trust. The node checks that the file is whole and holds together, not that it is the real Minima chain: a wrong or crafted file shows a different chain and different balances." Removes the memory figure (the node's memory is a setting, and the room is checked by the app). No longer suggests Resync node (megammrsync clears the MegaMMR: megammrsync.java). Says what catches the node up: its peers, by itself, as after any restart.
- **Startup Params' MegaMMR switch** (and its checkbox under All parameters) asks when it moves away from what is saved: off shows the same deletion warning as Turn MegaMMR off; on says the MegaMMR starts empty and is incomplete until a file is imported, and points to Resync from a MegaMMR file. Proven on the private chain: switched on without an import, the node's MegaMMR held 0 coins and did not know an old coin (`coins megammr:true coinid:` → []). Cancelled, the switch stays as it was.
- **Restore from file's question** says that every key's use count is raised by 256 (restore.java `updateIncrementAllKeyUses(256)`).
- Tests: scripts/importfile-test.cjs (the three questions and the switch wiring; fails on 0.19.389); zh / ru / uk for every new text.

## [0.19.389] — A broken MegaMMR or Cascade at boot is said, with the button that fixes it, on every launch, and Turn MegaMMR off works with the node stopped

- Proven on a private chain: a MegaMMR file whose MegaMMR is newer than its blocks, imported, makes the node print "Your MEGAMMR is BROKEN (does not start 21 where tree ends 19)" at every start. Without -mobile (the contribute role) it then HALTS (exit code 0) at every start, and the app said only "node exited unexpectedly (0)"; with -mobile (the light role) it goes on running with that MegaMMR.
- node-manager: the boot lines "Your MEGAMMR is BROKEN" and "Your Cascade is BROKEN" set the exit's words (fatalHint): what the node says, that the wallet, keys and funds are not touched, and exactly what to press (Turn MegaMMR off on the Node tab; for the Cascade: Rescue node in Startup Params, Save, Start again, and once more after the rescue stops it). `cascadeBroken` joins `megammrBroken` in the snapshot; both are read again at every start, so every later launch says it.
- The Node tab shows **Turn MegaMMR off** whenever the boot said the MegaMMR is broken, running or stopped (and Startup Params for a broken Cascade); with a running node it also shows the note. The notice (an action sheet with Turn MegaMMR off) comes from the node's status once per launch, not only from the import's reply. Its words no longer say "the node started" or guess a cause. Backup & Recovery's Turn MegaMMR off row and page work with the node stopped. Proven: with the node halted (state error), Turn MegaMMR off saved the setting, started the node light, megammr.mmr deleted, balance unchanged, caught up.
- Tests: scripts/importfile-test.cjs (the boot lines, the hints, the reset, the Node tab and hub wiring; 2 of 13 fail on 0.19.388); scripts/mainsaid-test.cjs (both hints in zh / ru / uk; fails on 0.19.388).

## [0.19.388] — Resync from a MegaMMR file (review of 0.19.387): the whole file checked by the node before anything is reset, a failure part-way restarts the node, the engines held throughout

- **The file is read by the node's own reader before the chain is touched.** `megammr action:import` reads the file's MegaMMR, then RESETS the chain (megammr.java `archiveResetReady`: cascade, chain tree, archive and txpow databases; network, timers and miner stopped) and only then reads the blocks, so a bad file used to leave the node answering but gutted. Now, after the switch to MegaMMR mode and before the import, the app runs `megammr action:integrity file:"…"` (changes nothing in the node; reads the whole file, checks that its blocks follow its MegaMMR and every coin's proof). Proven on a private chain: a truncated file and a file whose MegaMMR is newer than its blocks are refused there, and the node is restarted as it was (MegaMMR off again when this flow turned it on), balance unchanged. Before the node is touched at all, the file's first bytes must be a MegaMMR export's (versions 1 and 1, the MMR's block time and entry number: `importfile.megammrHeader`), so a renamed file or a saved web page is refused at once.
- **Why some exported files make "Your MEGAMMR is BROKEN"** (found on the private chain): the exporting node writes its live MegaMMR but its block list from a cache up to 100 s old (core IBDStore.createComplete), so an export made within 100 s of another one, or of a peer's sync, can carry a MegaMMR newer than its blocks (G: `megammrtip` 18 → 21 with the same cached `ibdtip` 52; integrity: "not incremental.. last_in_mega:21 new_block:19"). Such a file imports, then the node halts at every start. The integrity check refuses it before the import.
- **A failure part-way** (status false or no answer): the node is restarted at once; when this flow turned MegaMMR on, the setting goes back first, so nothing half-imported is kept as a MegaMMR. The screen says the import failed part-way, that the node was restarted, that the wallet and funds were not touched, and that until a resync the balance can show less than is held (Resync node).
- **The trading engines are held for the whole import** (node.onEnginesHold / releaseEngines; Freeze does not let them go meanwhile), and the import is REFUSED, before anything changes, while a swap, a take, a pending pool transaction or anything else Restore refuses on is open (this wallet's own standing pools are not: the wallet is kept).
- The MegaMMR setting: only `{ megammr: true }` is saved (config.save merges params), always before the import's restart, also on a node already in MegaMMR mode; a failed status read is "unknown" (nothing done, or the setting put back to exactly what it was), never "off". A MegaMMR node whose memory setting changed since it started is restarted first. Room per node kind: the plain Minima node gets Java's default memory, a quarter of the computer's. A file inside the node's own databases folder is refused. One of Import, Turn MegaMMR off, Restore from file at a time, and none while the node runs a resync. The file is checked again right before the integrity check and the import; the picked file is forgotten after use. The window reads its config again after a failed import. The path refusal also covers U+200B..U+200F, U+061C, U+2060, U+FEFF. The whole-file check has the import's one-hour reply bound.
- The flow moved out of main.js into main/megammrfile.js (create(deps), no electron) so it can be driven against a real node. Tests: scripts/importfile-test.cjs (the flow over stand-ins in every order and failure; header, characters, databases folder; 6 of 13 fail on 0.19.387), scripts/mainsaid-test.cjs (every new sentence in zh / ru / uk).

## [0.19.387] — MegaMMR is an option: Resync from a MegaMMR file turns it on, Turn MegaMMR off goes back to a light node

- Owner: the node stays light by default, with an option to turn MegaMMR on together with importing a MegaMMR snapshot file. **Resync from a MegaMMR file** no longer refuses a light node. One guided flow (main.js `mcd:importMegammr`): the file chosen in the open dialog → room checked (below) → the question, which says that a light node is switched to MegaMMR mode and restarted, stays in MegaMMR mode, and that Turn MegaMMR off goes back and deletes the file → the stop guard → on a light node: the `megammr` setting SAVED, then the restart (so it takes the MegaMMR heap, 3072 MB unless Parlons Node memory is set), wait until the node runs and says it keeps the MegaMMR → the stop guard again → `megammr action:import file:"…"` as a resync (the node stops itself; it is started again with the setting already saved). Order on purpose: the node imports a MegaMMR file only while it keeps the MegaMMR (megammr.java: "MegaMMR not enabled"), and a start without -megammr deletes databases/megammr.mmr (MinimaDB.java loadAllDB), so the setting is saved before both restarts. A node that does not come back in MegaMMR mode imports nothing, and the setting is put back to light (it had no MegaMMR to lose).
- **Room, before anything changes** (`importfile.roomProblem`): node memory at least about three times the file plus 1 GB (the 460 MB snapshot fits the default 3072 MB; the 1.6 GB unpruned file needs about 6 GB: raise Parlons Node memory), this computer's memory at least that plus 1 GB, the data folder's free disk at least twice the file plus 512 MB. Each refusal says the figures in MB.
- **A MegaMMR that does not fit its chain**: the node's boot line "Your MEGAMMR is BROKEN … please 'reset' your node" (MinimaDB.java loadAllDB; with -mobile the node goes on with a start-up error) is noticed during the boot only (main/node-manager.js `megammrBroken`, in the snapshot, reset at every start). After an import that ends so, the screen says what happened, that the wallet, keys and funds were not touched (nothing of the wallet was replaced), and how to go back to a light node (Turn MegaMMR off, then Resync node if it is behind).
- **Turn MegaMMR off** (a Backup & Recovery row on a MegaMMR node): the warning (the node restarts light; megammr.mmr in the data folder is DELETED at that restart; turning MegaMMR on again needs a file to import; the Web Wallet tab needs MegaMMR; the wallet is not touched), the question, the stop guard, the setting saved, the restart (`mcd:megammrOff`).
- Not changed: Startup Params' own MegaMMR switch still saves and restarts without that warning.
- Tests (scripts/importfile-test.cjs): the handlers run from main.js over a stand-in node and config: a light node switched on (saved before its restart, asked again, guarded again, imported, left on); a switch that does not take (nothing imported, the setting put back); a MegaMMR node; the guard saying no; broken after the import; no room for memory, this computer's memory or disk (nothing changed, no guard asked); Turn MegaMMR off in its order. The room figures against the two known files. The boot line believed during the boot only and reset at every start. The screens: both questions before their runs, the deletion said, the row only on a MegaMMR node. scripts/mainsaid-test.cjs: the new refusals said in zh / ru / uk.

## [0.19.386] — Backup & Recovery opens a backup or a MegaMMR file with the system's file picker

- **Restore from file** no longer has a text field for the backup's path: **Choose…** opens the system's own open dialog (macOS / Windows / Linux), in the node's data folder, for a `.bak` file. The main process checks the file (`main/importfile.js`): a plain file (not a folder, a link or a device; a link in a folder on the way is resolved and the real path used), named `.bak`, from 64 bytes to 1 GB, readable, and a path the node can be given safely. The path is shown in full and kept in the main process; the restore runs only on that same path, checked again (`restore.restoreBackup` `d.picked`), so the window can never name a file of its own. The file is never copied: the node reads it where it is. Everything else is the restore as before: the key-mode question, the open-work refusals, the question that says what is replaced, `megammrsync action:resync host: file: password:`, the engines held, the node started again.
- **Resync from a MegaMMR file** (new row): Choose… for a `.mmr` / `.megammr` file (from 1 KB), the question (the chain is replaced, the wallet and seed phrase are not, transactions waiting to be mined are dropped, the node carries on from the file's last block, a large file needs memory), the stop guard, then `megammr action:import file:"…"`, run as a resync (the node stops itself at the end and is started again). The bundled node imports a MegaMMR file only while it keeps the MegaMMR (core/minima-core megammr.java: "MegaMMR not enabled"); this app's default is MegaMMR mode off, so the picker and the import both refuse first, in plain words, saying to switch on MegaMMR mode in Startup Params. `megammr action:import` now has the resync's one-hour reply bound (main/rpc.js; it was 3 minutes).
- **The path in the command.** The node cuts a command at every `;` (quotes or not) and the Parlons admin RPC runs the whole chain and returns only the first reply; it has no escape character; its fast splitter puts spaces round every `:`; `{` / `[` start JSON. So a path holding `;` `"` `:` (but a Windows drive's) `{` `}` `[` `]`, a backslash outside Windows or a control character is refused with a plain message, and every other path goes in double quotes (spaces are fine). Read from the block line 1.1.2.31-euro14, CommandRunner.java.
- **The backup password is never echoed.** The node echoes every parameter, and a refused `megammrsync … file:` carries the inner restore's whole reply, password included ("Error restoring.. {…}"): the restore's reply to the window is now only its status and the node's own reason (`restore.withoutPassword`; "Error restoring.. Incorrect Password!"), and a reason that still names the password is replaced. The node log now also redacts `"password":"…"` / `"confirm":"…"` as a reply's JSON has them (main/nodelog.js).
- Tests (scripts/importfile-test.cjs, new, `npm run test:import`, in CI): hostile file names (`x;quit;y.bak`, a quote with `password:`, `:`, brackets, backslash, line break, tab, a bidirectional control) refused before anything is built, with the node's own reading (ported from CommandRunner.java) showing what each would have run; a cancelled picker; a folder, a link, /dev/null, a wrong extension, too small, too large (sparse 1 GB + 1), a missing file; a path the window invents, a file swapped for a link after it was chosen; the exact text a stand-in node (reading requests as the admin RPC does) receives for a path with spaces, read as ONE restore of exactly that file; the password absent from every reply and redacted from log lines; the MegaMMR handlers run from main.js with the node keeping the MegaMMR or not and the stop guard yes or no. scripts/mainsaid-test.cjs: every new refusal is listed and said in zh / ru / uk; the new screen text is translated.

## [0.19.385] — Dexicon Activity (review of 0.19.384): one time order, truthful pool labels, each swap's own token, an "unfinished" line

- Pool rows: a confirmed row only the history holds (a consolidation, a row from another device or a restore) has a node-checked depth but no inclusion block (the engine's `rowModel` copies only verifiedDepth/verifiedAt/confirmed/statusText), so it read "POOL · PENDING" for ever. PENDING is now `!failed && !confirmed && !(verifiedDepth >= 0)` and not checked yet; a row the node checked and did not find reads "POOL · NOT FOUND ON THIS NODE" (still counted unfinished: it may yet be mined, or was dropped), one without a transaction id "POOL · NO TRANSACTION ID SAVED". A pool row's key is its transaction id, not its list position.
- Order (`DexCore.activityMix`): where block order and time disagreed, a day header was drawn twice, and a row in a block with no time was drawn under "1 JAN 1970" and dragged the rows after it. The list is now ONE order by time (the day headers can never repeat); at an equal time the later block first, a row in a block before one that is not, then kind, then key. A row in a block with no time takes the time of its nearest block neighbour that has one; a row with no time at all goes last under UNDATED (a time before 2001 counts as none); no 1970 header is ever drawn.
- AtomiX rows name their own Minima-side token (ATOMIX · USDT ⇄ MxUSD / MINIMA), not the market the ⇄ pill is on now.
- Safety: when any row is unfinished (a pending take or on-chain order, an AtomiX swap not finished, so with Check now or Refund now, a pending pool operation or one the node did not find), one line above the list says "N unfinished — show"; it shows only those rows, in the same order, and toggles back ("Showing only the N unfinished — show all"). Nothing is reordered.
- Screen readers: a FAST / on-chain row's button says its kind and PENDING (hidden text inside the button; the drawn label above it is hidden from the reader). The About Activity text says the lines are in time order. zh/ru/uk for every new text.
- Tests: the reviewer's two probes (block order against time; a row in a block with ts 0), the drawn screen (each day header once, never 1970, UNDATED last; the unfinished line and its filter; the hidden kind text), history-only / not found / no id pool rows, each AtomiX row's own token. All fail on 0.19.384.

## [0.19.384] — Dexicon Activity is one list: FAST, on-chain, AtomiX and pool rows together, newest first

- Owner: a FAST order filled from Dexicon could not be found in Activity, because My activity drew AtomiX swaps first, then pool operations, then the Exchange's trades, each in its own section. His words: "They should all be together in chronological order, mixed." My activity is now ONE list (rooms ① and ③), each row marked with its kind above its card (FAST BOOK, ON-CHAIN, ATOMIX · USDT ⇄ MxUSD / MINIMA, POOL) and PENDING (accent colour) while it is not in a block yet. Order (`DexCore.activityMix`): rows a block holds are ordered by block among themselves (a later block first; in one block the later time first); every other row (pending, failed, an AtomiX swap, which spans two chains) by its own time (FAST / on-chain: the time it was sent; pool: the node's transaction time, else the submit time; AtomiX: created); the two runs are interleaved by time, a row in a block first at an equal time; last tie-break kind, then key. Day headers as before. Each card is the same card the sections drew (AtomiX's Check now / Refund now / hide, every identifier whole and copyable); room ② Activity, History and Market history are unchanged.
- The Exchange's rows are now the page's own merge (`AX.activity.merge`, the vendored ui/activity.js and the phone's ActivityLog do the same): every FAST fill with no activity row of its own (the maker's side, and older fills) is a row too. Before, the desktop drew only the stored `activity` rows, so a maker's FAST fill was never in Activity (only in History), although the screen's own help said "every fill your node co-signs". Its facts open with the txpow id, order, counterparty and output coins whole.
- Pool rows: the 60 newest and every unfinished one (was: the unfinished first, then up to 60); the block is the one the node's own check found (pp_txproof inclusionblock). Activity's help text says how the list is ordered (zh, ru, uk translated).
- Tests: scripts/dexicon-ui.test.cjs (the ordering rules and tie-breaks; the screen drawn over the real AX.activity with a taker fill, a maker fill, an AtomiX swap and a pool swap: one list in time order, kinds and PENDING marked, the maker row's facts with every id whole), scripts/dexicon-ax.test.cjs and scripts/dexicon-pp.test.cjs (the real screen files' Activity rows: time, block, pending, card, no section). All fail on 0.19.383.

## [0.19.383] — A manual proof of PandaPools on a throwaway private chain (real host, real engine, real node)

- `scripts/e2e-pandapools-private-chain.cjs` (MANUAL, never in CI, not a package script, not packaged): drives the real host shim (`main/pandapools.js` with its runtime-guard hook, the signing queue and coin reservation of `main/rpc.js`, its serial action queue) over the real vendored engine, and the real Dexicon host, against a node at a given RPC port. It refuses to send anything unless the port is 29000 or above and the node's own `status` / `network` / `txpow block:1` say: a -TEST chain, no peer connected or connecting, p2p disabled, block 1 less than a day old. Every other machine is refused (netfetch stand-in). Steps: mined MINIMA, a split, `tokencreate` (8 decimals), two pools of different prices through the host's createPool (with the complete backup it requires before the second), four swaps (MINIMA to token small and large, token to MINIMA small and large), then two more once every pool coin has left the node's tree; for each: the txpowid, the mined transaction (found by its inputs in the block that created the new pool coins, since `txnpost` answers the id before mining), every pool re-created with exactly the quoted reserves, the trader's output coin of exactly the quoted amount, the pp_activity row, the engine's own posted-to-mined mapping, `txnlist`, coin locks, input claims and reservations; then idle blocks recording what the background service posts by itself; then the Dexicon on-chain engine's start (READY, and the time per kind of command).
- `main/pandapools.js` `_setCreateTokenForPrivateChainProof` (the one seam the proof needs: MxUSD does not exist on a private chain): the token createPool makes pools of. It throws when `app.isPackaged`, and nothing but the proof names it. Without it createPool makes MxUSD pools only, as before. Tests: scripts/pandapools-e2e-seam.test.cjs (packaged refusal; the MxUSD-only refusal before anything is sent; no app file, IPC handler or package script names the seam or the proof; scripts/ is not in the packaged files).

## [0.19.382] — Dexicon's on-chain engine reaches READY on a slow node (a new block no longer hijacks its start)

- The on-chain (resting) engine never became ready ("its start did not finish within 5 minutes (a node command did not answer)"). Reproduced with the real vendored engine: the host fired NEWBLOCK while the engine's boot was inside its own key load (`PDService.loadKeys`, one `runscript` per wallet key); the refresh this started saw keys not ready yet and began a second key load (`retryKeysIfStale`), which replaced `PDService.keyLoad`, so the boot's own load returned silently and the boot never reached READY — every retry the same. `main/dexicon/service.desktop.js` (BOOT FIRST) now delivers NEWBLOCK to the resting engine only once its boot has ended READY (the boot reads the tip and refreshes by itself when it ends); a boot that fails is retried as before. The consolidate path's refresh is guarded the same way.
- Test in scripts/dexicon-host.test.cjs: a node whose key address reads take 600 ms and whose tip moves at every read; the engine reaches READY (fails on 0.19.381). scripts/dexicon-ui.test.cjs (allow-list test) now waits for the resting engine to finish starting before it counts network calls: the engine's first refresh (and its price read) now comes when its boot ends, no longer at the first block.
- The same fault can occur in the MiniDapps (mds/dexicon service.js and mds/pandadex-mds service.js deliver NEWBLOCK to `PDService.refresh` whether or not the boot has finished): reported to the donor, not changed here.

## [0.19.381] — A pool swap is no longer refused on every real node ("Script with that address not found")

- Every PandaPools swap stopped before signing with "the node could not say which key signs one of the coins it would spend … (its "scripts" command): Script with that address not found". The engine's signing check (`ReserveRecovery.checkSignature`) asked the node `scripts address:` with each input's address lower-cased; the node looks script rows up case-sensitively (stored upper-case), so it never found one. Fixed at the source, PandaPools MiniDapp 0.6.45 (2c99fbb07c7d493e5ace025fe5284295b8e9cbc5): the address goes to the node exactly as the coin row carries it, and the reply is still compared ignoring case. `main/pandapools/reserve-recovery.js` re-vendored (the only engine file that changed), `scripts/pandapools-parity.manifest.json` re-pinned.
- The stand-in nodes of the PandaPools tests echoed any address back, which is why the tests passed: `scripts address:` now answers case-sensitively, by the address exactly as the coins carry it, like the real node (scripts/pandapools-recovery.test.cjs, pandapools-signgate.test.cjs, pandapools-checkmode.test.cjs). New test in scripts/pandapools-recovery.test.cjs (fails on 0.19.380).

## [0.19.378] — Settings: Appearance, with Follow the system / Light / Dark for the whole app

- There was no way to choose light or dark: the app only followed the OS (the phone's shell has no in-app choice either: it follows Android's own Dark theme setting). Settings now has an Appearance card with three choices, Follow the system (the default, and what every saved value other than "light" / "dark" means, including the released app's old "current" / "original-*"), Light and Dark, saved as config `theme`.
- The main process makes the choice Electron's `nativeTheme.themeSource`, before the window is created (the window background and the first paint are already in it: no flash) and at once on every save (no restart). Electron then makes `prefers-color-scheme` follow it in every page, which is what style.css and shell.css switch their tokens on, so the whole shell changes at once; on Follow the system it follows the OS live. The renderer's `data-theme` (chat.css) is set from the first frame, no longer after the first IPC.
- Mail keeps its own Paper / Dark (its Settings, 0.19.1, as the phone's Mail): it is Mail's own choice inside Mail and does not follow the app's; the Appearance note says so. Dexicon's own sun button and History are 0.19.379.
- Tests (scripts/shell-test.cjs): the saved choice x the OS preference -> the applied theme for every value (legacy and garbage included), only the three values saved, applied before the window exists and on a save, the three choices in Settings; the old "no toggle anywhere" test is replaced on purpose (the owner asked for the toggle); scripts/core-screens-test.cjs: Appearance in the Settings card order. zh / ru / uk.

## [0.19.377] — PandaPools can write again: the host answers `checkmode`, and a refusal of the engine's guard is said

- PandaPools could not do anything that writes (a swap said "could not get a payout address", with "The node's last answer while this ran (its \"checkmode\" command): Command not found."). The vendored engine's write guard (main/pandapools/runtime-guard.js) asks `checkmode` before every command not on its read-only list. `checkmode` is an MDS command: the block-line node jars and the plain minima.jar this app runs answer "Command not found", so the guard refused EVERY write without sending it, and paused the background service at every block (retrackOwn, keep-fresh, re-announce, rescue-flow and ReserveRecovery's signature check were not running either; the pause went to MDS.log, a no-op here).
- main/pandapools.js now answers `checkmode` itself, as the Dexicon and P2P Chance hosts do: write mode true (the node's own RPC is its admin interface); `dblocked` is the node's own `status` "locked" (the same `!isBaseSeedAvailable()` that checkmode reports, Minima status.java / checkmode.java), from node-manager's 10 s health poll, false until the node has been read. Never sent to the node. Vendored engine untouched.
- A refusal of the guard (WALLET_LOCKED / MODE_UNAVAILABLE / WRITE_REQUIRED) is kept as the newest failure (`ppWhy`, `guard: true`, through the guard's own `onBlocked` hook) and room ② says it in plain words instead of the engine's sentence ("This node's wallet is locked: unlock it and try again. Nothing was sent."). A pause of the background service is shown above every screen of room ② and written once to the node log ("[pandapools] paused: …", then "running again"). zh / ru / uk.
- Tests: scripts/pandapools-checkmode.test.cjs (new; all four fail on 0.19.376) runs the real host shim over the real engine against a stand-in node that answers `checkmode` "Command not found" as the block jars do: getaddress reaches the node, a swap build goes past the payout address, the service becomes READY, a locked node is refused, said and lifted. scripts/engine-abort.test.cjs (now in test:pandapools; its "held" test fails on 0.19.376) and scripts/pandapools-signgate.test.cjs answer `checkmode` as the jars do (signgate's host answer is lifted from main/pandapools.js); scripts/dexicon-pp.test.cjs: the guard's reasons.

The same on-chain orders qualify; a pool may fill very slightly less, never at a worse price." ## [0.19.375] — Dexicon: SWEEP across FAST and on-chain is worked out as on the phone and shown, not yet sendable

- With a POOL bid above a FAST bid, a click on the FAST row left two buttons, each taking one source. The phone (Pandamonium 2.2.109's Dexicon) offers a third, SWEEP, that takes both. Its rules are ported into renderer/dexicon-core.js in exact decimals: `sweepSplit` (MainActivity.split: on-chain takes all it holds at or better than the best FAST price plus what FAST cannot cover, FAST the middle up to what it offers, on-chain capped at what it holds at the limit), `sweepAvg` (cut to 8 decimals; the phone rounds half up) and `decideBoth` (no sweep unless both parts are more than zero). The on-chain numbers are the hosted page's own plan: a new read-only page action, `quote` (renderer/dexicon/mds.js), returns PandaComposite.plan's totals for a side, a 6-decimal limit and an amount; it sets nothing, answers no dialog and sends nothing. Routing and pool maths are not re-implemented.
- Exchange › Trade: when both cross, SWEEP is drawn above the two single-source buttons in the phone's words ("SWEEP SELL 1734.56789012 · POOL + FAST · avg 0.00447473", "up to 500 instant, then 1234.56789012 on-chain"), and is off, with the reason: the phone's part 2 is an on-chain trade of exactly the re-quoted amount that rests nothing, but the page's only on-chain message is a LIMIT, and the engine (resting/pd_service.js) rests whatever of a LIMIT does not cross when it plans, or all of it when nothing crosses then, with no review. The donor needs a take-only LIMIT first (docs/PANDAMONIUM_DESKTOP_PLAN.md). zh / ru / uk.
- The FAST orders the book, the row click's cumulative amount and the sweep count go through one function (`takeable`, unfiltered), so a later change can leave out orders this node cannot take; no change in behaviour.
- Tests (scripts/dexicon-ui.test.cjs): SweepBothSplitTest.java's three cases, same inputs and splits (the average cut, 0.00316302, where the phone rounds to 0.00316303); decideBoth over a stand-in of the page's plan: the owner's case splits 500 FAST + 1234.56789012 on-chain, the quotes are at 0.004413, no sweep when either part is zero or the FAST list passed in is empty; the quote action is read only and refused while not ready; the investigator's repro on the Trade screen (the FAST row under a POOL row: SWEEP shown with FAST 500 and on-chain 1234.56789012, off and said why; BOOK / POOL alone sends 0.004413). All fail on 0.19.374; the action list check now includes `quote` (changed on purpose).

## [0.19.374] — Dexicon: BOOK / POOL from a FAST row's 8-decimal price is sent on the 6-decimal grid, not refused

- Clicking a FAST row fills the FAST order's exact price, which may have 8 decimals (0.00441234). BOOK / POOL then always answered "An on-chain order takes a price of at most 6 decimals. Nothing was sent.": on-chain prices sit on a 6-decimal grid (resting orders at 6 decimals, pool depth on 0.00001 ticks).
- The on-chain action is now sent at the typed limit cut UP to 6 decimals for a SELL and DOWN for a BUY (renderer/dexicon-core.js chainLimit, exact decimals). Every 6-decimal level at or better than the typed limit still qualifies and none worse than it does; the amount is not touched. A pool trades in small slices at its marginal price, and a slice priced between the typed limit and the 6-decimal one is left out, so a pool part can only be a little smaller, never at a worse price. When the sent limit differs from the typed one the review says so first: "On-chain prices have 6 decimals: your limit 0.00441234 is sent as 0.004413 (the same on-chain orders qualify)." The page's own review already names the price a remainder rests at ("Rest … MINIMA @ 0.004413 as a limit order after the trade confirms"), which is the sent price. The confirmed intent records the sent price, so main/dexicon.js matches the page's LIMIT. A BUY limit below 0.000001 is refused with its own sentence. The page's 6-decimal guard (renderer/dexicon/mds.js) stays as the last line of defence. zh / ru / uk.
- Tests (scripts/dexicon-ui.test.cjs): over every 8-decimal limit from 0.00440000 to 0.00443000 against 35 6-decimal levels, a level qualifies for the sent limit exactly when it qualifies for the typed one, both sides, and the sent limit is never worse; the owner's case end to end on the Trade screen (POOL bid 0.0045 over a FAST bid 0.00441234, the FAST row clicked: BOOK / POOL sends 0.004413 with the amount 1734.56789012, the review's first line, the intent at 0.004413); the intent-source check now wants the sent price (changed on purpose). All fail on 0.19.373.

## [0.19.373] — Dexicon: while the on-chain order book is not ready, the screen says why and asks for nothing

- After a wallet restore the on-chain engine (the vendored PandaDEX service) could stay not ready, and the Exchange screen did not know: BOOK / POOL stayed live, the page's own review was shown, and after the user had confirmed (twice, see below) the engine dropped the order with "PandaDEX is still starting…". A start that ends in the engine's own error (covenant mismatch, a script that could not be registered, the wallet identity not read, trade-history storage) was seen by nobody: the screen keeps only the engine's STATE, and the desktop wrapper only reset its boot timer.
- main/dexicon/service.desktop.js: an error that ends a boot of the on-chain engine (or a boot that hangs for 5 minutes) is logged once with the engine's message, also to the node log ("on-chain engine not ready: …", main.js), kept as the reason it is not ready (status().restingWhy, and restingWhy on every STATE packet the page reads), and the boot is still tried again every minute.
- Exchange › Trade: while the on-chain engine is not ready, BOOK / POOL is off and the ticket says why in one sentence: "The on-chain order book could not start: <reason>. It is tried again every minute. FAST orders are not affected." or "The on-chain order book is still starting. Nothing can be sent to it yet." A click shows no review and records no intent. renderer/dexicon/mds.js refuses an on-chain place before the page plans or asks anything while the engine's STATE is not ready (or it is building another transaction of this wallet), with the same sentence and "Nothing was sent.". zh / ru / uk.
- Why two confirmations: the review loop shows a new dialog whenever the page's review text differs from the text last confirmed ("The book moved. Review again"). A not-ready engine still re-reads the book and pools on every block (resting/pd_service.js refresh runs on NEWBLOCK whatever its state) and a boot retry once a minute re-reads the wallet's orders, so the page's plan text changed while the first dialog was open; the second confirmation matched, the LIMIT went to the engine (one recorded intent each time), and the engine refused it with a stage line, not an error, so nothing said it had failed. Now no dialog is shown for an on-chain action while the engine is not ready.
- Tests: scripts/dexicon-ui.test.cjs: the ticket's sentence, BOOK / POOL off, no dialog, no drive and no intent for a not-ready engine, with and without a reason; the page refuses before its review when STATE is not ready, gives the reason, refuses while busy, and still places a FAST order (all fail on 0.19.372); scripts/dexicon-host.test.cjs: a boot whose newscript is refused logs the engine's message once (node log line), shows it in status() and on STATE, and the next try goes ready with no reason (fails on 0.19.372).

## [0.19.372] — Future Cash Next starts again; its signature limit and notify switch work

- main/main.js passed Future Cash Next its options on one long line, and two later comments were typed into the middle of code: from 0.19.194 `dir: () => app.getPath("userData")` sat after a `//` on that line, so the engine never got its folder and every start threw "o.dir is not a function" (Future Cash Next could not start at all); from 0.19.212 `signCap` and `notify` sat after a `//` on the FC_OPS line, so changing the daily signature limit (and the notify switch) answered "Unknown Future Cash action.". The code is out of the comments again; the comments' text is kept.
- main/futurecash.js: create() refuses at once, naming the option, when run, dir, get or log is missing or not a function (the options it calls without asking first; log because a failed start is said only through it).
- Tests: scripts/source-wiring-test.cjs (new, in test:apps): no desktop-authored main/*.js or renderer/*.js line has code after a `//` comment (two known notes allowed by their exact text), and every `api.fc("…")` action renderer/futurecash.js calls is a key of main.js FC_OPS (both fail on 0.19.371: they name main/main.js:476 and :499, and "signCap"); scripts/futurecash-test.cjs: create refuses a missing or non-function run / dir / get / log (fails on 0.19.371).

## [0.19.371] — miniMall: a paid order keeps the note that another buyer's order has its reference (as Minima Core Desktop 0.18.315)

- Once an order with a secret was paid in full by its proved coins, every note on it was dropped (0.19.368), also the sentence that another buyer's order without a secret has its reference and was paid (0.19.366). So when a copier's order without a secret was paid by the real buyer's coin without its proof, and the real buyer then also paid with the proof, both orders ended PAID and neither said anything about the clash (review-desktop-r6 H4N1). Only the note of a coin without the order's proof is dropped now (it begins "a payment with this order's reference does not carry this order's proof"); the duplicate sentence stays.
- main/shop-store.js changes: its pin in scripts/apps-money.test.cjs is updated on purpose.
- Tests (scripts/shop-store-test.cjs): N1 now wants the duplicate sentence kept on the real order once its proved coin pays it, the copier's order still paid (changed on purpose: it wanted the note gone; fails on 0.19.370); an order with only a no-proof note still loses it once paid in full (unchanged).

## [0.19.370] — miniMall: coins of two worthless tokens taking turns no longer write the store once per coin (as Minima Core Desktop 0.18.314)

- The store was written whenever a coin of another token changed the token paid or the order's status, so coins of two worthless tokens taking turns wrote shop.json once per coin: 2000 such coins with an order's reference wrote it about 1000 times (0.8 MB each with a 400-order store), and again after every restart, as such coins are not remembered (review-desktop-r6 H5d). It is now written only when the order's status changes. The token paid is not shown anywhere.
- main/shop-store.js changes: its pin in scripts/apps-money.test.cjs is updated on purpose.
- Tests (scripts/shop-store-test.cjs): H5d (2000 coins over two worthless tokens: at most 2 writes at first sight and at most 2 after a restart, WRONG_TOKEN, and the real payment still makes it PAID; 977 writes on 0.19.369).

## [0.19.369] — the settling question says the buyer's total and what was received; no "as released" for versions not released; no shortened address

- miniMall: the check-by-hand question for a settling order did not say the buyer's total and what was received, which every other check-by-hand question here, and Minima Core Desktop's settling question, says. It now does: "The buyer's total: … (their own figure)" and "Received so far: …", before "Confirm only if …" (zh / ru / uk).
- Comments, test comments and this changelog called Minima Core Desktop 0.18.304 to 0.18.308 released: those versions are not released. They now say "as Minima Core Desktop 0.18.304" to "… 0.18.308". Commit subjects are left as they are.
- main/nodelog.js's header quoted the node's log line with the AtomiX HTLC address shortened; it now names it whole: 0x0CDCD61692F186EB0BBCFA289F438043F586FF7B3F6864193358E29166E8454A. main/atomix.js's comment on the publish pin named the PandaPools beacon address shortened; it now names it whole, 0x50414E4441504F4F4C53 ("PANDAPOOLS", main/pandapools/service.js SENTINEL).
- main/shop.js and main/shop-store.js change (comments only): their pins in scripts/apps-money.test.cjs are updated on purpose.
- Tests (scripts/shop-store-test.cjs): S3's check of the settling question now wants the buyer's total and received lines before "Confirm only" (changed on purpose; fails on 0.19.368); the check that the check-by-hand question is asked before the call allows 2000 characters for the question's text (1800: the two added lines made it longer).

## [0.19.368] — miniMall: the no-proof note names the coin's amount and token; a paid order drops its note (as Minima Core Desktop 0.18.311)

- The note left on an order with a secret by a coin with its reference and without its proof named only the coin's id, so a vendor could not tell a stranger's dust or worthless token from a buyer's real payment made without the proof (review-desktop-r5). It now names the coin's amount exactly as the node gave it (never rounded) and its token id whole, or Minima: "a payment with this order's reference does not carry this order's proof (payment coin <id>, amount <amount>, token <token id or Minima>): check by hand"; renderer/mainsaid.js reads it back (SHOP_NO_PROOF) and it is said in zh / ru / uk. A note may now be 600 characters long (it was cut at 300), so no id in it is cut.
- A note says "check by hand", and it stayed on an order after it became PAID, where no check-by-hand button is offered. Once an order is paid in full by its proved coins, its note is dropped (the simpler of the two choices; notes are only ever on orders with a secret, which only proved coins pay). An unpaid order, also one only part paid, keeps its note.
- main/shop.js and main/shop-store.js change: their pins in scripts/apps-money.test.cjs are updated on purpose.
- Tests (scripts/shop-store-test.cjs): the note with a 66-character coin id, a 63-character amount and a token id whole (over the old 300-character cut), read back and said in zh / ru / uk with every value whole, kept while unpaid and part paid, dropped once paid; "Minima" with the amount's trailing zero kept; N1's note goes once its proved coin pays it. Changed on purpose: the no-proof sentence in the tests; "the note stays shown" on a paid order is now "the note goes"; S2a, S2b and S2d's paid orders carry no note. All fail on 0.19.367.

## [0.19.367] — miniMall: a coin given up on and counted after all no longer holds its order (as Minima Core Desktop 0.18.310)

- A payment coin whose judging threw on three passes is given up on and leaves "payment coin <id> could not be judged: check by hand" on its order (0.19.350). After a restart the coin is judged again and counted, but that problem still held the order: UNCHECKED, never PAID (review-desktop-r5 R6; the released app paid it). When a coin is counted for an order, that order's problem for exactly that coin now goes; when no problem is left, the order is checked against the listing again exactly as a fresh order is (what is due, and any way it departs from the listing, which still holds it), so it is paid as before. Any other problem on it still holds it.
- main/shop-store.js changes: its pin in scripts/apps-money.test.cjs is updated on purpose. It compares the stored order state UNCHECKED (a wire value, never translated in main), so scripts/i18n-test.cjs lists it beside WRONG_TOKEN and UNDERPAID.
- Tests (scripts/shop-store-test.cjs): R6 (paid after the restart); the same with a second buyer's order with the reference (still held, the coin counted); the same with the listing's price changed meanwhile (held by the listing check). R6 fails on 0.19.366.

## [0.19.366] — miniMall: a newcomer with a secret that leaves a paid order without one as it is says so in a note (as Minima Core Desktop 0.18.309)

- Since 0.19.360 a second buyer's order WITH a secret leaves a PAID order without one as it is. That newcomer was kept silent: PENDING, nothing said, and a check by hand was refused (review-desktop-r5 N1). When its buyer had paid outside the app without the proof, that coin paid the other order, and the vendor could neither see the clash nor act on the real order (the released app put the duplicate problem on it, so it could be checked by hand). It now carries the duplicate sentence as ONE note: "another buyer sent an order with the same reference …: a payment with it cannot be told apart, so check both orders by hand" (said in zh / ru / uk as the problem already is). A note holds nothing (its own proved coin still pays it), and an unpaid order with a note can be checked by hand.
- main/shop.js changes: its pin in scripts/apps-money.test.cjs is updated on purpose.
- Tests (scripts/shop-store-test.cjs): N1 (the real order shows the note, can be checked by hand, and is still paid by its own proved coin); N1b (paid by its own proved coin: no note, the copier's order held as before); S5's newcomer now carries the note (changed on purpose). N1 and S5 fail on 0.19.365.

## [0.19.365] — Dexicon lanes test: the limit part waits for at least the two stuck posts, not for exactly two

- A third race in the same test, seen once in the 30-run stress of 0.19.364 (two copies of the suite at once): the limit part waited for exactly 2 posts at the stand-in network. That is true only from the second stuck post's arrival until the host's 400 ms limit frees a place and "after" goes out (then 3 are held, since the stand-in never answers the stuck ones); a poll starved past that window never saw 2 and timed out after 8 s ("timed out waiting for: () => net.held.length === 2"). It now waits until both stuck posts have reached the network (2 or more held); the timing check of 0.19.364 is unchanged. With the test's first poll made 500 ms late, 0.19.364's test times out and this one passes. Nothing in the app changes.

## [0.19.364] — Dexicon lanes test: "not before the limit" is timed by when the requests reached the network

- A second race in the same test, which 0.19.363 made more frequent: the limit part took its start time when the test's 15 ms poll saw the two stuck posts at the network, and asked that the next post go out at least 300 ms later. On a busy machine the poll saw them late (and 0.19.363 runs that part on a freshly booted host, whose engine is busiest then), so the next post seemed to follow in less than 300 ms although the host's 400 ms limit had run in full: "after the limit, not before" failed in 10 of 30 runs with two copies of the suite at once. The stand-in network now stamps each post when it arrives, and the test asks that the next post arrive at least 300 ms after the first stuck one did. The same bound, now measured where it happens; with the main thread held busy for 250 ms right after the poll sees them, 0.19.362's and 0.19.363's test fail and this one passes. Nothing in the app changes.

## [0.19.363] — Dexicon lanes test: the lanes' order no longer races the host's own 400 ms limit

- The intermittent `test:dexicon` failure ("Expected values to be strictly equal", review-desktop-r4 G6) is this test's `assert.equal(net.most(), 2)` (scripts/dexicon-host.test.cjs, the lanes test), with 4 !== 2: caught 3 times in 30 runs of the whole suite with two copies running at once. The test booted its host with a 400 ms request limit (`netLimitMs`) for all of it, and holds low0 and low1 at the network from the first posts until it lets them go after its order, take and long-poll checks. On a busy machine that took longer than 400 ms: the host's limit then ended both requests and freed their places, as it is meant to (main/dexicon.js makeLane), the order and the take went out, and the stand-in network, which still held the two abandoned posts, counted 4 at once. A 450 ms pause at that point gives the same failure every time. The lanes themselves never had more than 2 of their own requests out.
- Now the lanes' order, drops and priorities are tested on a host with the app's own limit (30 s), and the limit itself (a request that never settles frees its place, not before 300 ms) on a second host with 400 ms. No assertion is weakened or removed; nothing in the app changes. With the 450 ms pause the test passes.

## [0.19.362] — miniMall: the review's nits (a coin given up on flags the order it would pay; held in the list), as Minima Core Desktop 0.18.308

- A payment coin given up on after three passes that threw (0.19.350) put its problem on the order kept under the coin's reference, also when the coin proves another buyer's copy of it (ref~buyer): it now lands on the order the coin would pay, whenever that is known. One whose reference could not be read names no order, so no check by hand cleared it from the Inbox: the Inbox now offers Hide for those (the same Hide as the unread notice's). At most 64 coins given up on are said; the oldest go first (each was logged with its error, and its order keeps its problem).
- The Inbox's list showed a settling order as plain PAID, and only its page said it was held: the list row now carries a "held" mark beside its status (zh / ru / uk).
- Comments: the settling comments said every order from the coin's block up was known read. That is not so on a first pass whose orders read was cut, nor after a backfill that ended with blocks it could not read: there an order settles as if those blocks had been read, as released 0.18.286 paid it at sight. The comments now say so; nothing there changes.
- Not here: this app never wrote 0.18.299's waiting list (payWait), so nothing is dropped on load; no shortened covenant address is in this tree's changelog or tests.
- main/shop.js and main/shop-store.js change: their pins in scripts/apps-money.test.cjs are updated on purpose.
- Tests (scripts/shop-store-test.cjs): the coin given up on lands on the victim's copy, not the copier's order; 70 coins whose reference cannot be read: the newest 64 said, Hide clears them; a settling order is held in the list (fails on 0.19.361).

## [0.19.361] — miniMall: a check by hand made while an order settles does not outlast its hold (as Minima Core Desktop 0.18.307)

- A settling order can be checked by hand to move it on before its hold runs out (0.19.354). That check made it immune to the second order its hold was waiting for: the vendor checked a copier's settling order, the real order without a secret came 2 blocks later, and the copier's order stayed PAID and confirmable (review-desktop-r4 S3). The check now keeps the block the hold would have run to; a second order without a secret mined up to that block still unpays the order and holds both for a check by hand, unless it was already confirmed or moved on. The check-by-hand question for a settling order now says so: "Another order with this reference can still arrive until block N; it will put both orders back on a check by hand." (zh / ru / uk; the question's former text is replaced in the three catalogs.)
- main/shop.js and main/shop-store.js change: their pins in scripts/apps-money.test.cjs are updated on purpose.
- Tests (scripts/shop-store-test.cjs): S3 with the real order at the hold's last two blocks (held; checked by hand again), one block later (the check stands), and after the vendor confirmed (left as it is); the question's sentence (fails on 0.19.360: the copier's order stays paid).

## [0.19.360] — miniMall: an order with a secret does not unpay a paid order without one (as Minima Core Desktop 0.18.306)

- A PAID order without a secret (not moved on, not checked by hand) was unpaid and held for a check by hand (0.19.344) by any second buyer's order with its reference, also one that came with a secret (review-desktop-r4 S5; released 0.18.286 kept it PAID). An order without a secret is paid only by coins without a proof, and an honest buyer with a secret pays with its proof, so that newcomer cannot be the one whose coin paid it: the paid order now stays PAID, and the newcomer is kept as its own order and waits for its own proved coin. A newcomer without a secret, or with one that cannot be read, still holds the paid order, as does any newcomer an order stored before 0.19.352 (paid by any coin with its reference).
- main/shop.js changes: its pin in scripts/apps-money.test.cjs is updated on purpose.
- Tests (scripts/shop-store-test.cjs): S5 (the paid order stays paid and is confirmed; the newcomer is not paid and not held); a newcomer without a secret, with an unreadable one, or against an order stored before 0.19.352 still holds it (fails on 0.19.359).

## [0.19.359] — miniMall: a coin without an order's proof is a note, never a hold; coins of one block are judged by coin id (as Minima Core Desktop 0.18.305)

- An order with a secret is paid only by coins proving it (0.19.352). A coin with its reference that proved nothing and paid no order went through the duplicate rule: what is due was cleared and the order made UNCHECKED, so a stranger's coin with the reference (a worthless token's coin in the same block as the real payment, a dust coin between a half payment and its top-up, a coin with another proof before the payment) left the order unpaid and not confirmable (review-desktop-r4 S2a, S2b, S2d; released 0.18.286 paid all three). Now it is a note on the order, at most one, the first coin's id whole, shown on the order (in the app's language) and in the check-by-hand question: it does not touch what is due, the status, or whether the order may be confirmed, and the proved payment pays the order as ever. An unpaid order with such a note can be checked by hand (a buyer who paid by hand without the proof).
- Coins of one block were judged the larger first, by Number() of the amount: that compared amounts of different tokens and rounded long decimals. They are now judged by coin id within the block; nothing depends on the amounts' order (a remainder judged before its payment is gathered as dust and counted on the second look, as before).
- The check-by-hand question for such an order is new: "This order is not paid yet:" with the note, then the buyer's total and what was received; zh / ru / uk. main/shop.js and main/shop-store.js change: their pins in scripts/apps-money.test.cjs are updated on purpose.
- Tests (scripts/shop-store-test.cjs): S2a, S2b, S2c, S2d paid by the proved coin with the note kept; an unpaid order with a note is checked by hand; S4 (five variants, and two copiers); coins of one block judged by coin id; the copier and two-secrets tests now expect a note where they expected a problem (CHANGED on purpose) (fails on 0.19.358: S2a not paid).

## [0.19.358] — miniMall: a settled order stays settled; a confirmed order is never held (as Minima Core Desktop 0.18.304)

- 0.19.354 settled an order without a proof of its secret by the last pass's orders read alone, kept in memory and with nothing kept on the order, and the hold did not look at the order's status. So a paid, settled order went back to settling after one failed orders read, a CONFIRMED order could not be shipped after a restart until the first good pass, and a catch-up whose orders read was cut put a settled order back to settling (review-desktop-r4 S1a, S1b, S1c, R4; released 0.18.286 confirmed and shipped them). Now the first pass with a good orders read that finds a paid order settled marks it settled on the order (`settled`, kept on disk), and no failed or cut read, and no restart, takes that away. An order CONFIRMED or later is never held. An order paid before this version (no `settled` on it) is settled, as released.
- Kept: a fresh order without a secret still settles until a good orders read is 3 blocks past its newest coin; a PAID order not moved on that meets a second buyer's order with its reference is still unpaid and held for a check by hand, and that takes its settled mark away with the payment.
- main/shop.js and main/shop-store.js change: their pins in scripts/apps-money.test.cjs are updated on purpose.
- Tests (scripts/shop-store-test.cjs): S1a, S1b, S1c and R4 settled before the gap: confirmable, shippable and deliverable throughout; a CONFIRMED or SHIPPED order with the mark unset is not held; an order paid before this version is settled; a latched order met by a second buyer's order is unpaid and held (fails on 0.19.357: "S1a: not held again by a failed read").

## [0.19.357] — Dexicon lanes test: the queue behind two held posts is bounded above too

- The lanes test asked only that at least 34 requests wait behind the two held WORK places (32 refusals, the order and the take; 0.19.348 allowed the engine's one periodic read as a 35th). Nothing bounded it above, so a queue that grew past what is expected passed unseen. It now asks for 34 or 35, with 6 dropped. A test change only.

## [0.19.356] — miniMall: a payment coin given up on is no longer said once its order is checked by hand (as released 0.18.302)

- A coin whose judging threw three times is left for a check by hand (0.19.350): a problem on its order and "Payment coin <id> could not be judged: check by hand." on the Inbox. The line stayed for the rest of the session even after the vendor checked that order by hand. The coin is now kept with the order it flagged, and checking that order by hand takes it off the Inbox. main/shop.js pin updated on purpose.
- Tests (scripts/shop-store-test.cjs): the "always throws" test checks the order by hand at the end: nothing given up is said (fails on 0.19.355).

## [0.19.355] — miniMall: a tip that comes while a pass is running gets its own pass (as released 0.18.301)

- The poll took a new tip as scanned before scanOnce, which returns at once while a pass is running (`scanning`): a tip that came during a long pass got no pass of its own, and its blocks waited for the next one. Now a poll during a pass leaves the tip for the next poll (`tip !== lastTip && !scanning`). main/shop.js pin updated on purpose.
- Tests (scripts/shop-store-test.cjs): a poll at 1002 while the pass at 1001 is reading its orders, then the next poll scans 1002 (fails with the old condition).

## [0.19.354] — miniMall: a payment is counted the first time it is read again; an order without a secret settles before it can be confirmed (as released 0.18.300)

- 0.19.344 to 0.19.353 left a payment coin unjudged (not counted, not marked) until tip block + 3, and while its orders were not read. `coins` lists unspent coins only and the vendor's own wallet may spend a coin 3 blocks old, so a coin still waiting after tip block + 3 (no pass at exactly that tip, an orders read that failed, a gap above the coin, a reorganisation) could be spent before it was ever counted, and its order stayed unpaid with nothing said (review-desktop-r3 R1, R2, R2b, R4; released 0.18.286 paid them). Now every payments read counts what it lists at once, in every pass, whatever the orders read did. The wait, the Inbox's "counted after block N" line (gone from the zh / ru / uk catalogs too), the skipping of the payments read after a failed orders read and its waiting rule are gone.
- What the wait guarded is now the verdict's: an order without a proof of its secret (no secret, or stored before 0.19.352) that is paid in full SETTLES. It has no next step (the Inbox says "Paid. Held until block N in case another order with the same reference arrives." with "I checked this order by hand"; advanceStatus refuses with "This order's payment is held until block N in case another order with the same reference arrives. Check it by hand to move it on now.", renderer/mainsaid.js SHOP_SETTLING) until a pass whose orders read succeeded has reached N = the newest counted coin's block + 3, with every order from that coin's block up known read. A copier's order mined after the payment is paid at sight but settling; the real order mined up to 3 blocks after the payment meets it as a second buyer's, and the paid one is held again (0.19.344's rule). A settling order can be checked by hand. A coin of unknown block, and an order stored before this version, settle at once, as before.
- An order whose payment proves its secret (0.19.352) does not settle: it is paid and confirmable at sight. A coin with its reference and no or another proof is never counted for it, and is now noted at sight.
- Kept: the per-coin catch and 3 strikes (0.19.350), the "older than the order" hold, the duplicate rule, and the PAID order held again by a second order with its reference. zh / ru / uk for the three new sentences. main/shop.js and main/shop-store.js pins updated on purpose.
- Tests (scripts/shop-store-test.cjs): R1, R2, R2b and R4 from the review, for an order without and with a secret: PAID and confirmable (fail on 0.19.353); the copier sequences rewritten on purpose (the copier's order paid but settling, refused by advanceStatus, then both held); one buyer's order confirmable at tip block + 3 and settling at + 2, after a restart too; a failed orders read settles nothing and a settling order can be checked by hand; a cut orders read whose gap covers the coin settles nothing; R3a with a secret.

## [0.19.353] — miniMall: the desktop buyer's order carries a secret and every payment of it carries the secret's proof (as released 0.18.298)

- Orders placed here carried no secret and their payments only the reference (state port 1), so a desktop buyer had none of the protection 0.19.352 gives an order with a secret, at a phone vendor or at a desktop one. Now placeOrder makes the secret as the phone Shop's `MerchMessage.newNonce` does (32 random bytes from `crypto.randomBytes`, `0x` and capital hexadecimal digits), sends it inside the sealed order (`nonce`, ORDER only, as the phone's `toWire`), and the payment carries its proof in state port 2: `0x` + the SHA-256 of the 32 bytes in capital hexadecimal digits, exactly what the phone Inbox (`DeliveryRules.settle`, `MerchDb.recordPaymentIn`) and this vendor check.
- The buyer's order keeps only the proof (`commit`), so a Retry sends the same proof; the secret itself is kept nowhere (not in shop.json, not in a log, not in what the screen is given). An order placed before 0.19.353 keeps no proof and its Retry carries none. A stored proof that is not `0x` and 64 capital hexadecimal digits is refused before anything is sent: "Invalid order proof." (renderer/mainsaid.js SHOP_BAD_PROOF, zh / ru / uk). main/shop.js pin updated on purpose.
- Tests (scripts/shop-store-test.cjs): the sealed order carries the secret; the payment and its Retry both carry `{"1": ref, "2": proof}`; the secret is not stored, logged or given to the screen; a second order has another secret; the same coins at a desktop vendor pay the order; an order without a proof retries without port 2, and a tampered proof sends nothing. Fails on 0.19.352.

## [0.19.352] — miniMall: a payment counts for an order with a secret only when it carries that order's proof (as released 0.18.297)

- The phone Shop puts a secret in the sealed order (`nonce`: 0x and 64 hexadecimal digits) and its proof on the payment coin, state port 2: `0x` + the SHA-256 of the secret's 32 bytes in capital hexadecimal digits (base-vestr-2.2.76 `comms/MerchMessage.java` validNonce / commitment, `merchshop/PaymentFlow.java` stamp). This vendor ignored both and matched payments by the plain-text reference alone. Now an incoming order keeps the proof of its secret (`commit`; the secret itself is never stored or logged), and a payment coin pays only the order whose secret it proves: the one kept under the reference or another buyer's copy (`ref~buyer`). A copier who puts a payment's reference in an order of their own can never make that payment theirs, whatever order the coins and orders arrive in.
- An order with a secret is no longer held, nor unpaid, by another buyer's order with its reference (each is paid only by coins proving it), unless that order came with the very same proof; a payment proving the order's secret is not held as "older than the order". The 3-block wait and the orders-read rule are kept for every coin.
- A coin with the reference that pays no order (a different proof, or none, while no order without a secret is there to take it) is one check-by-hand problem on each unpaid order with a secret and that reference: "a payment with this order's reference does not carry this order's proof (payment coin {coin}): check by hand", once per order; never on an order already paid by its proof.
- An order with no secret (an older Shop or this desktop before 0.19.353) keeps every rule of before, with the phone's one addition: a coin that carries anything in port 2 never pays it. An order stored before 0.19.352 (its secret, if any, was not read) is paid as before. A secret that is present and not 0x and 64 hexadecimal digits holds the order for a check by hand ("this order's secret cannot be read, so no payment can be proved to be its buyer's: check by hand"), never taken as an older Shop's.
- The two sentences are in renderer/mainsaid.js (SHOP_NO_PROOF, SHOP_BAD_SECRET) and in the zh / ru / uk catalogs (review list). main/shop.js and main/shop-store.js pins updated on purpose.
- Tests (scripts/shop-store-test.cjs): the phone's own vectors (its Java run as it is); the copier sequence in every arrival order (copier first, real first, already paid, same block, real order's read failed, real order past the tree), with the copier with and without a secret; a copier copying both the reference and the proof; no-secret orders and orders stored before this version; nine malformed secrets; the secret in no file, log or screen reply; two orders with different secrets, the same secret re-sent, the same proof from another buyer, one without a secret. All seven fail on 0.19.351.

## [0.19.351] — Vestr: the node's mempool answer decides a second collect; the session list is gone (as released 0.18.296)

- 0.19.347 kept the coins collected in this session and refused them again while the node still listed them. `checkmempool:true` already leaves out a coin that a collect is really spending, so the list refused exactly the collects that had NOT gone through (an unanswered post whose coin is still listed and not in the mempool), for the rest of the session. It is removed; both coin reads keep `checkmempool:true`.
- The phone's rule (base-vestr-2.2.76 `Contract.alreadySpending`, its `l10n_vestr_already_spending` in en / zh / ru / uk): a clean, successful, empty answer to `coins coinid:<id> checkmempool:true` refuses with "A collect of this coin is already waiting for the chain, or the coin is already spent. Nothing was sent." — but not while the node has no tip (it then lists nothing): the tip refusal comes first. A failed or malformed answer does not block: the coin is read without the flag, and `txninput` and `txncheck` judge the collect; "Contract coin not found (already collected?)" only when neither read finds it. The old sentence ("This contract coin was collected a moment ago…") is gone from main, renderer/mainsaid.js (VESTR_ALREADY_SPENDING in place of VESTR_COLLECTED_HERE) and the zh / ru / uk catalogs.
- Tests (scripts/inject-test.cjs): the session-list tests replaced on purpose (V1: the clean empty answer, the tip first, five failed or malformed answers that do not block; the 0.19.239 test: a post that may have gone, its coin still listed, collected again); V4, the review's scenario: an unanswered post, the coin still listed and not in the mempool, three list refreshes, collected again (refused on 0.19.350). main/vestr.js pin updated.

## [0.19.350] — miniMall: one payment coin whose judging throws no longer stops every newer one (as released 0.18.295)

- Since 0.19.346 a coin whose judging threw aborted the whole payments loop: every newer coin was left unjudged on every pass, the cursor never moved, and the Inbox said the node did not answer (it had). Now every coin is judged; a coin that threw is not marked seen, and the pass is a failed one for that coin (nothing advances, the same blocks are read again) with the Inbox line "Payment coin {coin} could not be judged. It is judged again on the next block." (the id whole), never as the node not answering.
- After 3 passes that threw, the coin is marked seen, logged with its id whole and the error, and left for a check by hand: the problem "payment coin {coin} could not be judged: check by hand" on its order (so it cannot advance automatically; said through renderer/mainsaid.js, SHOP_JUDGE_GAVE_UP) and "Payment coin {coin} could not be judged: check by hand." on the Inbox; the cursor then moves. The count is kept in memory for this session (after a restart a coin gets 3 passes again); one try per pass. zh / ru / uk.
- Tests (scripts/shop-store-test.cjs): ORD-P1's coin always throws, the unrelated ORD-P2 is paid in the same pass, the cursor stays for two passes and moves on the third with the problem and the log line (on 0.19.349 ORD-P2 is never paid); the single-throw test changed on purpose (scanState says the coin, not the node; the cursor). main/shop.js pin updated.

## [0.19.349] — miniMall: no payment is judged before the orders that may go with it are read (as released 0.18.294)

- When a pass's orders read failed, its payments were still judged, so "orders are read before payments" broke: payment 1010, a copier's order 1011, the real order 1013 and a channel read that failed at the judging tip paid the copier's order (PAID, may advance) until the next good pass. Now a pass, a backfill step or the read past the tree whose orders read failed judges no payment; the coins wait, unseen, for the next pass.
- Complete enough: a payment coin from block pb is judged only when every order from pb up to the tip is known read (the orders that may go with it are mined up to pb + 3). For the recent window that is from what this pass's orders read reached when it reached the last pass's tip (and then, with a backfill pending, from its floor), otherwise only what it reached; for a backfill step, from what its orders read reached (with what was read above the floor), and everything once it reaches the gap's target; past the tree, the root read judges what it lists, except that while a cut orders read is still tried again the coins below what it reached wait, and the try that ends the backfill judges them.
- Tests (scripts/shop-store-test.cjs): the failed orders read at the judging tip (recent window and past the tree); a cut window, step and read past the tree each holding back the payment until its real order is read (the copier's order is PAID after the first pass on 0.19.348); a step that reaches its target judges what it lists below it. main/shop.js pin updated.

## [0.19.348] — miniMall: a payment is judged at tip block + 3, before the wallet can spend it (as released 0.18.293)

- 0.19.344 judged a payment coin created at block pb only from tip pb + 4. The node's `send` may spend a coin from tip pb + 3 (coin age `MINIMA_CONFIRM_DEPTH` = 3), and `coins` lists unspent coins only, so any send of the vendor's own wallet (a miniMall reply, chat, Web Wallet, a Vestr lock, an AtomiX publish) could spend the fresh payment in block pb + 4 before it was ever counted: the honest order stayed unpaid, with no problem recorded and nothing to check by hand, and the "a payment has arrived" line vanished. A payment now waits only while the tip is below pb + 3 and is judged by the pass at tip pb + 3; orders are still read first, so a real order mined up to block pb + 3 is held by then, and a spend posted at tip pb + 3 is mined in block pb + 4 at the earliest. The waiting line says "It is counted after block pb + 2" (the same sentence in every language, the figure one lower).
- The waiting line is cleared only by a payments read that read every payout address; one address's failed read cleared it one pass early.
- Tests (scripts/shop-store-test.cjs): the two boundary tests changed on purpose (tip pb + 2 waits, pb + 3 judged); new: order 1009, payment 1010, the wallet's send at tip 1013 mined in 1014, counted by the pass at 1013 (never paid on 0.19.347); the waiting line kept when the coin's payout address read fails at the judging tip. main/shop.js pin updated.
- Test only (scripts/dexicon-host.test.cjs): the lanes test asserted exactly 34 requests waiting and failed now and then with 35, when the engine's own periodic read queued behind the two held posts; it now asserts at least 34 waiting and still exactly 6 dropped (the order and the take wait, nothing more is dropped). test:dexicon passed 10 runs in a row.

## [0.19.347] — Vestr: no second collect of a coin being collected; the node judges every collect before it is posted (as released 0.18.291 / 0.18.292)

- Both reads of contract coins send `checkmempool:true` (the node then leaves out a coin it is mining a transaction for or that a mempool transaction spends), and a coin collected in this session is refused again while the node still lists it: "This contract coin was collected a moment ago and the node still lists it. Wait until it is gone from the list before you collect again. Nothing was sent."
- `txncheck` runs after the build and before `txnpost`; the collect is posted only when it answers status true with `response.validtransaction` the boolean true (as the phone's Vestr), otherwise "The node checked this collect and judged it invalid, so it was not sent. Nothing was collected."
- Refusals in the order: the coin's shape (states 2, 3 and 4 must be plain numbers, the created block a whole number: a malformed grace or end block read as no wait), the tip ("The node's block height could not be read, so nothing was built. Nothing was sent."), "Nothing available to collect yet", the grace wait. The grace wait is counted at the tip (refused while tip < final block and tip − created < the grace blocks): `txncheck` and the node's own relay check of a txpow it mined both run the script with `@BLOCK` = the tip, so a collect only the next block accepts never leaves the node. The list's Collect button reads the same figure (at coin age 0 it no longer offers a collect the app then refuses).
- `decSub` gives "" for a figure it cannot read (it gave "0"); every use treats it as a refusal, and an unreadable "collected" is shown as nothing. zh / ru / uk for the three new sentences.

## [0.19.346] — miniMall: the released tree's behaviour where this tree differed unlisted; the shop test b5c9fbb lost is back; restoresync on a block wallet says what is true there

- A payment coin whose judging threw is no longer kept as seen: the pass fails and the coin is judged on the next pass (it was caught, logged and marked seen, so it was never counted in that session). A payout address whose read threw no longer keeps the other addresses' payments from being judged in that pass (each read is caught on its own; the pass still counts as failed). A "may not have been read" notice stored with no cause says "Orders and payments older than block N may not have been read: the node keeps only its recent blocks." (it said the reply cap's sentence). All three as released (minimaCore Desktop 0.18 tree); zh / ru / uk for the new sentence.
- Tests: the released tree's "a second gap keeps the oldest target" (dropped by b5c9fbb in 0.19.338) is back, and the stall reset when a backfill step reads below its floor, "a failed read moves nothing" past the tree, and the step's `BACKFILL_CHUNK` (512) are pinned; each fails when its line is changed. `docs/PANDAMONIUM_DESKTOP_PLAN.md` corrects its "no shop test was lost" and lists the store-shape differences kept on purpose (each order's own coin list, 500 coins per order).
- `keyuses:` on restoresync above 10000 on a block key wallet: "keyuses: on restoresync is added to how many times every key in the backup has signed; the usual figure is small (the node's own default is 256). On this block key wallet it can be at most 10000: a key's count follows the block number, and a key counted far above the chain's tip stops signing after one signature (the funds stay, but this wallet can no longer sign). Nothing was run." (the classic sentence named the 262144-use limit, which a block key does not have; the bound stays 10000). zh / ru / uk.

## [0.19.345] — miniMall: one "older than the order" problem per order; a re-sent order keeps its oldest block (as released 0.18.290)

- Each old payment coin carrying an order's reference added its own "a payment with this order's reference is older than the order" problem (the coin id made each text new) and a rewrite of `shop.json`; a coin past the order's 500-coin cap, or one of another token, rewrote it too though nothing changed. Now the order gets that problem once, naming the first coin whole, and a coin that changes nothing writes nothing.
- The same buyer's order sent again (a second coin, the same sealed sender): the order's block is now its oldest coin's. The later copy, listed first by the node, set the block and a real payment between the two copies was held as "older than the order". Another buyer's coin with the same reference is a second buyer's order and never moves this one's block.

## [0.19.344] — miniMall: a copier's order is never paid by another buyer's coin (as released 0.18.289)

- A payment coin created at block N is judged only once the chain is past block N + 3 (`PAY_BEFORE_ORDER`); until then it is not judged and not marked seen, and a later pass reads it again (the payments read never skips what an earlier pass read, every window reaches at least 64 blocks below the tip, and the cursor is on disk, so a restart reads it too). Orders are read before payments in every pass, so the real order, mined within the 3-block allowance, is held by then: a copier who put the payment's plain-text reference into an order of their own mined the block after the payment had that order marked PAID before the real order arrived. Now both meet as two buyers' orders with one reference, and neither moves on until the vendor checks it by hand.
- An order marked PAID (not confirmed or moved on, not checked by hand) that meets a second buyer's order with its reference is held again: unpaid, UNCHECKED, the duplicate problem first, what was received still counted. Confirmed, shipped, delivered and hand-checked orders are left as they are.
- While a payment waits, the vendor's order says so: "A payment with this order's reference has arrived (payment coin <whole id> at block N). It is counted after block N+3, so that another order with the same reference is seen first." (zh / ru / uk).
- `docs/DEV_RUN.md` and `scripts/dev-run.sh` name the jars package.json pins now (minima 1.1.2.3-euro8 and parlons-node 0.2.162; the flavour's minima 1.1.2.31-euro14 and parlons-node 0.2.160), not minima-1.1.2.31-euro5 / parlons-node-0.2.153.

## [0.19.343] — Vestr: a collect at or just after the end block is no longer dropped by the chain (as released 0.18.288)
