- The node's contract maths (`runscript` on CHECK_MATHS) works out its `change` before an expired contract's `cancollect` is raised to the whole coin, so at and just after the final block it reported a full payout AND a change: outputs above the input, a transaction `txnpost` took and the chain dropped while the app said collected (a sample contract coin, coin `0x23456789ABCDEF0123456789ABCDEF0123456789ABCDEF0123456789ABCDEF01`). The payout is the node's `cancollect` (a plain number above 0, at most the coin); the change is the coin less the payout, in exact decimals, and there is no change output once the whole coin is paid out. Nothing is built within the grace wait or for a payout above the coin, each said with a plain sentence (zh / ru / uk).

## [0.19.342] — The node jars with the command-injection fix and the RPC hardening

- **Released identity:** `resources/minima.jar` is minima-core 1.1.2.3-euro8 (sha256 `fc63eb672374e7ceb7cfba17d0e098e3c2991c9a0ef124b0a14fae111c088ef9`), and the Parlons node pin moves to 0.2.162 (`parlonsNode` / `parlonsNodeSha256` `65ee56a6ffb302ea0ffcb0d81c1af6ce3f64f0abd662f7885346863d80eb34c1`; fetched from the maxima release `node-v0.2.162`). Both carry the 21 Nov 2026 fix set: built sub-commands are never re-parsed from text (no `;` / quote injection), the RPC binds 127.0.0.1 unless `-rpcbind`, checks Host and browser Origin, reads its body by bytes with a cap, a read-mode RPC user runs as non-admin, and non-admin callers only create plain `.txn` files in the base folder. The app already talks to its node on 127.0.0.1 and passes no flag these jars refuse.
- **Pandamonium flavour** (`flavors.pandamonium.jars`): minima 1.1.2.31-euro14 (block-mode line, `e9399cce238971138d5efe7aa2fba93bfc601bce9e536fcf7477c3c2970e72e0`) and parlons-node 0.2.160 (`e40ad7bc812285ba49a93715df6a9e855dcc7bfd3df9c450bee58b8b89558a39`), the same fixes on the block-capable line.
- Not in these jars (owner, 21 Nov 2026): the wallet-key capacity guard; the node wallet's own keys on the 1.1.2.3 / 1.1.2.4 lines still restart at leaf 0 past 262144 uses, as upstream.
- Test: `scripts/flavor-test.cjs` pins the new digests.

## [0.19.177] — The seed gate covers keys action:genkey too

- **Parity with the released desktop (its 0.18.173, `84d946b`, `main/cmdgate.js`):** `keys action:genkey` prints a new private key, so it is gated like `seedrandom` (SHOW KEY, Touch ID where there is one, that exact command once within 60 seconds), in the main process and in the Terminal's question. Other `keys` commands are unchanged.
- **Kept different on purpose:** the released desktop refuses a `;` chain that holds a gated command; here the whole chain is gated and runs once its own pass is given (the owner's rule: "anywhere in a `;` chain"), so a chain is never half run.
- Test (`scripts/seedgate-test.cjs`): `keys action:genkey` in any case, quoted, or inside a chain is gated; `keys`, `keys action:list`, `genkeys` and a `genkey` that is not the action are not; the window and main agree. It fails on 0.19.176.

## [0.19.176] — The Terminal gates vault and seedrandom; Show seed phrase asks first

- **Owner decision (20 Nov 2026):** the commands that show what controls the funds no longer run on a bare call. `vault` (any action, any letter case, anywhere in a `;` chain) answers with the wallet's seed phrase or handles its keys; `seedrandom` (any modifier) answers with a key made from the seed. Before, the Terminal ran both like any other command, kept them in its history and its Copy output, and Backup & Recovery's "Show seed phrase" put the seed on screen with one click.
- **Main process:** `mcd:cmd` refuses either unless a gate was just passed for that exact command (`main/seedgate.js`, as `ethwallet.exportKeyGated` and the released desktop's `main/seedgate.js`, `e3946acad8e5029e85d4bd65e558dc58c54e7aa0`): the typed phrase (SHOW SEED for `vault`, SHOW KEY for `seedrandom`), then Touch ID where the Mac offers it; the pass is for that one command, used once, within 60 seconds. `mcd:revealSeed` does the same for Show seed phrase. The first-run "Your recovery seed" reads the new wallet's seed through `mcd:firstRunSeed`, which answers only while a new wallet is being made in this launch. Every other command is unchanged. The engines' own uses (AtomiX's and Dexicon's seed-derived keys) are in the main process and are not affected.
- **Terminal:** such a command asks for the phrase in a dialog, runs once on success, and shows its reply in its own window that closes after 60 seconds and when the window is left, says when no system check took place, offers no copy, and is never kept in the output, Copy output or the ↑ history (the output shows only the verb and a note).
- **Show seed phrase:** the same question, then the seed in that window (no longer left in the page).
- New text in the four languages (review lists); main's refusals listed in `renderer/mainsaid.js` (`MAIN_SECRET_CMD`, `MAIN_FIRSTRUN_SEED`, `SG_NO_CHECK`, `SG_NO_SEED`; the phrase and Cancelled refusals reuse the ETH key export's).
- Tests: `scripts/seedgate-test.cjs` (new, in `npm run test:wallet`): which commands are gated (chains, case, look-alikes not) and the window agreeing with main; wrong phrase, wrong kind, a refused Touch ID and an ungated command leave no pass; the pass is for the exact command, once, within 60 seconds; Show seed phrase asks the node only after both checks; `mcd:cmd` checks first; no window code asks the node for `vault` or `seedrandom` itself; the Terminal keeps neither the command nor the reply. `scripts/core-screens-test.cjs` follows the two seed screens to their gated calls. They fail on 0.19.175.

## [0.19.175] — Logs: "seedphrase" and "mnemonic" are redacted too

- **Parity with the released desktop (its 0.18.169, `1c668ae`):** a reply's JSON `"seedphrase"` or `"mnemonic"` field is taken out of every log line and saved log, as `"seed"` and `"privatekey"` are since 0.19.172.
- Test (`scripts/node-log-test.cjs`): both names redacted, a longer name kept. It fails on 0.19.174.

## [0.19.174] — miniMall vendor: a payment mined before its order is counted when the order comes

- **Found while comparing with the released desktop (its 0.18.163, `b349fde`):** `scanPayments` marked every payment coin as seen before it looked for its order, so a payment mined in an earlier block than its order message (the buyer's app sends the message first, but the two need not land in the same block) was skipped for the rest of the session and the order stayed unpaid until the app was restarted.
- **Now:** a coin whose order is not in the store yet is not marked seen; it is read again on the next scan and counted when the order has come (the store counts each coin once, 0.19.167). A coin with no readable ref, or for an order that is already paid or is not a sale, is marked seen as before.
- **Pin:** `main/shop.js` is sha256-pinned in `scripts/apps-money.test.cjs`; the pin is updated on purpose to this file.
- Also: the 0.19.173 entry no longer names a version it could not vouch for.
- Test (`scripts/shop-store-test.cjs`): a payment scanned before its order is counted when the order arrives (PAID), and a later scan does not count it again. It fails on 0.19.173.

## [0.19.173] — Logs: the node's last line is written out when its output has ended

- **From the release review:** the unfinished last line of each of the node's streams (kept back until its line end comes) was written out on the process's `exit` event. Node fires `exit` before the pipes are drained, so output arriving after it waited for a line end that never came and was lost, often the last words of a node that died.
- **Now:** it is written out on `close`, which comes once both streams have ended, so the last line is whole. Output still arriving from a process that a new start has replaced is no longer mixed into the new process's lines, and its `close` leaves the new process's buffers alone.
- Test (`scripts/keymode-test.cjs`, on the stand-in java of the key-mode tests): a half line before `exit` and its rest after it are logged as one line on `close`, after "node exited", with the error stream's unfinished line; a replaced process's late output and its close do not touch the new process's lines. It fails on 0.19.172.

## [0.19.172] — Logs: a reply's "seed" is taken out too

- **From the release review:** the log scrub (`main/nodelog.js` `scrub`, run on every line and again on a saved file) took out the RPC secret, a seed phrase (`phrase:` and `"phrase":`) and `privatekey:0x…`, but not the `"seed"` field of a reply's JSON: `vault` answers with the wallet's seed there, and the test even pinned it as kept.
- **Now:** a JSON `"seed"` or `"privatekey"` field, in any letter case and spacing, becomes `"•••"`; a field merely starting with the word ("seeds") is left alone.
- Test (`scripts/node-log-test.cjs`): the vault reply loses its phrase and its seed; JSON seed and private key fields in other spellings are redacted, others kept. It fails on 0.19.171.

## [0.19.171] — miniMall vendor: only the order's next step can be set

- **From the release review:** `advanceStatus` accepted any state the window asked for and sent it to the buyer: `PAID` on an order nothing had paid (it was then shown as paid on both sides), a jump from PAID straight to DELIVERED, a word that is no state at all. Only CONFIRMED, SHIPPED and DELIVERED were held back for an unpaid order (0.19.166).
- **Now:** the main process takes exactly the one next step the screen draws its buttons from (`AppsAct.shop.nextStatus`, the same function): CONFIRMED, then SHIPPED, then DELIVERED, for an order paid by what the vendor's listing says is due or checked by hand, and nothing for an enquiry. Anything else is refused before anything is sent: "That is not this order's next step." (in the four languages, `SHOP_NOT_NEXT`), or the hand-check refusal of 0.19.166 for an unchecked order. The two allow-list entries of 0.19.166 for the old pattern in `main/shop.js` are gone with it.
- **Pin:** `main/shop.js` is sha256-pinned in `scripts/apps-money.test.cjs`; the pin is updated on purpose to this file.
- Test (`scripts/shop-store-test.cjs`): an unpaid order refuses every state (PAID among them) and sends nothing; a paid one takes CONFIRMED, SHIPPED and DELIVERED only in turn and refuses a jump, a step back and anything after DELIVERED; an enquiry takes nothing. It fails on 0.19.170.

## [0.19.170] — miniMall vendor: the counted payment coins of an order are bounded

- **From the release review (H1):** counted payment coin ids lived only in memory in the released desktop; here they are kept on the order since 0.19.167 (the reviewer's restart probe leaves a half-paid order UNDERPAID on this branch). What was left: the list had no bound, and wrong-token coins were kept in it too, so anyone could grow an order's record (and `shop.json`) one tiny coin at a time.
- **Now:** only coins that are counted (the order's own token) are kept, at most 500 per order (`MAX_PAY_COINS`). A coin of another token is never counted, so it is not kept and seeing it again changes nothing. A coin past the bound is not counted: the order becomes `UNCHECKED` with "more than 500 payment coins came for this order; check the payment by hand" (said in the four languages, `SS_TOO_MANY_COINS`), is never PAID by itself, and the vendor may check it by hand. A coin id longer than 80 characters is not counted.
- **Globally:** a payment coin carries the one ref it was sent with, so it can only ever count for that order; the order's own list is the whole record, and it is bounded.
- **Pin:** `main/shop-store.js` is sha256-pinned in `scripts/apps-money.test.cjs`; the pin is updated on purpose to this file.
- Test (`scripts/shop-store-test.cjs`): 500 counted coins and 50 wrong ones keep a list of 500; the 501st is not counted and the order is UNCHECKED, said once, never paid, and may be checked by hand; the reason is translated. The kept list holds counted coins only. They fail on 0.19.169.

## [0.19.169] — Mail: what a sender writes is stored as capped primitives

- **The same pattern as the miniMall finding (0.19.168), checked in Mail and Chat:** Mail stored an incoming message's fields as the sender wrote them (subject, message, name, ids, payment fields, picture, pay address), so a sender could make every save of `mail.json` throw with one field nested 12,000 arrays deep, or end a whole scan page with a value whose `String()` throws. Chat is not affected: `main/chatschema.js` `chatDecode` already keeps only strings and numbers.
- **Now:** `localOf` builds the stored row from capped strings and finite numbers only (subject 1,000, message 200,000, name 64, ids 128 / 80, amount 64, token name 200, picture 400,000, pay address 90; an optional payment field the sender left out stays absent; a date must be a plain number). A message whose `from` is not a string is skipped. Each coin is read in its own `try`: one that cannot be read is said in the log and the rest of the page goes on. A failed save of `mail.json` is said in the Logs view (`mail-store` `setLog`, once until the reason changes).
- Test (`scripts/mail-test.cjs`): a message whose every field is nested 12,000 deep becomes a row that serialises, every field a primitive, in the empty subject's thread; an honest message is kept as it was; each coin in its own `try` with the log line; `main.js` sends Mail's save failures to the Logs view. It fails on 0.19.168.

## [0.19.168] — miniMall vendor: what a buyer sends is stored as capped primitives

- **Found by the release review of the released desktop (H2), present here too:** an order's items were stored as the buyer sent them. Items nested about 12,000 arrays deep made `JSON.stringify` throw on every save of `shop.json`; the error was swallowed, so from then on NOTHING was saved (new orders, payments, statuses) and it was lost at the next quit. `String()` or `Number()` of such a value throws as well, which aborted the rest of the scan batch.
- **Now:** `routeIncoming` rebuilds every stored order line from its own fields, each a capped string or a finite number (product 200, prices 64, quantity 20, size 60, NFT token id 80; anything else is "" and a line that is not an object is `null`), and caps every other field a buyer or vendor sends (name, shop name, amount, currency, delivery method and address, note, status, reply id and text; a date must be a plain number). A reply with no usable id is not stored. Each coin of the order channel and each payment coin is read in its own `try`: one that cannot be read is said in the log and the rest of the batch goes on.
- **A save that fails is said:** `shop-store` reports it ("miniMall: shop.json could not be saved: …") once until the reason changes; `main.js` sends it, and a coin that could not be read, to the Logs view.
- **Pins:** `main/shop.js` and `main/shop-store.js` are sha256-pinned in `scripts/apps-money.test.cjs`; both pins are updated on purpose to these files.
- Tests (`scripts/shop-store-test.cjs`): the reviewer's 12,000-deep order (nested in a line's extra field, its quantity, a whole line, the amount, the name and the note) is stored as plain lines, the next order in the same batch is read, and both are on disk; a coin whose box cannot be opened is logged and the next one is read; a store that cannot be written says so once, and again after a good save. They fail on 0.19.167.

## [0.19.167] — miniMall vendor: each payment coin counts once, also after a restart

- **Checked against the phone (read only):** the Inbox 0.5.15 (`apks/merchinbox-fix-2026-11-20`, `e2900eb9b3f8f74763c180fe602d41dd97c516c7`): coins of the order currency's token add up, each coin id counted once, and a wrong coin never pays or blocks. The desktop's `recordPayment` summed coins but knew no coin id: the only guard was the scan's in-memory "seen" set, which is empty after every restart (and cleared at 40,000 coins), and the first scan after a start reads the vendor's address again at its deepest (`depth:256`). An order paid in part was counted again at each restart, so a buyer who paid half saw it PAID after one restart of the vendor's app.
- **Now:** `recordPayment(ref, coinid, amount, tokenid)` keeps every counted coin id (lower case) on the order itself, in the store, and a coin it has counted gives nothing; a call with no coin id counts nothing. `scanPayments` passes the coin's id. As on the phone, a coin of another token never pays and never blocks: it does not undo a partial payment, the token shown stays the right one once a right coin has come, and a right-token coin moves `WRONG_TOKEN` on (it stayed `WRONG_TOKEN` before). Only PAID locks the order.
- **Not in the released desktop:** `desktop/minimacore-desktop-sec` (0.18.159) has the same restart double count; nothing there was changed.
- **Pins:** `main/shop.js` and `main/shop-store.js` are sha256-pinned in `scripts/apps-money.test.cjs`; both pins are updated on purpose to these files.
- Tests (`scripts/shop-store-test.cjs`): the same coin id in either letter case counts once, coins of the right token add up to PAID, a coin with no id counts nothing, a wrong coin after a partial payment keeps UNDERPAID, a stranger's tiny wrong coin then the honest payment is PAID, PAID locks; and through the real `main/shop.js` loaded afresh (an empty seen set, as after a restart) the same half payment is not counted twice. They fail on 0.19.166.

## [0.19.166] — miniMall vendor: an order is due what the vendor's own listing says

- **Ported from the released desktop (read only):** `desktop/minimacore-desktop-sec` 0.18.159 (`1b7f8e8a836e67f7469f65d32e2f6828826129d9`, branch `security/backport-2026-11-20`), itself the rule of the phone Inbox 0.5.18 `Listing.check` (`apks/merchinbox-fix-2026-11-20`, `edb77da9c82a4197c04580f49e917505c1fa79e2`). An incoming order's items, prices and total were the buyer's own figures, so a buyer could write a total of 0.000000001 for real goods, pay that and see the order PAID.
- **Now:** `main/shop-listing.js` `check` recomputes what is due from the vendor's OWN stored shop: each line a listed product (name, and NFT token id / edition / bundle where present), at the listed price, a whole quantity above zero within stock (1 for an edition or a bundle), plus the listed delivery fee, exact BigInt; the order's currency must be the shop's. `recordPayment` compares the coins with that figure only: PAID when received >= due > 0 with every line matched. An order that does not match is `UNCHECKED`, never paid by any amount; Confirm, Ship and Deliver are refused in the main process (`advanceStatus`) and on the screen (`nextStatus`) until the vendor answers an explicit "Checked by hand?" question (`mcd:shopVendorCheck`, `handCheck`; it does not mark the order paid). A listing that says nothing is due gives `BAD_TOTAL`. Orders stored before are checked against the shop the first time they are read. Phone-placed orders (items as a JSON string, delivery by id) are read; they were stored with no items before.
- **One change from the released file:** a currency in a problem is named MxUSD or Minima, never by the wire value "USDT".
- **Inbox:** the order list shows what is due by the listing (the buyer's figure marked as theirs when the order is unchecked); the order shows "Buyer's total", then "Due by your listing" or the red "Unchecked: the items or total do not match your shop; check by hand." with every reason and "I checked this order by hand". Every new text in the four languages (review lists); main's new refusals and each reason are listed in `renderer/mainsaid.js` (`SHOP_NOT_DUE`, `SHOP_HAND_ONLY`, `SL_*`) and said in the app's language.
- **Pins:** `main/shop.js` and `main/shop-store.js` are sha256-pinned in `scripts/apps-money.test.cjs`; both pins are updated on purpose to these files.
- Tests: `scripts/shop-store-test.cjs`: due = price x quantity + fee for both wire shapes; every way an order departs from the listing is a problem, each a listed sentence said in every language; whatever the buyer wrote as total, the listing decides; an unmatched order is UNCHECKED, never paid, refused Confirm / Ship / Deliver until checked by hand (and a matching one cannot be "checked by hand"); a phone-placed order is checked and paid end to end through the real `main/shop.js`. `scripts/mainsaid-test.cjs` holds the `SL_*` sentences to `main/shop-listing.js`. Four fail on 0.19.165.

## [0.19.165] — miniMall vendor: an order is paid in the token of the vendor's own shop

- **Ported from the released desktop (read only):** `desktop/minimacore-desktop-sec` 0.18.151 (`dc757ddc7c90d24ea3d40a1a1b100612fdfb909b`). `routeIncoming` in `main/shop.js` took the token an incoming order must be paid in from the currency the BUYER wrote (`tokenFor(w.currency)`), so an order to an MxUSD shop that said "Minima" was marked paid by a payment in Minima.
- **Now:** the token and the currency come from the vendor's own stored shop (`tokenFor(own.currency)`), and so does the shop's name on the order and in the notice; the notice says MxUSD, never USDT. The wire value "USDT" in the stored order is unchanged.
- **Pin:** `main/shop.js` is sha256-pinned in `scripts/apps-money.test.cjs`; the pin is updated on purpose to this file.
- Test: `scripts/shop-store-test.cjs` runs the real `main/shop.js` over the real store (stand-in crypto and node): an order to an MxUSD shop that says Minima is stored as MxUSD, keeps the vendor's shop name, and 5 Minima leaves it `WRONG_TOKEN`, not paid. It fails on 0.19.164.

## [0.19.164] — miniMall vendor: a malformed, zero or negative buyer total is never paid

- **Ported from the released desktop (read only):** `desktop/minimacore-desktop-sec` 0.18.149 (`7d73315f6420a515f5b9e04c50983663f20f7830`). `main/shop-store.js` `decCmp` answered 0 ("equal") for a figure it could not read, so an incoming order whose total the buyer wrote as "abc" or "1e3" counted as paid by any coin; a total of 0 or below was accepted too.
- **Now:** `decParse` reads only a plain decimal (digits, at most one point with digits after it: no sign, exponent, hex or spaces); `decCmp` is an exact BigInt compare and answers `null` for anything else, never "equal"; `payableTotal` is a readable figure above zero. `recordPayment` gives `BAD_TOTAL` for any other total and never marks it paid, whatever arrives (what arrived is still recorded).
- **Inbox:** a `BAD_TOTAL` order has the red pill and says "The buyer's total is not an amount above zero, so this order is never marked paid. Received so far: …", in the four languages (review lists).
- **Pin:** `main/shop-store.js` is sha256-pinned in `scripts/apps-money.test.cjs`; the pin is updated on purpose to this file (each pin now names the version it belongs to).
- Test: `scripts/shop-store-test.cjs` (new, the real store with a throw-away folder, in `npm run test:apps`): exact compare past a double's precision, every unreadable figure `null` on either side, malformed / zero / negative totals `BAD_TOTAL` whatever is received, a readable total paid exactly and cumulatively in its token. Two of them fail on 0.19.163.

## [0.19.163] — Entropy: the node never sees the device bytes

- **Brought over from the phone (read only):** Entropy 0.1.16 (`apks/entropy-fix-2026-11-20`, `2c3f010cd87686dcbbe186cf81d17182f1f7376f`). 0.19.132 checked the node's `hash` answer, but `hash data:A||B` still sent the device bytes B to the node, so whoever answers the node learned every die and could veto throws it disliked by answering with an error.
- **Now:** the node is asked for `random` and nothing else (still 0x + 64 hex, never one it gave before). The face comes from SHA3-256(A ++ B), worked out in the main process (`main/entropy.js`, Node's own `sha3-256`; the window has none of its own), and B is never sent to the node. `mcd:entropy` has two kinds: `random` (the node) and `sha3` (this computer only); the `hash` kind is gone.
- **A node that fails:** any node error or bad or repeated random during a throw or the last-word pick discards it, says which ("The node did not answer; this roll was not used." / "The node's random value was malformed or repeated; this roll was not used."), marks the node not OK and checks it again before another roll may use it.
- **Audit:** each digital roll keeps A, B and the local hash H (`#n A:… B:… H:… n:… face:…`), so it can be checked with the same `runscript`.
- **Wording:** "node random + device randomness, SHA3 on this computer" in the source line and the summary; the mode note and the audit hint say what happens; in the four languages (new texts in the catalogs' review lists; the summary's source labels are now translated too).
- Tests (`scripts/entropy-test.cjs`): the node receives `random` and nothing else and never B; a refused ask sends nothing; the face and H equal SHA3-256 (FIPS 202 vectors and Node's digest of A ++ B); empty, malformed, repeated random and a node error each discard the roll; a failed pick marks the node not OK and checks it again. They fail on 0.19.162.

## [0.19.162] — Terminal: the completion's descriptions in the phone's own words

- **Brought over from the phone (read only):** Pandamonium's Terminal shows its command help translated (`integrated/terminalide` `HelpText.java`, with the `l10n_terminal_help_*` strings of `apks/base/localization`). Where the desktop's completion dropdown shows the same English (a command's one-line summary, a parameter's first line), it now shows the phone's Chinese, Russian or Ukrainian: 330 of the 373 descriptions the desktop can show. They are in a generated file, `renderer/i18n/terminal.js`, loaded after the three catalogs; a text the screens' own catalogs already hold is left out of it, so nothing of the screens is overridden.
- **Never translated:** command names, parameter names (`amount:`), values, addresses, and the command line itself. A description the phone does not have (43, for help lines the desktop's node words differently), or one whose translation drops a parameter name, an id or a number (4), stays English.
- The node's own `help` output (the JSON the node answers) is the node's and is shown as it comes, as before.
- Test: `scripts/i18n-test.cjs` reads `renderer/i18n/terminal.js`: the same texts in each language, every one a description the completion actually shows, every parameter name, id and number of the English kept, no text of the screens overridden, drawn through the catalog, and loaded before the language is chosen.

## [0.19.161] — Month names, Dexicon's chips and miniMall's order states in the app's language

- **Month names:** the short months the app writes (Mail's list dates "16 Jul" and day headers "16 Jul 2026", Dexicon's Orders stamps, the node's block time in the footer and the Node tab "Jul 16, 2026, 14:54:04 BST", and the Quit / Shutdown warning's "at 14:05 on 21 Nov") are catalog keys (`I18N.MONTHS`), and the date is put together by a key per language ("{day} {month}", "{month}{day}日"…). English is word for word as before.
- **Dexicon's single-word chips:** room ① swap chips (LIVE, DONE, REFUNDED, FAILED) and its own-order lines (BID, ASK), room ②'s FAILED, room ③'s fill chips (DONE, LOST, UNKNOWN, WAITING) and the Exchange engine's activity chips (DONE, CONFIRMED, SETTLING, IN BLOCK, CO-SIGNED, POSTED, REFUSED, DROPPED, LOST) are said in the app's language where drawn. The values stay English: the Activity tone is still read from the engine's own English chip. The fill route on an Activity row ("fast book" / "on-chain") is translated too.
- **miniMall order states:** the pill on an order, the Inbox filter chips and the Studio's status buttons show ENQUIRY, PENDING, PAID, CONFIRMED, SHIPPED, DELIVERED, UNDERPAID, WRONG TOKEN and ALL in the phone's own words; the state sent, stored and compared is the wire's English (`data-chip`, `data-advance` unchanged).
- 40 texts: 11 are the phone's words, 29 for native review. The text scan's allow-list names the engine's CONFIRMED chip, read for its tone before it is translated.
- Test: `scripts/i18n-test.cjs` runs the Quit warning's clock and the footer's block time in Chinese, Russian and Ukrainian (the month in that language, no English word left; English unchanged), checks each chip and state word has its translation, and that the screens draw them through the catalog with the English value under them.

## [0.19.160] — The tray apps' refusals and the rest of main.js in the app's language

- **Translated (through `renderer/mainsaid.js`; no main file changed):** ETH Wallet (Dexicon frozen, the export-key phrase, cancelled), miniMall (seed unreadable, message too large, vendor address and id, payout address, amount, token, order not found, already paid, "may already have been sent"), Minima Vestr (every create and collect refusal), the Web Wallet (seed, MegaMMR node, derivation, recipient, amount, key-uses confirmation, the two "send status UNKNOWN" warnings), the Faucet, Minima Explorer, KeyUses (the four audit failures), updates (a build that does not update itself, the https and sha256 refusals, size mismatch), and the rest of `main/main.js` (backup passwords and passphrases, files too large, not a .shop file, unknown language, the Terminal's "node not running"). `main/shop.js`, `main/vestr.js`, `main/webwallet.js` and `main/faucet.js` are pinned by sha256 and were not edited: their exact English is listed, and the test holds the list to their source. 69 new texts, for native review (three already existed).
- **Where it shows:** every result of the tray apps' actions (`AppsAct`'s `errOf` now takes Electron's "Error invoking remote method …" wrapper off and says the sentence in the app's language; the English stays in `raw`), the KeyUses error card, the update feed's error and the update buttons' status.
- **Decided by code, not by words:** the ETH key export's "Cancelled" (`/cancel/i` on the text) is now `MainSaid.has(r.raw, "EW_CANCELLED")`.
- The text scan's allow-list gains one case: Mail reads main/mail.js's raw English "may have been submitted", never translated.
- Test: `scripts/mainsaid-test.cjs` holds every listed sentence of these files to their source (each fixed piece present; a sentence with no value present whole), reads each back in the four languages, and runs the key export through `AppsAct` with an IPC rejection in each language (translated text, English `raw`, the code read from it). It fails on the 0.19.159 code.

## [0.19.159] — The coin guard's refusals and "held for" reasons in the app's language

- **Translated (through `renderer/mainsaid.js`; `main/reserve.js` unchanged):** every refusal the coin guard returns in the node's failure shape: a coin held for something else, a parameter given twice (in a `txninput`, a `txnpost`, a send, a consolidate, a chained command), a send whose address holds a held coin, "Not enough free funds" (with or without "no single address holds"), a consolidate refused while coins are held, and a send or consolidate inside a chain of commands. What a coin is held for ("an AtomiX swap", "a live Dexicon order", "a Dexicon take that is being voided"… and several of them joined) is said in the app's language on the Wallet's held lines, the token dialog's Held row, the coin list and the coin's details. Amounts, coin ids and token ids are as main gave them. 22 new texts, for native review.
- **Every toast** now goes through `MainSaid.say`: a sentence of the main process or the coin guard that reaches a toast (a refused send, split or consolidate) is said in the app's language; any other text is shown as it was.
- A value that appears twice in one sentence (the token of "Free: …") must be the same text both times when it is read back, so a longer sentence can never be read as a shorter one.
- Test: `scripts/mainsaid-test.cjs` runs the real `main/reserve.js` guard in front of the model node (`scripts/reserve-model.cjs`) through every refusal, reads each back in the four languages, and checks that each "held for" reason and their joined forms are said in each language; the reasons' English is held to `main/reserve.js`.

## [0.19.158] — Key mode and node start refusals in the app's language

- **Translated (through `renderer/mainsaid.js`, main unchanged):** every reason the node is not started for its key mode (`main/keymode.js`: no wallet yet, a data folder of the other kind, a block wallet on a node that cannot run it, a key-mode or app-owned flag in the extra arguments, a parameter value that starts with a dash, a `-conf` file that cannot be read or sets what the app sets, the finished start command's own check); the "wrong key mode" stop and its block over the window, for each of the four things the node can be found doing; the key-mode refusals of Settings and of the first run (`main/config.js`, `main/main.js`); a port in use (with the port's role; the program holding it is shown as the system names it), a node left over from an earlier launch, java that cannot start, an unexpected exit and the two boot hints; the three reasons the Parlons Node cannot be switched to; the three failures of a wallet replacement after it was asked (`main/node-manager.js`); the Terminal's refusal to restore a backup file. 48 new texts, for native review.
- **Where it shows:** the Node tab's Error row, the first-run progress line, the restore and resync results, the wrong-key-mode block, the Parlons switch and the node-kind switch in Settings (which used to show the raw "Error invoking remote method…" text; they now go through `ipcError` like the rest).
- `npm run test:i18n` also runs `scripts/mainsaid-test.cjs`.
- Test: `scripts/mainsaid-test.cjs` runs `main/keymode.js`'s start and start-command checks over every refusal (with real config files) and reads each back in the four languages; what node-manager, config and main.js say (java, ports and Electron cannot run here) is held to the source: each fixed piece of each listed sentence must be in the file that says it, in order, and the sentences built around a mode word are checked against the builder itself.

## [0.19.157] — What the main process says, in the app's language: Freeze, and restoring or replacing a wallet

- **How:** `renderer/mainsaid.js` lists every sentence these parts of the main process can say, each with a stable code and the English template main builds it from (the catalog key). The main process is not changed at all: it keeps saying them in English (its log, its tests and the only thing Electron carries from a handler that throws, an Error's message). The window reads a message back against the templates; an exact, whole match gives the code and the values, and the sentence is said in the app's language. A list of reasons inside a message (Freeze's bullets, what stands in the way of a wallet replacement) is read sentence by sentence. A message that matches nothing is shown as it came, in English, never blank or cut. Values (a transaction id, an address, an amount, a system error) are kept exactly as main gave them.
- **Translated now:** every Freeze refusal and every reason in its "still watching funds in flight" list; the backup restore refusals (key mode, host, file, password, node stopped); the wallet-replacement refusal with each line of what is still open (swaps, maker and OTC offers, pool transactions, this wallet's own pools, Dexicon orders and trades, P2P Chance), the "go on without this check" and "confirm" hints, and the two warnings shown before going on. Counts are plural keys (the English one / other forms are in `renderer/i18n/en.js`). 68 new texts, listed for native review.
- **Decided by code, not by words:** the restore screen's choice to offer "go on without the check" (`offerWithoutDexicon` in `renderer/app.js`) matched English sentences in the refusal. It now asks `MainSaid.has(raw, code)` of the raw English refusal (`ipcRaw`), so the translation on screen cannot change what it decides.
- `ipcError(e)` now gives the translated text; `ipcRaw(e)` the English as main said it.
- Test: `scripts/mainsaid-test.cjs` runs the real `main/freeze.js` and `main/restore.js` over many situations and reads back every message: each is listed in full (nested lines included), English comes back word for word, and Chinese, Russian and Ukrainian leave no English line and no placeholder; every Freeze and restore code is reached by a real message. `scripts/dexicon-pp.test.cjs` checks the screen's decision against main's own refusal.

## [0.19.156] — Pool calculator: a comma is refused, not read as a separator ("1,5" was 15)

- **Fixed (pre-existing, more likely now that Russian and Ukrainian users type a decimal comma):** the what-if pool calculator in room ② read its five number fields by deleting commas and spaces first, so "1,5" was taken as 15, "0,5" % as a 5 % fee and "1 000" as 1000. It now reads a field only when it is digits with at most one decimal point; anything else (a comma, a space, a sign, an exponent, letters) is refused and the calculator says so in a plain sentence instead of showing figures, as the send paths refuse such input. A comma is not accepted as a decimal separator. The prices the calculator writes into its own price field (from the slider and the chips) no longer carry the thousands commas it shows elsewhere, so they read back. The calculator builds no transaction; nothing sent or stored changed.
- Test: `scripts/dexicon-pp.test.cjs` reads accepted and refused entries through the calculator's reader, checks that every price it writes back reads as a number, and that every field goes through the reader. It fails on the 0.19.155 code.

## [0.19.155] — KeyUses: the Russian and Ukrainian "Common causes" say again that a one-time key is reused

- **Fixed (review of 0.19.140 to 0.19.144):** the KeyUses verdict's "Common causes" text in Russian and Ukrainian (the phone's wording) said only that independent signers, an old counter or an old wallet snapshot "can lead to a repeat", losing what is repeated: a one-time key, the very reason for the risk. Both now say "can lead to reuse of a one-time key" in full, with the rest of the sentence (this audit never changes counters or signs transactions) kept. Listed for native review. Chinese already said it.
- Test: `scripts/i18n-test.cjs` requires every text about a one-time key to say "one-time key" in each language (一次性 / одноразов…). It fails on the 0.19.154 catalogs.

## [0.19.154] — Tests: code that matches translated text is refused; the apostrophe rule; the shell, Send and Split run in every language

- **A scan of the code that decides by text** (`scripts/i18n-test.cjs`): every regex literal, and every string compared with `===`, `!==`, a `case`, `includes`, `startsWith`, `endsWith` or `indexOf`, in the shipped renderer and main files (vendored engines and catalogs excluded), is checked against the translated catalog keys. A phrase of 9 or more characters found inside a translated key, or a whole translated key, fails the test unless it is on a short allow-list with its reason (eight cases: engine and node texts that reach the code in English before anything is translated, and the miniMall delivery label, a wire value). The scan finds the 0.19.144 Trade-button match on a probe; it found the room ② failure-card defect fixed in 0.19.153.
- **Catalog values may not add an apostrophe (`'`)** that their English does not have, as for the other quote characters.
- **Run in each language:** the whole renderer is started with Chinese, Russian and Ukrainian saved (every script's top level runs in that language; the shell's decisions are the same as in English) (`scripts/shell-test.cjs`); Send and Split are lifted from the shipped source, not the English view, and run in the three languages: the same command strings, the same refusals, told in that language (`scripts/wallet-money-test.cjs`). Dexicon ③'s Trade buttons already run in the four languages since 0.19.145.
- No app code changed.

## [0.19.153] — Room ②: a failed pool action's card ends the same way in every language

- **Fixed (found by the new matcher scan of this phase):** a pool operation the engine marked failed showed its reason and then "Nothing was sent and no funds moved." The card made that ending by cutting the English words "Look at Activity: if no new line is there, …" off a longer sentence. In Chinese, Russian and Ukrainian there was nothing English to cut, so the card pointed at Activity (the screen it is on) instead of saying that nothing was sent. `failText()` now takes a flag and picks the right whole sentence; nothing is cut out of a translated text. The subjects "This" and "That" put in front of a failure, and "no reason was given", are translated too. English reads as before.
- Test: `scripts/dexicon-pp.test.cjs` builds a failed Activity card in all four languages and requires the "nothing was sent" sentence of that language. It fails on the 0.19.152 code.

## [0.19.152] — A broken language catalog can no longer stop the main process

- **Fixed (review of 0.19.140 to 0.19.144):** `main/lang.js` is required at the top of `main/main.js` and loaded the four catalogs with plain `require` calls: a catalog file that failed to load (a syntax slip in a later edit) would have stopped the main process before the window opened. Each catalog is now loaded on its own; one that fails is left out (that language is not offered, a saved or system choice of it falls back to English), the others load, and the reason is written once to stderr.
- Test: `scripts/i18n-test.cjs` loads `main/lang.js` in a child process with the Russian catalog made to throw: the process runs, Russian falls back to English, Chinese still works. It fails on the 0.19.151 code.

In Chinese, Russian and Ukrainian the match never hit, so the title appeared twice. `offline()` now returns the body sentence only ("Your Minima node isn't running, …"), and the card draws its title apart; nothing is cut out of a translated text. The English card reads exactly as before.
- Test: `scripts/dexicon-ax.test.cjs` builds the card's body in Chinese, Russian and Ukrainian and requires it without the title, and checks that no text is stripped by a match. It fails on the 0.19.150 code.

## [0.19.141] — The shell and the core screens in 简体中文, Русский and Українська

- **What is translated:** the toolbar, tabs, tray, overflow menu, footer and the stopped-node strip; first run (launcher, new wallet, the seed typed back, Restore & resync, the key-mode question); Wallet, the coins dialog and coin detail, Split, Consolidate, Token details; Send; Receive; History (list, filter, detail, import and export messages); Node (dashboard, contribute, the port-forwarding help); Startup Params with the whole parameter list; Backup & Recovery; Settings (updates, node kind, signing keys, coin guard, open at login); Apps (Hide, Freeze and their reasons); Terminal and Logs chrome; the Faucet screen; the shared dialogs. The chooser now lists the four languages.
- **Where the words come from:** a text the phone already has (Pandamonium for Android, `apks/base/localization`) uses the phone's translation exactly. A text only the desktop has was translated for it and is listed under `review` in `renderer/i18n/<code>.js` for a native speaker to check.
- **What stays as it is, in every language:** amounts (the node's own strings; no grouping, no rounding), identifiers, token and app names, command names, the words to type (RESTORE), the CSV and JSON exports (`histType`, `HIST_COLS` and the files are untouched), log lines, everything sent to the node.
- **How:** text is wrapped where it is drawn: `th("…")` in markup, `t("…")` for text set as text, `tr("…", {…})` where the sentence carries values the code had already escaped. English output is unchanged. Tables read while a script loads (`SUB_TITLE`, `BK_TITLE`, `NO_FREEZE`, `LOGS_CHIPS`, the history ownership tags) keep their English and are translated where shown (`N_`). The page's own fixed text is set from a table (`renderer/i18n/start.js`), the markup of `index.html` is as it was. Counts use plural forms ("1 coin", "4 coins"; one / few / many in Russian and Ukrainian). "31 minutes ago" comes from `Intl.RelativeTimeFormat`, as the phone's from ICU.
- **English changes, on purpose:** nine sentences lose their em dash (the port-mapping lines, the update download lines, the stored-secret placeholder); "this Mac" in the port-forwarding help reads "this computer" in one sentence that was joined from three pieces.
- **Fonts:** the bundled Manrope is Latin only, so Russian and Ukrainian are drawn in the system font; the stacks name the system Chinese fonts (PingFang SC, Microsoft YaHei, Noto Sans CJK SC). Nothing is downloaded.
- Tests: `scripts/i18n-test.cjs` (10): every translated key is in all three catalogs with the same placeholders, not empty, not left in English, without markup or an em dash; the page table matches the page; no helper is hidden by a local variable named `t`. The tests that pin a screen's English words in the source read it through `scripts/i18n-plain.cjs` (the source as it was before its text was wrapped).

## [0.19.140] — Languages: the helper, the chooser and where the choice is kept (English only so far)

- **"Language"** is pinned at the bottom of the app tray, is in the overflow menu after Shutdown, and is at the top right of the first-run launcher, as on the phone. It opens the phone's chooser ("Language · 语言 · Язык · Мова", each language named in itself, the current one marked). It lists the languages that have a catalog: in this version English alone; 简体中文, Русский and Українська arrive with the screens' text in the next versions.
- **The choice is kept by the main process** (config `language`; empty = the system's language when it is one of the four, else English). The window learns it before its first paint (`window.mcd.lang`, read once by the preload), and a pick is saved first (`mcd:langSet`, which refuses any code but the four) and applied after. It is applied without a restart: the page's fixed text, the shell, and the screen on show. A screen that holds typed text is not redrawn under the user; it changes the next time it opens.
- **`renderer/i18n.js`**: `t(key, params)`, `th` (escaped whole, for markup), `tr` (escaped text, the caller's own markup inserted as given), `N_` (a key kept in a table). The key is the English text, so a text no catalog holds shows its English, never a blank or a key name. Named placeholders; plural forms through `Intl.PluralRules`. An amount is inserted as the string given: no grouping, no rounding. Catalogs are `renderer/i18n/<code>.js`; the main process loads the same files (`main/lang.js`).
- Dev preview: `&lang=` in the address, `--lang=ru` on `build.js --shots` (files `shots-lang/ru-…`, not committed).
- Tests: `scripts/i18n-test.cjs` (7: the helper, the plural forms, the system-language rule, the chooser's three places and its save-first order, and the rule every later version is held to: each translated key present in zh, ru and uk with the same placeholders, not empty, not left in English, no markup). `scripts/shell-test.cjs` (16): the menu's order; a catalog script dropped from the page is caught by the folder rule. `npm run test:i18n`.
