## [0.19.138] — Settings: "Signing keys" per key family

- The card showed one "most-used key" for the whole wallet, as "N / cap sigs", choosing between a classic key and a block-based key by share of capacity, and warned at 80% whatever the key was. A classic key's counter is a count of signatures; a block-based key's is its key block position, which follows the chain's block number. The two are not the same kind of figure.
- Now one line per family the wallet has, with the two shapes `main/keyuses.js` uses (classic 64 / 3 / 262,144; block-based 128 / 4 / 268,435,456): "Classic keys: most-used key: N of 262,144 signatures", with the bar and the warning at 80%; "Block-based keys: key block position: position N of 268,435,456", with a sentence saying what it is, no bar and no warning. Neither is ranked against the other.
- A key row that is not exactly one of the two shapes with a whole counter shows "could not be read", as before for an unread counter. The footer's fallback (a node whose `keys` answer has no `maxuses` line) takes the classic count when there is one, never the larger of two unlike figures.
- Tests: `scripts/keyuses-test.cjs` (15).

## [0.19.137] — KeyUses: what was not checked is said in the verdict; lookups one at a time

- **The verdict says what it does not cover.** "No known key reuse was found in the checked keys." stood alone, with the gap (the keys the same seed gives in the other key mode, and minimaWallet's keys, which the phone checks and the desktop does not) only in the explainer at the bottom. The verdict card now opens with the checked scope and that gap, side by side, for every verdict. The separate scope line above the card is gone (said once).
- **Lookups go one at a time and stop at the first failure.** They were all sent at once, so when the service failed on one request the public keys and addresses of every other chunk had already left. Now a failed request ends the audit and nothing after it is sent.
- **A reply may weigh at most 512 KB**, the phone's limit (`AuditApi.MAX_BODY_BYTES`), instead of the shared 8 MB (`netfetch.fetchJson` `opts.maxBytes`: a caller may ask for a smaller limit, never a larger one). Worked out from the server's rows (`mds/keyuses` `backend/KeyUsesServer.java`): a row is under 450 bytes with every field at its longest, so a full reply for the 64 entries of one request is under 29 KB, and the server's own limit of 256 entries under 115 KB. The Web Wallet's one-key check uses the same limit.
- Tests: `scripts/keyuses-test.cjs` (15), `scripts/netfetch-test.cjs` (13, a stand-in server; no socket is opened).

## [0.19.136] — Minima Explorer: the latest-blocks list loses no block

- **A new block during a page load was lost for good.** The new blocks were read "by gap" from a remembered tip, the read was skipped while "Load older" (or the first page) was running, and the tip was moved on regardless, so those blocks were never asked for again. Now the tip the node gave (`EX.seen`) and the tip the list has reached (`EX.tip`, the one shown) are two values: the new blocks are walked from the tip down to the list's own top block, and a walker that ends brings the list up to date if it fell behind meanwhile.
- **A failed read left a hole.** The new blocks are held aside and put on the list only when every one of them was read. Otherwise nothing is added, the tip shown stays the list's, and the next node status tries again (the blocks already read are kept). A node that answers with another block than the one asked for counts as a failed read.
- **A list started again got an old walker's blocks too.** Every walker carries the list's number, raised each time the list starts again, and checks it after every await: a walker of an abandoned list writes nothing. A chain that went back (a resync) starts the list again.
- **The tip chip** reads "○ node stopped" in a neutral colour when the node is not running (it was a green dot with "no block yet").
- **"Load older blocks" is not offered at block 1**: the list ends with "block 1: the first block" (the button was there and did nothing).
- Tests: `scripts/explorer-test.cjs` (14), the walkers run against a stand-in node: a block arriving during Load older and during the first page, a failed read among the new blocks, a restart during a walk, a large gap, the chain going back; in each the list is strictly descending with no duplicate and no hole, and no two block reads overlap.

## [0.19.135] — Web Wallet: the public key leaves only on a click

- **Logging in to the Web Wallet no longer sends anything to the audit service.** Until now the wallet's public key went to `eurobuddha.com/keyaudit` by itself at every login, to pre-fill the "secret key uses" field. It is now sent only when the user clicks **Check on-chain** or **Confirm** in the send form, and the form says so before either button: only this wallet's public key is sent to eurobuddha.com, this reveals that key to that server, the secret key stays on this computer.
- **The safeguard is kept, and closed.** Confirm reads the chain's figure itself when it has not been read, records the typed figure or the chain's, whichever is higher, and **refuses** when the figure could not be read. An unread figure used to count as 0, the one value that can reuse a one-time signing key. Consequence: a wallet that has never been confirmed on this computer cannot be confirmed for sending while the audit service is unreachable. A wallet already confirmed sends as before; the main process still refuses any send before that confirmation (`main/webwallet.js`, unchanged, same sha256).
- **No redirect is followed** (`netfetch.fetchJson` with `noRedirect`), the request names one key, and the answer is a whole number or "not read" (`main/keyuses.js` `onchainUses`): an error row, another key's row, or a count that is not a whole number is "not read". The second copy of the host in `main/main.js` and its stale comment ("the ONLY user-initiated external call") are gone.
- Tests: `scripts/apps-money.test.cjs` (41).

## [0.19.134] — Entropy: screen capture protection is reported as it was set, and turned off when the page goes

- `mcd:secureScreen` answered "protected" on every platform but Linux, whatever the call did, and nothing turned the switch off again if the page reloaded or its renderer crashed while Entropy was open.
- Now `main/securescreen.js` answers what was set and on which platform, `{ on, platform }`: on only when `setContentProtection(true)` was called without an error on macOS or Windows; never on Linux, on a failed call, or without a window. A page load, a reload and a renderer crash turn it off.
- The screen no longer claims protection. The banner is always shown: "This window may still be captured by screen recording…" until the main process has answered, on Linux, and whenever nothing was set; on macOS and Windows "Asked the system to keep this window out of screen captures. That was not confirmed here…", because the result could not be tested where this was written.
- Tests: `scripts/entropy-test.cjs` (24), the module run against a stand-in window.

## [0.19.133] — Entropy: leaving the screen erases the phrase

- **The phrase no longer outlives the screen** (the phone's own note says closing the screen destroys it; 0.19.130 kept it, concealed). Leaving Entropy erases the phrase, the rolls, each roll's audit values, everything the page drew of them (the words, the roll string, the audit log, the summary, the roll ticker) and every typed last-word box. The routes: another screen (tab, tray row, Back, the overflow menu, a notification), the window hidden or closed, the node stopping, a wallet restore, Hide or Freeze of Entropy, and quitting.
- **Asked once before an in-app route erases a finished phrase:** "Leaving erases this phrase. Have you written it down?" (Erase and leave / Cancel). Hiding or closing the window, a node stop and a wallet restore erase without asking; rolls with no finished phrase are erased without a question.
- **Five minutes without a click or a key on the screen erase it too**, with a countdown on the screen through the last minute. A screen still open after such an erase is drawn again, empty, and says why.
- **While the phrase is concealed, so are the roll string and the audit log** (each of them is the phrase to whoever reads it). The audit button says "reveal the phrase first" while it is off.
- **The screen now says where a digital roll's values go.** Beside the mode choice: each roll's two random values and their hash pass through this app's main process and the node on 127.0.0.1, and Physical dice are recommended for a wallet that will hold real value. The security note lists what erases the phrase. The code comment that said nothing reaches the main process is corrected.
- **The phrase length is fixed once a roll exists** (a length chosen after seeing rolls would let the same rolls be tried at every length); "Start over (erases these rolls)" appears with the first roll.
- **Self-test:** besides the fixed vectors it now checks the SHA-256 of the whole word list (`2f5eed53a4727b4bf8880d8f3f199efc90e58503646d9ff8eff3a2ed3b24dbda`), so a changed word that no vector uses blocks generation; the list is frozen (`renderer/wordentry.js`, and Entropy's own copy).
- **The node coming back** (stopped to running) proves it again on an open Entropy screen, as its going away already did.
- Tests: `scripts/entropy-test.cjs` (23): the erase run on a stand-in page for every route, the question (once, Cancel keeps everything), the idle clock second by second, concealment of the roll string and the log, the length rule, the list digest.
- Not done: the preview captures (`scripts/dev-preview/shots/*-entropy*.png`) were not retaken. Not proven here: whether macOS reports a fully covered window as hidden (then covering the window erases too); it is in the dev-node checklist.

## [0.19.132] — Entropy: the node cannot decide a die face

- **Review finding, HIGH.** A digital die face is SHA3(node random ++ device random), and the hash was the node's own answer to `hash data:`, taken on trust (as the phone takes it). A node that answered with a value of its choosing chose the face, and with it the seed phrase, while the screen said "node SHA3 + device CSPRNG verified".
- **Now the hash is worked out in the main process first** (`main/entropy.js`, `crypto.createHash("sha3-256")` over the raw bytes) and the node's answer is passed on only when it is that same digest, in any letter case. Confirmed from source: Minima's `hash` command defaults to type `sha3`, which is BouncyCastle `SHA3Digest(256)` over the bytes the hex spells (core `commands/base/hash.java`, `utils/Crypto.java` `hashData`); it is the digest `main/keyuses.js` derives addresses with, pinned there by vectors checked against the live service.
- **The node's random value** is passed on only as 32 bytes of hex (the command's default size), the screen refuses one the node gave before, and the value hashed is exactly 64 bytes, so a short, empty or repeated node value never makes the device's 32 bytes the lesser part.
- **When the node's answer does not match**, the whole throw is discarded and the screen says "The node's answer did not match; this roll was not used. Nothing was recorded.", auto-roll stops, and the node is proved again before another roll may use it. There is no quiet fall back to the device alone: if the node fails that proof, the source line turns amber ("the node's answers did not match: device CSPRNG only (single source)") and the rolls after it are labelled as one source in the summary. The last-word picker picks nothing and says so.
- **"Verified" rests on the same check:** the source line turns green only after one whole node + device hash has been confirmed this way.
- Tests: `scripts/entropy-test.cjs` (16): a stand-in node behind the real `main/entropy.js` that answers with a hash making every die a 1, a constant, an empty string, the hash of other bytes, a cut value; a random value that is empty, short, long, not hex or repeated; one lie among five dice. They fail on 0.19.131.
- The phone's Entropy has the same finding (`RollEngine.java:84-108`); it is recorded in the plan and nothing was changed there.

## [0.19.131] — The renderer-load test runs the whole renderer

- Tests only; nothing in the app changes. `scripts/shell-test.cjs` no longer keeps a list of the names the other renderer scripts provide. It reads the `<script src>` tags of `renderer/index.html` and (1) refuses a `renderer/*.js` file that no tag loads, (2) runs every file's top level, in tag order, in one vm context whose only stand-ins are the browser's own objects and the preload bridge, (3) checks that every name a script uses anywhere, also inside its functions, is defined once all have loaded.
- Step 3 takes the names from TypeScript's checker over the same files with the browser's type library ("Cannot find name", and a property read off `window`). A name is settled when the vm context holds it, or when the file itself asks `typeof name` first. `typescript` 5.9.3 was already installed and locked as a dependency of the build tools; it is now named in `devDependencies` because a test requires it.
- Proven by changing the input: each of the 20 tags dropped in turn, two wrong orders, an export renamed that is used only inside functions (`EntropyCore`), a function renamed in one script and called in another (`entropyNode`), a name read off `window` (`micon`), and the 0.19.125 slip (`short`). Each fails.
- Limit: a script in a sub-folder (today only `dexicon/lib/activity.js`) is caught by its names, not by the folder rule.

## [0.19.130] — Entropy, as Pandamonium shows it

- **A new tray app, Entropy** (after Minima Vestr, where Pandamonium has it), with its Apps row. Pandamonium's native Entropy (`apks/base` `integrated/entropy`, `1c60c2593325f70cba5089e2ba430925d6ccca3c`; standalone `apks/entropy` 0.1.14), reproduced: DICE ROLL (phrase length 12 to 24, five dice thrown from node and device randomness or real dice tapped in, the bits filling, the phrase concealed until asked, the roll history and the per-roll audit log) and LAST WORD (type N-1 words with completion after four letters, every valid last word worked out and checked, pick one or have one picked). Its own arcade palette, dark as the original.
- **It makes wallet seeds, so the maths is the original's line for line** (`renderer/entropy-core.js`): dice to bits (6 counts as 0; 4 and 5 give one bit), the last whole 32-bit blocks, the SHA-256 checksum, and the die face = SHA3(node random ++ device random), first four bytes, mod 6, thrown again at or above 4294967292 so no face is favoured. The original's fixed vectors (Trezor's, and the dice vector produced by the genuine iancoleman code) run in the window before anything can be generated, and in the tests; the word list is the original's byte for byte (sha256 `2f5eed53a4727b4bf8880d8f3f199efc90e58503646d9ff8eff3a2ed3b24dbda`).
- **Randomness:** the device's 32 bytes per roll come from WebCrypto (`crypto.getRandomValues`), where the original uses SecureRandom; the node's from its `random` command, hashed by its `hash` command. `main/entropy.js` allows exactly those two commands, the second for plain hex only. With no node a roll is the device alone, by byte rejection, and the screen and the summary say "single source". `Math.random` is used nowhere, not even to flick the tumbling faces.
- **The phrase stays in the page's memory.** It is never stored, logged, sent to the main process or put on the clipboard (the original has no copy button either). It is concealed until Reveal, concealed again on a tab switch and on leaving the screen, and gone after Wipe or when the app quits. Typed seed words are kept from autocomplete and spell-check. The roll values pass through the main process to the node and back, as the original hands them to the node; nothing there logs or keeps them.
- **Screen capture:** while Entropy is open the window is kept out of screenshots and screen sharing (`setContentProtection`, the phone's FLAG_SECURE; macOS and Windows). Where the system cannot do that, the screen says so.
- **Not built:** the sounds (and their button), and demo mode (the phone's switch that allows capture and then refuses to roll).
- **Different from the phone, on purpose:** no em dashes in the text; "Click to reveal"; the security note says the phrase is destroyed by quitting the app or by Wipe (leaving the screen conceals it and keeps it, so a seed half written down is not lost by a click on another app).
- Tests: `scripts/entropy-test.cjs` (12). Captures: `scripts/dev-preview/shots/*-entropy*.png` (the phrase in them is made from the public pattern 1 2 3 4 5 6 and is not a wallet).

## [0.19.129] — Minima Explorer, as Pandamonium shows it

- **A new tray app, Minima Explorer** (after ETH Wallet, where Pandamonium has it), with its Apps row (Hide and Freeze). Pandamonium's native explorer (`apks/base` `integrated/blockexplorer`, `1c60c2593325f70cba5089e2ba430925d6ccca3c`; standalone `apks/blockexplorer` 0.1.10), reproduced: the header with the tip and the theme button, the search row, the three tiles, LATEST BLOCKS with "Load older blocks", and the pushed pages with Back: block detail, transaction (inputs, outputs, state variables, the confirmations line), address results, and "View raw JSON". Its own dark and light palettes, as the original (it does not follow the system; the choice is remembered).
- **Read only, and bounded.** The screen names a kind and one value; `main/explorer.js` builds one of the seven reads the original sends (`block`, `status`, `txpow action:info`, `txpow block:N`, `txpow txpowid:`, `txpow onchain:`, `txpow address:`) and refuses anything that is not a whole number, a 0x hex string or an Mx address, so typed text can never add a parameter or a second command. Blocks are read one at a time, 16 to a page, with the original's 60 ms pause. An address's records are cut to the first 200 and the total is said. No `coins`, no history scan.
- **Identifiers whole.** The phone cuts ids and addresses to 10…8 characters in the list, the links, the difficulty, coin addresses and the address page. Here every one is whole and one click from the clipboard (link rows carry a Copy button beside the link; a message that something was not found copies the value when clicked).
- **Different from the phone, on purpose:** a new block's small gap is added above the list in the right order (the original inserts each at the top, which reverses a gap of more than one); "Copy JSON" replaces long-press; a token is named MxUSD only by its id, and any other token's name is shown with its whole id; values the node did not give read "Not given" / "Not read yet"; the block time tile is cut to one decimal, not rounded; "yes, also a block" without the dash.
- Tests: `scripts/explorer-test.cjs` (10, the list walker and the pages run against a stand-in node). Captures: `scripts/dev-preview/shots/*-explorer*.png`.

## [0.19.128] — Settings and the footer never show a key-use figure that was not read

- **Settings, "Signing keys":** the most-used key was worked out with `parseInt(uses) || 0` and a capacity that fell back to 262144. A counter the node did not give read as 0, and a block-key wallet could be measured against the classic capacity; the highest use and the highest capacity could also come from two different keys. The card now shows the key nearest its own capacity, in the node's own whole numbers, or says "The node's key uses could not be read" and shows no figure. The percentage is cut, not rounded.
- **The footer** ("Key uses: N") used the same helper when the node's reply had no `maxuses` line: an unread counter there now leaves "Awaiting blocks…" instead of 0.
- Test: `scripts/keyuses-test.cjs` (12).

## [0.19.127] — KeyUses, as Pandamonium shows it

- **A new tray app, KeyUses** (after Minima Vestr, where Pandamonium has it), with its row on the Apps tab (Hide and Freeze). It is Pandamonium's native KeyUses 0.1.4 (`apks/base` `integrated/keyreuses`, `1c60c2593325f70cba5089e2ba430925d6ccca3c`), reproduced: the badge, the title, Run audit, the privacy line, the verdict card with its blocks, the table in sections (classic keys, block keys) with a status chip per key, the coverage note, the explainer. Its own dark palette (`Design.java`), in both system themes, as the original. The plan proposed hosting the MiniDapp page; Pandamonium shows the native screen, so that is what was copied.
- **What it does:** reads the node's keys (`keys action:list`, a read), derives each key's default address in the main process, asks `https://eurobuddha.com/keyaudit` and `/reuse` (the one host, pinned in `main/keyuses.js`; no redirect is followed), and joins the answers as the original's `KeyAudit.join` does. It offers nothing that signs or changes key uses, as the original offers nothing.
- **Both key modes.** A key is read only as exactly a classic key (64 / 3 / 262144) or a block key (128 / 4 / 268435456). Spend counts are compared with a classic counter only; a block key's leaf position is never compared with them. The classic "keyuses" estimate is shown for classic keys only.
- **It fails closed.** A key list that is not wholly readable (a missing or non-whole counter, an unknown shape, a duplicate, a malformed key) refuses the audit and sends nothing. The address derivation is self-tested against the original's golden vectors before every audit and cross-checked against the address the server derived. A key the server did not answer for shows "Not read" and "Unknown", never 0. The keys are read again after the server answered; if anything changed, the audit says "run it again".
- **Different from the phone, on purpose:** only the keys the node holds are checked. The phone also derives 64 classic and 32 block keys from the seed and can include minimaWallet's keys; both need the seed phrase in the app's own process and are not built (the screen says so). No "Include minimaWallet" box. Rows are in public-key order. "Not read" where the phone prints 0 for a key the server did not answer. No em dash in the estimate's label. No version number in the footer.
- **Settings:** the old "Address key uses" list with its "Deep reuse audit" is replaced by a button that opens KeyUses. The old card sent the node's public keys to the server whenever Settings was opened, counted an unreadable counter as 0, and compared block keys with spend counts. `mcd:keyReuse` is removed.
- **Tray order:** an app that an existing user's saved order has never seen now takes its default place (straight after the app before it by default) instead of the end of the list.
- Tests: `scripts/keyuses-test.cjs` (11), the tray order in `scripts/shell-test.cjs`. Captures: `scripts/dev-preview/shots/*-keyuses*.png`, `*-apps-5b.png`.

## [0.19.126] — 0.19.125 did not load: a removed helper was still named at the top of the renderer

- **The slip:** 0.19.125 removed `short()` and its call sites, but its name was still in the list of helpers handed to the Mail panel (`MailPanel.init({ …, short, … })`), which runs when `renderer/app.js` is loaded. That is a ReferenceError at the top level: the script stops there and no screen is wired. The whole check list was green, because no test ran the file as a program. It was found in the dev preview's console. 0.19.125 must not be built or run.
- **Fixed:** the name is out of the list (Mail never used it).
- **Pinned:** `scripts/shell-test.cjs` now runs the top level of `renderer/app.js` against stand-ins; a name nothing defines fails the test, and the test proves it by putting the 0.19.125 line back.

## [0.19.125] — The send result's transaction id is one click from the clipboard; the helper that once shortened identifiers is gone

- **The wallet send's result dialog** printed its transaction id through `short(txid, 20)`. That helper has returned the whole string since identifiers stopped being shortened, so the id was already whole, but it was plain text in a box that did not wrap a 66-character word. The id is now the app's usual copyable identifier (click it, or "Copy tx id" as before) and wraps inside the dialog.
- **`short()` is removed.** Its seven other call sites (History rows and the History detail: counterparty, coin addresses and ids, state values, the txpow id; the Send screen's "Sent" toast) passed a length it ignored. They now name the value itself. Nothing on those screens changes: each already showed the whole value.

## [0.19.124] — Small things from the review: no lone dashes, own-property lookups, a token's whole id beside an unknown name

- **A token that is not known by its id shows its whole id** beside its name, in Vestr (the contract cards, the token list and the "Create vesting contract?" question) and in the Web Wallet's token list. Characters that cannot be seen (control, format and zero-width characters) are taken out of the name first, so "MxUSD" followed by a zero-width character now reads as what it is: a name that imitates a known token is not shown at all, only the id. Before, such a name passed the check and stood alone.
- **No lone "—"** where a value is missing: ETH Wallet says "Not read yet" for a balance the Ethereum RPC did not answer; a miniMall order shows "Not given" for a missing delivery method and leaves the address row out when there is none (as the phone does); Vestr's block badge is empty until the block is known (as the phone).
- **Lookups by a name from outside** (an order's status, a token's symbol) use the table's own entries only (`Object.hasOwn`): a status called "constructor" gets the default colour instead of a function's text in the page.

## [0.19.123] — Web Wallet: the UNKNOWN lock survives Logout (review fix)

- **Found in review:** after a send whose status the node did not confirm, Send is blocked until the user says "I've checked". The block was a flag of the open session: Logout, or leaving the screen (which logs out), reset it, so logging in again offered Send at once and the same payment could be made twice.
- **Now** the lock belongs to the wallet address. Logout and leaving the screen leave it in place; logging in to that wallet again shows the warning and keeps Send off; another wallet is not affected; only "I've checked: let me send again" clears it. The send itself also refuses while the lock stands, and the lock is recorded for the wallet the send left even if the screen was logged out before the answer came.
- It is held in memory, as everything on this screen: restarting the app starts clean.

## [0.19.122] — Terminal: a chain of commands is never pinned, the note says what the app does, long output is copied whole (review fix)

- **Found in review:** the beacon-dust pin appends `fromaddress:` to the end of a MINIMA `send`. For a chain typed in the Terminal (`send …; status`) the end is the LAST command: the send went out unpinned and the last command was given a stray parameter. A command that contains `;` is now never pinned (`main/sendpin.js`, for every caller) and goes to the node exactly as typed.
- **The note agrees with the main process.** The Terminal said a send "may be given a fromaddress:" for `tokenid:0x0` and `tokenid:0x000` as well; the main process pins only a send with no token id or exactly `tokenid:0x00`. The note now uses the same rule, and is not shown for a chain. Tested as one list run through both.
- **Long output.** A reply of more than 20,000 characters is shown as its last 20,000, and the screen now says so in words ("This output is cut: the last 20000 of its N characters are shown. Copy output copies all of it."). **Copy output** copies the whole reply while it is on the screen (it copied only the tail).
- Not changed: whether the Terminal should bypass the pin altogether is the owner's call (plan, open questions).

## [0.19.121] — Logs: the RPC secret, bare seed words and text-direction controls never reach a log line (review fix)

- **Found in review:** the node prints its own start parameters, and with them `-rpcpassword` and its value. The app's own "starting node" line hid it; the node's line did not, so the RPC password could be read in the Logs view, in `node.log` and in a saved log file.
- **Now**, in the one function every log line passes (`node-manager.log()`, through `nodelog.scrub`): every occurrence of this node start's RPC secret becomes `•••`, wherever it stands in the line and before the 400-character cut of the on-screen ring, so no part of it is left on either side of the cut. Only a secret of 8 characters or more is looked for, so a short or empty value cannot blank the log.
- **Seed phrases:** besides `phrase:"…"`, a phrase given as bare words (`phrase:ONE TWO THREE host:…`, up to 24 words; the next parameter and the rest of the line stay) and as a reply's JSON (`"phrase":"…"`) are redacted.
- **Bidirectional controls** (U+202A to U+202E, U+2066 to U+2069, U+200E, U+200F) are stripped from every line: text a remote party wrote can no longer reorder what the Logs view shows beside it.
- **Save to file** applies the same to every line again in the main process, with the secret handed to it there.
- Not covered: a secret that the node splits across two physical lines (a line over 64K characters is cut) would be logged in two halves. The node's start line is far shorter.

## [0.19.120] — One gated way to the ETH private key (review fix)

- **Found in review (high):** ETH Wallet shows its key only after a typed phrase checked in the main process and, on a Mac with Touch ID, the system's prompt (0.19.108). Dexicon room ① MxUSD still had the old export: a warning, then `mcd:axExportKey`, which answered any call from the window with the key, and a "Copy key" button that carried the key in a `data-copy` attribute and used the ordinary clipboard.
- **Now:** `mcd:axExportKey` and the preload's `axExportKey` are removed. Room ①'s "Export key" (and the halt card's "Export the stale ETH key") opens ETH Wallet's dialog; the room's page never holds the key. One IPC handler can answer with a key, `mcd:ewExportKey`, and it is the gated one.
- **No system check is said.** Where the computer offers no Touch ID prompt, the key dialog says so: "No system check took place: this computer offered no Touch ID prompt, so the typed phrase was the only check." No second factor was invented for that case (an open question for the owner).
- **The clipboard forgets** (`main/clipsecret.js`): a copied key is cleared after 60 seconds and when the app quits, each time only if the clipboard still holds exactly that key.
- **The dialog closes itself** after 60 seconds and when the window loses focus or is hidden; the key is set as text in one node, which closing removes, and the dialog's own copy is dropped with it.
- Not changed, for the owner to decide: the Terminal still runs `vault` and `seedrandom`, and the application menu is Electron's default one (see the plan's open questions).

## [0.19.119] — Freeze, after its review: a wallet replacement's hold is never released by an unfreeze, and nine smaller faults

- **Found in review (high):** Freeze stops Dexicon's engines with the hold a wallet replacement uses. Unfreezing Dexicon while the node was replacing the wallet (Restore from seed or file) released the engines in the middle of it. Now a replacement's hold is its own: while one runs, unfreezing saves the choice and leaves the engines stopped, and the replacement's own settle starts them (on the same stores, or on new ones after the old were set aside); freezing Dexicon during a replacement is refused with the reason. The node manager says when a replacement holds the engines (`replacingWallet`, from before its hold until the command has ended, and again while stores are being set aside). Not as asked: the condition is that flag and not `resyncing`, because a plain resync (no wallet replaced) holds nothing and releases nothing, so an unfreeze during it would have left Dexicon stopped for good.
- **The hold comes first.** Freezing Dexicon now stops anything new from starting (AtomiX and PandaPools held with their engines up; the Exchange takes no new command), then reads what is in flight. Anything in flight, anything unreadable, or a hold that failed releases it and refuses. A swap started a moment before the click is seen or never starts.
- **Fail closed.** AtomiX with a store that has not started, and the Exchange with a store that has not started, each refuse the freeze ("has not started, so its … could not be read"), as the Quit warning already treats them.
- **Five seconds.** The read of what is in flight is given five seconds; later is "could not be read in time" and refuses. A read that never answers no longer blocks every later Freeze switch.
- **A stop that fails is undone.** If an engine cannot be stopped the app is not frozen, not saved as frozen, its hold is released, and the reason is shown. Opening a frozen app whose unfreeze fails shows the reason ("Still frozen") instead of a blank screen, and does not ask again in a loop.
- **miniMall's scan could start twice, and a freeze could be undone.** Its start waits for the node before it sets its timer, and since 0.19.117 it was asked at every node status. One start runs at a time (for every app), a freeze waits for a start still running, and a start that failed (a locked node) is not tried again until the node next starts or the app is unfrozen by hand, instead of at every status.
- **ETH Wallet** with Dexicon frozen from startup says "Dexicon is frozen. Unfreeze it on the Apps tab to use this wallet." instead of "Setting up wallet…".
- **A frozen engine's refusal says Freeze.** AtomiX, PandaPools and Dexicon answered "stopped while the wallet is being replaced" when the cause was Freeze. They now say that Dexicon is frozen and where to unfreeze it (desktop glue only; the vendored engines are untouched).
- **Apps tab:** why Minima Core and Parlons have no Freeze is text on their rows, tied to the switch for screen readers, no longer only a hover title.

## [0.19.118] — miniMall pays the token its currency names, and nothing else (review fix, the most serious)

- **Found in review (high):** a `.shop` file is written by the vendor and carries both a `currency` and a `tokenid`. The labels came from the currency and the payment from the token id, so a file saying "Minima" with the MxUSD token id (`0x7D39745FBD29049BE29850B55A18BF550E4D442F930F86266E34193D89042A90`) made the buyer pay MxUSD under a Minima label.
- **Now:** the app pays shops in Minima or MxUSD only (the currencies "Minima", and "USDT" / "mxUSDT" on the wire, which mean MxUSD). The token paid is always the one the currency names. A file whose `tokenid` is present and differs (compared as hex, any case) is refused with the reason: when the file is added, when the shop is opened, before the "Pay …?" question, in the main process before the order message is sent, and on Retry payment for an order saved earlier. One rule (`AppsAct.shop.token`), used by the screens and required by `main/shop.js`.
- **The "Pay …?" question** now also shows, each whole on its own line: the amount sent, the token (its name and its id) and the vendor's payment address.
- **Product pictures** from a `.shop` are shown and kept only when they are inline (`data:image/png|jpeg|gif|webp;base64,…`). Anything else is no picture: a `file://host/…` address would open a network connection on Windows. Pictures from the phone's Studio are bare base64 and were never shown here; they still are not (owed).
- **Prices, fees, quantities:** a price or a fee is a plain decimal (digits, at most one point; no sign, no exponent, no hex), a quantity a whole number above zero. Line totals and the total are worked out in whole units (BigInt), never through a floating-point number. The figure in the question is the string that is sent. A zero total is refused before the order message goes.
- **What vendors receive:** unchanged for prices of six decimals or fewer (six decimals, as before). For a price with more than six decimals the old screens rounded the total to six (7 x 0.0000004 was sent as 0.000003); the exact figure is now sent (0.0000028), as the phone app sends it. Nothing is rounded and nothing is cut. The vendor's side compares amounts as numbers, so both forms verify.
- `main/shop.js` is changed on purpose; its sha256 pin in `scripts/apps-money.test.cjs` is the new file's. New `scripts/shop-test.cjs` runs the main process's order path on a stand-in node.
- Not changed: the phone apps have the same mismatch (see the plan's owed items).

## [0.19.117] — The Apps tab's Freeze switch (owed since Phase 1)

- **Each app on the Apps tab has Hide and Freeze**, as Pandamonium's Internal list. Freeze stops the app's background work, puts its drawer row away and adds "· Deep frozen" to its line; nothing the app holds is touched, and unfreezing resumes it. The list is kept in the app's config (`frozenApps`) and honoured at startup: a frozen app's loop is never started.
- **What Freeze stops** (`main/freeze.js`): MinimaMail, the scan for new mail and the address renewals; miniMall, the scan for orders, payments and messages; Minima Vestr, its block poll; Chat, the message drain and the file transfers; Dexicon, the three trading engines (AtomiX, PandaPools, the Exchange) through the same hold a wallet replacement uses. ETH Wallet, Minima Faucet and Web Wallet do no work while closed, so Freeze only puts them away.
- **Dexicon is refused while money is in flight**, with the reasons listed: an unfinished AtomiX swap, a market on the book or OTC availability live, an open OTC deal, an unconfirmed pool transaction, an open pool of this wallet, an Exchange trade unanswered or unsettled, coins behind live Exchange orders; and equally when any of that cannot be read. "Dexicon is still watching funds in flight. It can be frozen once they settle." Unfreezing is never refused.
- **Never frozen:** Minima Core, and Parlons (its account runs inside the node). P2P Chance, removed in 0.18.68, has no row: its settle-only loop runs only while a bet is open.
- Dexicon and Chat are tabs: their rows have Freeze and no Hide, and opening the tab unfreezes them, as opening a frozen app does on the phone.
- While Dexicon is frozen, ETH Wallet (which signs through Dexicon's Ethereum engine) refuses a send and says to unfreeze Dexicon first. A wallet command that holds and releases the engines leaves a frozen Dexicon held.
- The window cannot write the list itself: `mcd:saveConfig` drops `frozenApps`; only `mcd:freezeSet` changes it, after the check.
- The owed items after Phase 5a are listed in the plan.

## [0.19.116] — Logs as Pandamonium's: Pause, the filter, the verbosity chips, Save to file (Phase 5a)

- **The screen is Pandamonium's Logs** (apks/base `view_logs.xml`, `LogsView`): three equal buttons **Pause** (Resume) | **Clear** | **Save to file** (the phone's Share), the **Filter** field (any case, anywhere in the line), the row of chips **Scripts, Mining, Blocks, Networking, Initial block download (IBD), Maxima**, and the log in monospace beneath, in the shell's colours, light and dark.
- **The chips drive the node's `logs` command**, as on the phone: a chip sends `logs <key>:true` or `logs <key>:false`, and every chip then shows what the node answered, not what was clicked; the first time the screen is shown with the node running it asks plain `logs` for the present state. They are disabled while the node is stopped.
- **The 30,000-character rule** is the phone's: the screen shows the last 30,000 characters of the lines that match the filter; a live line is appended, and once the text is over the limit it is cut to its last 30,000 characters first. Paused, live lines are not drawn; Resume draws the buffer again.
- **Save to file** asks where, then the main process writes what the screen shows (the filtered text). Log lines can carry text a remote party chose: they are written to the screen as text only, never as markup, and a Parlons message line keeps who sent it and never the message (redacted in the node manager before a line reaches the screen, and again by the main process on every line that goes into the file). The file is readable by the user only.
- Gone: the "Follow" tick (the phone always follows, and Pause holds the view still) and "Copy" (Save to file, or select the text).

## [0.19.115] — Terminal as Pandamonium's, with Run, the banner and coloured JSON (Phase 5a)

- **The screen is Pandamonium's Terminal** (apks/base `view_terminal.xml`, `TerminalView`): the MINIMA banner with "Welcome to Minima Core <node version>" and "You can run commands in this Terminal..", the output beneath it in monospace, and at the bottom the command field ("Enter command") with the orange **Run** button. It wears the shell's own colours in light and dark, as on the phone. Enter still runs; an empty Run does nothing; output is kept to 20,000 characters, as the phone keeps it.
- **Kept from the desktop:** completion with its parameter hints, the command history on the arrow keys, Copy output and Clear (small, at the top right), the one-line warning that commands run at once.
- **Coloured JSON**, as `mds/terminalIDE` colours it: keys, strings, numbers, true, null, and red for `false` and for strings that say error, invalid or fail; the command echo after an accent ">". terminalIDE is dark only, so the light theme has darker inks of the same hues. The output is built from text nodes: a reply is never written as HTML.
- **A typed `send` and the coin guard (checked, executed test):** in the default Observe mode the guard forwards a wallet `send` byte for byte, whatever another part of the app holds, and only logs what Enforce would have done. One thing can still be added to a typed send, and it is not the guard: since 0.16.x the app appends `fromaddress:<one of the wallet's addresses>` to a MINIMA send that names none, so that the node does not pick a coin it cannot sign. That is unchanged; the Terminal now says so under such a command, and the node's reply shows the parameters it received. A send that carries its own `fromaddress:`, and any token send, goes out as typed.
- `restore`, `restoresync` and `megammrsync file:` are still refused here (0.18.95).

## [0.19.114] — Minima Faucet as its one screen in Pandamonium (Phase 5a)

- **The screen is Pandamonium's Faucet** (apks/base `integrated/faucet`): "MINIMA FAUCET" in orange, "Get Free Minima", "Receive 0.0001 Minima to try out the blockchain.", the "Minima Address" field ("Paste or fetch an address…"), the outlined "Get My Address", the orange "Request Minima", the answer in green or red beneath, the help line and the footer, on `#0A0A0F`, centred. The address field is two lines so that a whole Mx address is in view.
- As on the phone, the address is not filled in until "Get My Address" is pressed (the old card filled it by itself), and the answer is said as "Faucet: …".
- The request is unchanged: `main/faucet.js`, one GET to its one host, after the address check (sha256 pinned).
- A sentence in the Web Wallet's checklist entry (0.19.113) is corrected.

## [0.19.113] — Web Wallet in the look of the official Web Wallet 2.5.2, with every desktop safeguard kept (Phase 5a)

- **The screens are the official Minima Web Wallet's** (reference `mds/webWallet`, its dark theme: page `#090A0C`, card `#282B2E`, fields `#17191C`, the orange `#FF8630` button, Manrope): the centred card "Minima Web Wallet", the login with a hidden secret-key field and Show / Hide, the tabs **Balance | Send | Receive**; Balance with "Tokens", the **Search tokens** pill (by name or token id, "No results found") and the round refresh; a token opens its **detail** (balance, the whole token id, decimals); Send with Token, "Enter amount", "Enter recipient address (Mx or 0x)" and **Secret key uses**; Receive with the QR on white, the address and Copy; **Logout** with the original's "this is your last chance" warning and the key uses to note down.
- **Every desktop safeguard is still there:** the "never type your secret key into a website" notice on the login, the MegaMMR gate (in the original's words: "A -megammr node is required to use this Wallet."), the one-time key-uses confirmation with the on-chain check and its suggested minimum, the two-click Send, and the UNKNOWN-status lock that blocks a second send until the user has checked.
- **Not reproduced, on purpose:** "Remember me" (the original keeps the seed in the browser's storage in plain text), "Generate secret key", the Settings page (theme, delete account: nothing is stored to delete), and showing the secret key back in the Logout dialog. The seed is held in memory only and cleared by Logout.
- **Secret key uses** is shown, not typed per send: `main/webwallet.js` keeps a durable counter per address that is reserved before each signature and never goes down, which is what protects a one-time key. "Raise it" reopens the confirmation; lowering is not possible. The original's field, which lets a user type a lower figure, is the unsafe part of it.
- A token is named from its id where the id is known (Minima, the one MxUSD); an unnamed token shows its whole id (the old list showed its first 12 characters).
- **Money unchanged:** the send is the old screen's call with the same arguments, the phrase passed exactly as held (`scripts/apps-money.test.cjs`); `main/webwallet.js` is untouched (sha256 pinned).

- **miniMall opens on Pandamonium's launcher page** (`MallActivity`: `#12171B`, cards `#192024` with a `#344144` outline, the lime `›`): "Shop, create storefronts and manage orders." and three cards, **miniMall**, **miniMall Studio**, **miniMall Inbox**. The three stay in one tray app, as kept by the owner; each card opens that app's own screens in its own design (`Design.java` of `apks/merchshop`, `merchstudio`, `merchinbox`: `#0A0A0F` / `#15151F` / `#1F1F2B`, accent `#F7931A`, the bar with ‹ and the title).
- **miniMall (the shop):** the shop list with "＋  Add a shop" and 🧾 My orders; the storefront ("Pays in …", product rows with the price in orange and the – / + stepper, the cart bar "N items · total" / "Cart empty" with Checkout); Checkout with "Delivery method" buttons ("label  +fee" / "label  · free"), "Delivery address / email", "Message to seller (optional)", Items / Shipping / Total and "Pay … MxUSD"; the original's question before paying ("Pay …? This sends your encrypted order to … Payment is final." Back / Pay), which the old screen did not ask; My orders; an order with its pill, the lines, Retry payment and "Messages with the vendor".
- **miniMall Inbox:** "✓ Ready to receive orders · shop key" with the key whole, the status chips ALL / PENDING / PAID / CONFIRMED / SHIPPED / DELIVERED, unread orders marked ●, the Vendor Card dialog (≡) with its QR; an order with Items, Total, Reference, Shop, Delivery, Buyer note, Buyer key, the 2 x 2 "Update status" grid and "Message the buyer".
- **miniMall Studio:** "✓ Shop key ready (from this node's seed)"; products ("Unnamed product", "no price" in red, "＋  Add product", "Continue · N in shop  →"); "Your product" (photo, Name, About, Price, Stock (max per order), Done, Remove product); "Your shop" ("e.g. Zebra Gallery", Pays in Minima | MxUSD, Shipping (label + fee)). The desktop keeps several shops, so Studio starts on their list (the phone builds one).
- **MxUSD on every label**; the value in an order and in a `.shop` file stays "USDT". The vendor card on "Your shop" is whole (it was cut to 18 characters).
- **Kept stricter than the phone:** a vendor can set only the next status, and none before the payment has arrived (the phone lets any of the four be pressed); the buyer's "payment timed out, check before paying again" warning and its blocked retry. **Added from the original:** an order with no delivery address is refused before anything is sent.
- **Money unchanged:** the order's lines, its six-decimal total, the delivery label, the retry, the status, the reply and the saved `.shop` are built in `renderer/apps-act.js` and compared with the old screens' (`scripts/apps-money.test.cjs`); `main/shop.js`, `main/shop-store.js` and `main/shopcrypto.js` are untouched (sha256 pinned), and so is `shop.json`: no change to what is stored.

## [0.19.109] — Minima Vestr as the original in Pandamonium (Phase 5a); a collect or create with no fee works again

- **The screens are the Android Vestr's** (apks/base `integrated/vestr`, `VestrDesign.java`: orange `#F7931A` on `#0A0A0F`, cards `#15151F` / `#1F1F2B`): its own header ("vestr" and the block), the bottom bar **Creator / Collector / About** in place of My Contracts / Create / Calculate; Creator with "Create a contract", "Calculate a contract" and "My contracts"; Collector with "Collect" and the same cards; About with the original's text. A contract's card is the original's (token letter, contract id, remaining amount, total vested, collected, collection, starts, ends) and opens the **contract screen**: "Available to collect" (green when there is something), the nine figures, Token id, Coin id, Withdrawal address and Contract id whole (click copies), the fee field and one Collect button. Create and Calculate are their own screens with the original's fields, order, defaults (grace Daily, no dates filled in) and refusals ("Pick a start date.", "Start must be in the future." …).
- **Found and fixed: with the fee left alone, nothing could be collected or created.** The old screens sent a fee of "0"; `main/vestr.js` has refused that since 0.16.97 ("Invalid burn amount": its amount check wants a figure above zero). The screens now send no fee as an empty value, which the main process reads as no burn. `main/vestr.js` is unchanged, byte for byte (pinned).
- **Amounts are the node's own figures.** The old cards formatted through a floating-point number with eight decimals (rounded). "Collected" is total minus remaining worked out on the decimal strings.
- **Kept from the desktop:** the review before a create (the phone sends at once): the withdrawal address whole on its own line, "This cannot be cancelled". The contract id is whole on the card (Pandamonium's copy cuts it to 6…4; the standalone app shows it whole).
- **Not in the original, so not here:** nicknames and search. They belong to the blue Vestr MiniDapp; the native app has neither (its card code says so).
- **What a collect sends** (checked by an executed test over the real `main/vestr.js`): `txncreate`, `txninput … scriptmmr:true`, the payout `txnoutput` then the change, ten `txnstate`, `txnpost`, `txndelete`. With no fee nothing in it signs (the covenant needs no signature), so nothing waits in the signing queue; with a fee the `txnpost … burn:` is a signed burn and is queued behind every other signer. Every command passes the coin guard unchanged, also while another part of the app holds coins.

## [0.19.108] — ETH Wallet as the original in Pandamonium (Phase 5a)

- **The screen is the Android ETH Wallet's** (apks/base `integrated/ethwallet`, `Design.java`: page `#0A0A0F`, cards `#15151F`, pills `#1F1F2B`, accent `#F7931A`): the title with the "Mainnet · real funds" pill, the version line, the address card with the whole address, one card per asset (the token's whole contract address above its balance), the two rows of three pills (Send, Receive, Refresh; Add token, Etherscan, Settings), full width at any window size. Its dialogs are the original's: Receive, Send (token line, recipient, Scan QR, amount, MAX), Confirm send (addresses whole with the first 8 and last 6 characters in orange, Low / Medium / High, the fee line), Sent, Add token, the token menu, RPC endpoint, and Settings with the original's six entries in its order, now including **View last transaction** and **Switch wallet source**.
- **Export private key is never one click.** The original asks "I understand", then the phone's lock screen. Here the user types `SHOW KEY`, and the main process checks that phrase again and, on a Mac with Touch ID, asks the system's prompt before the key leaves it (`ethwallet.exportKeyGated`; a bare call to the old handler no longer returns a key). The copied key is cleared from the clipboard after 60 seconds unless something else was copied.
- **Sending while a swap is in flight is said.** This address is the one Dexicon ① MxUSD swaps sign with. The send review reads the unfinished swaps (`mcd:ewSwaps`) and says in red how many there are and that they need this ETH for gas, or that it could not be checked. A send to the wallet's own address is refused before the fee is estimated.
- **Left out, with the reason on screen:** "Import key". On the phone it gives ETH Wallet a key of its own while swaps keep the node's key. The desktop has one Ethereum engine and one key; importing would either add a second key store and signer or replace the key swaps in flight need. "Switch wallet source" says so.
- **Differences kept:** balances are cut to six decimals as on the phone (never rounded); token logos are drawn as lettered discs (the phone fetches them from a CDN, the desktop makes no such request); a token's menu is on a ⋮ button and on right click (the phone's long press); "click" for "tap"; the original's long dashes are not in the wording.
- **Money unchanged:** every call that spends or shows the key is in `renderer/apps-act.js` and run against the calls of the screen it replaced (`scripts/apps-money.test.cjs`, 8 tests): review then send with the typed text as the amount, Max, add and remove token, the RPC endpoint.

- **Now** `scripts/build-flavor.cjs` writes the flavour's own name into those two sentences. The released build's wording and `package.json` are not changed.

## [0.19.106] — The two branches are one: Phase 4 messaging (0.19.1 to 0.19.9) merged with the side-by-side work (0.19.100 to 0.19.105); the two log guards dropped in 0.19.8 are back

- **Merge** of `pandamonium-sxs` (0.19.100 to 0.19.105: coin guard per the review, restore gate, token label, quit, node output, build flavours) into `pandamonium-desktop` (0.19.1 to 0.19.9: Mail as the phone, Chat files and voice notes, Parlons in the browser with the notifier, payment verification, the Mail publication notice). Both sides' behaviour is kept. The entries below are in two runs for that reason: 0.19.100 to 0.19.105, then 0.19.1 to 0.19.9.
- **Conflicts**: the versions (set to 0.19.106); `main/node-manager.js` `nodeLine` (one implementation: the side-by-side branch's shape, this branch's notifier branch in it, the side-by-side wording of the "already in use" hint); `scripts/keymode-test.cjs` (this branch's notifier test kept after the shared four); this file and the plan (both sides' entries, rows and checklist items).
- **Phase 4 follows the flavour**, checked by reading: `mail.json`, `chat.json`, their one-time copies (`mail.json.before-0.18.148`, `chat.json.before-0.19.2`), `chat-files/`, `chatimg/` and the Mail key's keychain entry are all under the running app's own data folder and keychain service; payment verification keeps nothing on disk; the Parlons panel link is accepted only on the loopback panel port of the running app (base port + 586: 17587 for Pandamonium Desktop). One name did not follow: the "downloaded by" mark on a file saved from Chat said minimaCore under either app; it now names the app that saved it.
- **Guard 1, back**: the Parlons flags (ready, error, version, cape, REFUSING) are read from the node's output only until the account is up for this node start. After that a forged line, even a whole one, changes none of them. The next start reads them again.
- **Guard 2, back, and wider**: what continues a cut line is never a fresh line. That is (a) the rest of a line the app itself cut at 64K, which the merged code judged as a new line (so text filled to the cut could begin exactly as a node line does: new in this version), and (b) the lines after a call-signal or greeting line that a peer's own line end cut short, up to the line with the node's ending (as 0.19.4; a run without its ending is over after 64 lines). A continuation is logged as "(continued) …", a message's words still left out, and read by nothing: no flag, no boot line, no notification. Per stream.
- **Still open**: a peer who cuts a line AND imitates the node's own ending makes a line the app cannot tell from the node's. Before the account is up no peer text passes through the account; a greeting can arrive before the first health poll. It ends when maxima and core strip control characters from what they print (plan, section 11).
- **A test changed**: the shared 0.19.104 flags test printed REFUSING after "account up"; the jar prints it before, and the test now does.
- **Tests**: `scripts/keymode-test.cjs` 42 (two new, executed on the real `nodeSaid` behind a stand-in java; both fail with the guards taken out); `scripts/flavor-test.cjs` 8 (one new: under each flavour the panel link is opened only on that app's own panel port, and `mail.json` / `chat.json` and their one-time copies are read and made only in that app's own folder). Nothing was launched.

## [0.19.105] — A second app from this tree, Pandamonium Desktop, that can run beside the released one; the node start never stops another program

- **Flavours** (`main/flavor.js`): the app's identity comes from one table, chosen when the app is BUILT (`"flavor"` in the packaged `package.json`, written by `scripts/build-flavor.cjs`). Nothing at run time switches it. The source names no flavour, so the ordinary build is the released app with every name as before, and for it nothing is set.
- **Pandamonium Desktop**: bundle id `com.eurobuddha.pandamonium.desktop`, data folder `~/Library/Application Support/PandamoniumDesktop`, keychain service `com.eurobuddha.pandamonium.desktop.rpc`, its own Safe Storage keychain item (named after the app), base port 17001 (RPC 17005, keets sidecar 17006, Parlons gateway 17585, panel 17587), no update feed at all, never registered to open at login, artifacts `PandamoniumDesktop-<version>-…`. The toolbar still reads "Minima Core" / "Pandamonium" as on the phone; the window title, the tray, About and the menu bar say "Pandamonium Desktop". The full table is in `docs/DEV_RUN.md`.
- **A flavour that borrows a released name does not load**: the released app's name, bundle id, folder, keychain service or port range in a flavour is refused at start, and the data folder is checked again after it is set.
- **Updates**: a flavour without updates, and a TEST profile, read no feed (not at start, not from Settings) and download nothing; the refusal is inside `main/updater.js`, under every entry point.
- **TEST profile** is now "this flavour in a folder of its own" (`main/testprofile.js` is removed). Review HIGH fixed: the released folder is `<application data>/minimaCore`, taken from the application-data folder and never from the app's current folder, and `--user-data-dir` is refused (`scripts/dev-run.sh` passed it, so the app's own overlap check always refused).
- **Start, all flavours** (review MEDIUM): before a start the app stopped whatever LISTENED on its port if the command line named a node jar and that port, so another app's node there would have been sent `quit`, SIGTERM and SIGKILL. Now the only process ever stopped is this app's own earlier node: the pid in its own `node.pid`, and only while that pid's command line names its own data folder. `quit` over RPC is sent only when that process is the one on the RPC port. Every port the node needs is then looked at; one held by anything else is named with what holds it, and the node is not started.
- **Node jars** for Pandamonium Desktop: `parlons-node` 0.2.153 and `minima` 1.1.2.31-euro5 (block and classic keys), pinned by sha256 under `flavors.pandamonium.jars`; the build refuses any other bytes. The released app's pin (`parlonsNode` 0.2.152) is untouched. With these jars a new wallet is block key uses by default, with the Classic switch at first run (0.18.88), and the key shapes are read after start (0.18.94). No `-mdsenable` is ever passed.
- **Tests**: `scripts/flavor-test.cjs` (7, executed against the modules loaded as each flavour). Nothing was launched; see `docs/PANDAMONIUM_DESKTOP_PLAN.md` section 9.

## [0.19.104] — HIGH: text from a remote party in the node's output could make the app kill a classic wallet's node

- **The fault**: any line of node output that CONTAINED "Using NEW Block number as key uses" was taken for the node's own block-mode boot line (0.18.94), and for a classic wallet that kills the node at once and refuses to start it again until the app is reopened. But remote text reaches the node's output: core logs a P2P peer's greeting version as it came, and the Parlons account logs fields of a contact's message. The Parlons status flags (ready, error, version, relay) were matched with "contains" in the same way.
- **The boot line** is now believed only when the WHOLE physical line is the one core prints, anchored at both ends: `Minima @ dd/MM/yyyy HH:mm:ss [<memory>] : Using NEW Block number as key uses keys!` (`MinimaLogger.log` prefix, `MinimaDB.loadAllDB` message). And only before this node start's first answered health poll: a running node no longer prints it, and from then on the `keys` shape check of 0.18.94 is the authority.
- **Parlons status lines** are matched by the exact prefix the jar prints them with, from the first character of the line: `[parlons-node] Parlons Node <version> `, `[parlons-node] account up: `, `[parlons-node] Maxima cape up on port `, `[parlons-node] account layer FAILED to start: `, `[parlons-node] Maxima cape FAILED to start: `, `[parlons-node] REFUSING to start: `.
- **Lines**: the node's output is split on line feed AND carriage return, an unfinished last line is kept for the next chunk (a line is never judged by half), control characters are stripped before a line is judged or logged, and each physical line is judged alone. Only the node's own streams are judged: the app's own log lines drive nothing.
- **What this does not close**: a remote text that carries a line end produces a second physical line, and that line can be written to equal one of the jar's own lines exactly. Inside this app that is limited to the boot window for the key-mode line; for the Parlons flags it would show a false status, not stop the node. The remote text must also be made safe where it is printed, in the maxima repo (`ParlonsControl`) and in core (`NIOMessage`). Owed there; not edited from here.
- **Tests**: 3 new, executed, in `scripts/keymode-test.cjs`; the older boot-line test now sends the real line. Not run on a node.

## [0.19.103] — Quit: a cancelled system shutdown no longer silences the question; live test scripts refuse a node that holds funds

- **Quit**: "the system asked us to quit" was a flag that stayed set for the rest of the session. A shutdown or logout the user cancelled left it set, and every later Quit went through with no question, whatever was in flight. It is now true for one minute after the system said so (`main/inflight.js` `recently`), then it is the user's quit again and is asked.
- **Live test-node scripts** (`scripts/test-node.cjs`, used by `atomix-unit.js`, the two AtomiX gates): before the first command the node is sent `balance`. A node with any non-zero balance is refused, unless `AX_TEST_NODE_ADDRESS` carries one of that node's own addresses and the node confirms it with `checkaddress`. A wrong container name can therefore never pass. An answer that cannot be read is a refusal, never "empty". `docs/DEV_RUN.md` says how.
- **Tests**: 1 new in `scripts/inflight-test.cjs`, 1 new in `scripts/hermetic-test.cjs` (stand-in transport and a stand-in docker; nothing real is reached). Not run against a node.

## [0.19.102] — Token label: the "(not MxUSD)" mark goes first; names capped and cleaned; right-to-left names isolated

- **Why**: the mark stood AFTER the token's own name, so a long name pushed it out of sight, and the closed pair picker (which shows only the start of its text) could read "MINIMA / MxUSD" for a token that merely calls itself MxUSD.
- **Now**: every token but the real MxUSD (by token id) reads `(not MxUSD) <name>`, and a pair reads `MINIMA / (not MxUSD) <name>  ·  token <whole id>`. In records the glue writes `(not MxUSD, token <whole id>) <name>`.
- **Names** are capped at 32 code points (the token id beside them is always whole). Removed from a name: control and format characters (zero-width, direction marks and overrides), every code point that draws nothing (the Hangul fillers U+3164, U+115F, U+1160, U+FFA0, variation selectors, the grapheme joiner, the soft hyphen) and the blank Braille cell U+2800. Runs of space characters become one space. A name of only such characters is "unnamed token".
- **Right-to-left**: the name is wrapped in a direction isolate (U+2068 … U+2069), so Hebrew or Arabic letters in a name cannot reorder the mark before it or the token id after it.
- **Records** labelled the old way (the mark last, written by this branch's earlier versions) are recognised and not labelled twice.
- **Tests**: 1 new in `scripts/dexicon-pp.test.cjs` (25 invisible code points, the cap, the isolate, every cut of the picker's text); the older label assertions moved to the new order. Not looked at in a running app.

## [0.19.101] — Restore gate: unfinished AtomiX swaps are read from the open store; a store set aside disowns an init in flight

- **The gap**: when AtomiX's store was open but its service had not become ready (it waits for the node's identity reads, which a locked or slow node never answers), a wallet replacement could be confirmed past "cannot be checked" after two minutes, with a swap in flight in that very store.
- **Now**: the unfinished swaps are read straight from the store (`main/atomix.js` `storeSwaps`: not finished, or failed and still inside its own timelock) and any of them refuses the replacement, each hash whole. It is a refusal, not a check to skip. With no swap in the store the old wording and the old way on stand (a maker order or an OTC deal still cannot be read without the service).
- **Set aside**: `setAsideStore` in AtomiX and PandaPools disowns an init that is still opening the file (generation / epoch bumped first), so it cannot finish beside the init that starts on the empty store. Confirmed by a test that fails without the bump.
- **The dialog** names the store's whole path and says how to put it back: quit the app, then rename the dated file back. The note after the move repeats it with the real name.
- **Not changed, seen while here**: Dexicon's takes read from its store while it is not ready (`activeWork().takes`) are still not used by the restore gate.
- **Tests**: 2 new in `scripts/dexicon-ax.test.cjs`. Not run on a node.

## [0.19.100] — Coin reservation: sends and consolidate are observed, not enforced; the review's findings

- **Why**: the security review of 0.18.143 to 0.18.147 said "do not ship fully enabled". An enforced `send` can only be pinned to an address, so a wallet with a live order could be refused a send it has the coins for.
- **What is enforced now**: the `txninput` refusal and the engines' own coin reads (held coins left out). **What is only observed**: `send` pinning and refusal, and `consolidate` refusal. Observed means the command goes out byte for byte and the Logs say what would have been pinned or refused, and why.
- **Settings, "Coin guard"**: a master switch (off = the whole guard passes every command untouched), "Sends and Consolidate" with three states (Off / Observe / Enforce; Observe by default), and "Release all holds" with a confirm. Releasing cannot spend a coin twice: the node refuses that itself.
- **Wallet id**: the reservation file is named after the wallet's first PUBLIC key (modifier 0x00). `seedrandom modifier:coin-reservation-v1` is no longer sent. It works on a locked node. An existing file is renamed once (only when the folder holds exactly one).
- **M1**: when the node does not say what a coin is, every engine's input is let through and logged; only the wallet's own command is refused.
- **M2**: a watched command waits at most 3 s for the reservation file to open, then goes on.
- **M3**: every pin is given a coin age of 3 blocks (Wallet Send, Web Wallet, Vestr, miniMall, the reservation's own pin; Mail and AtomiX already had theirs).
- **M4**: a coin is released as spent only when the node did not show it in two sweeps running, with a chain tip; a coin shown again is held again.
- **M5**: a hold's token is read when the hold is set; `consolidate` is held up only by coins of its own token.
- **L1**: the verb is read in any letter case (`SEND` is a send) in `main/sendpin.js`, `main/main.js` and the reservation; a parameter given twice is refused; a quoted coin id is read as the node reads it. The shapes still not guarded are listed in `docs/COIN_RESERVATION.md`.
- **L2**: a retried `txnpost` the node rejects no longer releases the coins the first post put in the mempool.
- **L3**: AtomiX and PandaPools release their unfinished builds when they are stopped.
- **L4**: the file is mode 0600; a record with no post time, or a time in the future, still lapses.
- **L5, not done on purpose**: the node ends a command at every `;`, inside a JSON value too. Reading it any other way here would let an input written after such a `;` reach the node unchecked. The guard splits exactly where the node does, and a test pins it.
- **H1**: the proper fix (build the send from explicit free coins when the address pin fails) is written as a design in `docs/COIN_RESERVATION.md`. Not built.
- **Tests**: 13 new in `scripts/reserve-test.cjs`; the model node answers `keys`. Not run on a node.
## [0.19.9] — Mail: a one-time notice before your address is first published; a wallet reset can no longer overwrite the old inbox; a recipient found only by six words is confirmed by its whole key

- **Found in review (medium, privacy)**: Mail publishes the owner's six-word address by default (released in 0.18.67, as on the phone) and said nothing. The post is a coin on chain carrying the six words, the Mail key and a signature, paid from one of the wallet's addresses: public, permanent, and it ties the Mail address to that wallet address.
- **Now**: publishing stays the default, but this install's own card is not posted until the owner has answered a one-time notice, shown when Mail opens: what is published, that it is public and permanent and cannot be removed, that it links the Mail address to a wallet address, with "Publish my address" (the first button) and "Don't publish" (which turns it off; Escape is the same). It can be changed later under Your key. An install whose card is already on chain is not asked: there is nothing left to choose. The phone has no such notice, so there was no wording to take: this one is new. Until the answer, Your key shows the address as not published.
- **Low, data loss**: on a wallet reset, if the old mail could not be moved aside, the store was emptied instead, and its next write replaced the old wallet's `mail.json`, the only copy of that inbox. The fallback is removed: the failure is reported in the reset's "NOT reset" list and the file stays as it is.
- **Low, six words are 66 bits**: before the first send (mail, photo or funds) to a key this install knows only from a beacon, or only from mail that key itself sent, a dialog shows the whole Mail key, when its beacon was first seen and last renewed (block and rough age), and asks. Saved contacts, keys written to before, contact links and whole keys are not asked about. The main process refuses the send and the payment until the confirmation (`needConfirmed`), so it does not rest on the screen alone.
- **Tests** (`npm run test:mail` 43): nothing is posted before the answer although the renewal loop runs with a coin; each answer; an already-published install; a beacon-only recipient refused for mail and for payment until confirmed, then "sent" and not asked again; a key known only from its own mail; the dialogs' words with the whole key; a reset whose move fails leaves every message in `mail.json` and in the store.

## [0.19.8] — Log isolation brought to the same code as the side-by-side branch (its 0.19.104), so the two merge cleanly

- **Why**: 0.19.4 here and 0.19.104 on the `pandamonium-sxs` branch fixed the same finding in two shapes. This version replaces 0.19.4's shape in `main/node-manager.js` with that branch's (commit ca9e922), ported by hand because this branch also has the Parlons notifier in the same function.
- **The boot line** is now `/^Minima @ \d{2}\/\d{2}\/\d{4} \d{2}:\d{2}:\d{2} \[[0-9.,]{1,8} (?:bytes|[ KMGTPE]B)\] : Using NEW Block number as key uses keys!$/` (`BLOCK_BOOT_LINE`, exported by the node manager; 0.19.4 allowed any text inside the memory brackets and kept it in `keymode.js`). Believed only before this start's first answered health poll (`polled`).
- **The node's output** goes through `nodeSaid` (split on `\r` and `\n`, an unfinished line carried to the next chunk, per stream) and `nodeLine` (control characters removed, then the checks, then the log). `log()` no longer decides anything: it also takes the app's own lines. The Parlons flags by `startsWith` on the jar's exact prefixes (`PARLONS`).
- **Only on this branch**: `nodeLine` hands a Parlons message or call line to the notifier's anchored parser and to nothing else, and logs it without the message's words.
- **Removed, and what that costs**: 0.19.4's two extra guards are gone, so that the code is the same on both branches: (1) the "(continued)" run after a call-signal or greeting line cut by a peer's line end, which a peer could step around by imitating the node's line ending; (2) reading the Parlons flags only until the account is up. So a peer whose text carries a line end can again make a WHOLE line of its own: it can show a false Parlons account error or raise a notification. It still cannot stop the node once the node has answered its first health poll. This is the residual the other branch states too; it ends when maxima and core strip control characters from what they print (plan, section 11). If guard (2) is wanted back it is four words in `watchParlonsLine`, on both branches.
- **Tests**: the other branch's four executed tests, taken as they are (`scripts/keymode-test.cjs`, named 0.19.104), and one for this branch: message and call lines carrying the block-mode line, a forged account error and "Address already in use" set nothing; a message under a 324-digit key cut across two chunks is notified once and logged without its text. `keymode-test.cjs` 40.

## [0.19.7] — Mail and Chat: a payment message is the sender's claim until this node has found the payment; the currency is named from the token id only

- **Found in review (medium)**: a payment bubble showed what the SENDER wrote. Anyone can send a `payment` message with no payment behind it, or pay one unit of a worthless token they named "MxUSD"; the bubble then read "<name> sent you 5000 MxUSD" (Mail) or "Payment received 5000 MxUSD" (Chat).
- **The currency is named from the token id only** (`renderer/paylabel.js`, shared by Mail, Chat and the notifications): `0x00` is MINIMA, `0x7D39745FBD29049BE29850B55A18BF550E4D442F930F86266E34193D89042A90` is MxUSD, and anything else is "another token" with its whole token id under the amount. The sender's name for a token is never shown.
- **A received payment is "Not verified" until this node has found it** (`main/payverify.js`): the transaction id must be on chain (`txpow onchain:`), and its outputs of that token id to addresses of this wallet (`checkaddress`, `relevant`) must add up to exactly the amount claimed. Until then the bubble says "<name> says they sent you" / "Payment notice" and "Not verified: this node has not found this payment. Check your balance before relying on it."; after, "<name> sent you" / "Payment received" and "Received: this node found the payment to this wallet on chain". List rows and notifications say "Payment notice, not verified: ...". The same in Mail and Chat.
- **When it looks**: once when the message is first shown, then on each new block until found, at most 12 times per payment, one read at a time, at most 200 payments followed; forgotten at the next node start. Reads only (`rpcRead`), never the signing queue.
- **What it cannot find, said plainly**: a sender whose node mines asynchronously reports the id its transaction had before mining, which is not the id on chain. Such a payment stays "Not verified" although it arrived: the bubble then understates, it never overstates. And a Chat payment received BEFORE this version was stored without its token id (only the sender's name for it), so it now reads "another token, No token id was given"; from this version the id is stored.
- **Tests** (`scripts/payverify-test.cjs`, 6, in `npm run test:mail`): the label by id; fourteen ways a claim is not "received" (not on chain, another address, less, a 10^-43 difference, another token named MxUSD, a failed read, a malformed claim that asks the node nothing, 500 outputs); asked once per show, again per block, given up after 12; and the real `bubble` of `renderer/mail.js` and payment bubble of `renderer/chat.js` run on a fake, an empty and a real payment.

## [0.19.6] — Parlons: a real sender key is read (notifications fire, message text stays out of the log); voice notes are allowed to play

- **Found in review (medium)**: the notifier read a sender key of at most 200 hex digits. A Maxima identity key is an RSA-1024 public key and prints as 324. So for a real message the line was neither parsed (no "Parlons: new message") nor redacted: the message's first 80 characters went to the Logs view and to `node.log`, which 0.19.3 said they did not. 0.19.3's fixtures used a 64-digit key. Not seen on a live node (none may be run here): the fixture is now a key of the real size and shape, and a real line is on the checklist.
- **Fixed**: a key of up to 1024 hex digits is read. And the redaction no longer depends on the key at all: any line that starts as a message line is logged without what follows its first `": "`, and a sender that is not a plain key is not shown either.
- **Voice notes can play** (low): the page's content security policy had no `media-src`, so `default-src 'none'` applied to the `data:` URL the Chat player is given. `media-src data:` is added, and nothing wider (the recorder never plays a `blob:`). Playing a note in the built app is on the checklist: this was not run here.
- **Tests**: `parlons-notify-test.cjs` 9 (the 324-digit fixture, six shapes of message line none of which keeps its text), the 0.19.4 node-manager test now sends its real message under the 324-digit key and finds it notified and redacted; `chatfiles-test.cjs` 34 (the URL `chat.fetchMedia` returns is checked against the policy parsed from `renderer/index.html`).

## [0.19.5] — Chat files: the first download of a received file works; a temp folder with a space in its path; saved files carry the system's downloaded mark

- **Found in review (high, functional)**: every first download of a received file failed with "Transfer not found". Run on the transfer sheet (`main/chat.js` `fileRun`) looked the transfer up by its id to see whether it was one of ours, and that lookup throws for an offer that has never been downloaded, which is every received file the first time. The lookup is now guarded: no transfer on disk means a download from the offer. 0.19.2's tests called the engine's `download` directly and never went through `fileRun`.
- **A temp folder with a space in its path** (a Windows user name such as "John Smith") refused every file send and every voice note, because the node's command reader splits on spaces.
