The node is now handed the same folder under its short (8.3) name (`chatfiles.spaceFreeDir`), which has no space and is the same private folder. Quoting the path was not used: core's reader takes a different path for Windows file names and the Parlons node has its own front. Where a volume has no short names the refusal stays, with the whole path. Not run on Windows here: on the checklist.
- **A file saved from a chat carries the system's "downloaded" mark**: `com.apple.quarantine` on macOS (so the system asks before the first open) and a `Zone.Identifier` stream on Windows. Best effort: a file system that cannot hold the mark still gets the file.
- **Tests** (`npm run test:chat` 43): `fileRun` itself for a never-seen offer, a paused one resumed, and one of our own (no download); a publish from a folder with a space, the node reading the block at the space-free name; the mark's command and stream, and that a failure of either is swallowed.

## [0.19.4] — Security review of Phase 4: a peer's text in the node's log can no longer stop a classic wallet's node or forge the Parlons status

- **Found in review (high)**: 0.19.3 kept Parlons message and call lines away from the state checks, but the block-mode check still fired on ANY line that held the words "Using NEW Block number as key uses", and the Parlons flags on any `[parlons-node]` line that held "account up:", "account layer FAILED" and so on. A peer's text reaches the node's output in two more ways: core prints a connecting peer's greeting version as it came (`NIOMessage`, "Greeting with Incompatible Version!"), and the Parlons node prints a call signal's state as it came, so a state holding a line end made a second line of the peer's choosing. Either could make the app kill the node of a classic-key wallet and refuse to start it until reopened, or show a false account error.
- **The block-mode boot line is believed only when the whole line is the node's own** (`keymode.BLOCK_BOOT_LINE`: MinimaLogger's `Minima @ dd/MM/yyyy HH:mm:ss [memory] : ` then MinimaDB's exact words, nothing before or after), **and only before this start's first answered health poll**. After that the log is never read for the key mode: the keys the node really holds (`verifyKeyMode`) stay the authority.
- **The Parlons flags** (version, cape, ready, error, refused start) are read only from a line that begins exactly as the jar prints its own (`[parlons-node] account up: `, `[parlons-node] account layer FAILED to start: `, ...), never from a phrase inside a line, and only until the account is up: all of them are boot lines, and nothing a peer sends through the account exists before it.
- **Lines**: the node's output is split on `\r` as well as `\n`, each physical line is judged alone, and control characters are removed before anything is matched. A call-signal or greeting line that does not end with the node's own ending was cut by a line end in the peer's text: every line after it, up to the one carrying that ending, is logged as "(continued)" and read by nothing (no flag, no notification).
- **What this does not close, said plainly**: a peer who puts a line end in its text AND imitates the node's own ending before it can still produce a whole line the app cannot tell from the node's. After this change such a line can raise a Parlons notification (which that contact could raise by really writing or calling) and, only in the seconds between a node start and its first answer, the block-mode line or a Parlons boot flag. Closing it needs the two nodes to strip control characters from what they print: written in the plan as owed (maxima `ParlonsCore.log`, core's greeting log). Neither repo was touched.
- **Tests**: the source-text test "a remote sender cannot drive the node manager" is replaced by three executed ones in `scripts/keymode-test.cjs` (the real `log()` behind a stand-in java process): a greeting line and eight other lines holding the words do not stop the node, a forged line inside a cut line does not, the genuine line does; the genuine line after "running" is ignored and believed again on the next start; forged `account layer FAILED` / message / call lines set no flag and raise no notification. `keymode-test.cjs` 39, `test:parlons` 12.

## [0.19.3] — Parlons opens in the system browser; the app keeps a notifier for its messages and calls; a remote sender can no longer drive the app through the node's log

- **Parlons opens in your browser** (owner decision, 19 Nov 2026). The tray's Parlons row opens the node's own panel in the system browser with a fresh one-time link, and only ever the loopback panel (`main/parlons.js`). The Parlons screen in the app says so, has "Open Parlons in the browser", shows your Parlons address whole, and says what is wrong when the account is starting or failed.
- **Removed**: the embedded `<webview>`, its `persist:parlons` session, `main/parlons-calls.js` (its media permission and its `will-attach-webview`), `webviewTag`, and the IPC that handed the one-time link to the screen (`mcd:parlonsPanelUrl`): the link is now read and passed to the browser in the main process only. Any attempt to attach a web view is refused. Calls, with camera and microphone, belong to the browser tab.
- **The app still tells you** (`main/parlons-notify.js`). How it learns: from the two lines the Parlons node itself prints (the same in parlons-node 0.2.152 and 0.2.153), nothing else. `[parlons-node] message from <key>: <text>` gives a desktop notification "Parlons: new message" (at most one every five seconds; a burst is counted, not repeated). `[parlons-node] node: call offer from <name> → pushing to devices` gives "Incoming Parlons call", the dock bounce or taskbar flash, for 45 seconds or until clicked. Clicking either opens the panel.
- **What it cannot do yet, said plainly**: the node prints the call line only while some device is live (a Parlons tab open in a browser, or a paired phone seen in the last three minutes). With none, the node declines the call at once and prints nothing, so the app cannot know of it. And a tab opened by clicking the notification does not receive a call that is already ringing: it is answered in the tab that was open. No unread badge on the Parlons row: no truthful count exists outside the panel's session. The small addition to the Parlons node that would close this is written in `docs/PANDAMONIUM_DESKTOP_PLAN.md` for approval; the maxima repo was not touched.
- **Found on the way, and fixed (security)**: the app read the node's log by looking for phrases anywhere in a line, and the node prints up to 80 characters of any sender's message into a line. So a message containing "Using NEW Block number as key uses" made the app stop the node of a classic-key wallet and refuse to start it until the app was reopened; one containing "account layer FAILED" showed the sender's text as the account's error and hid the panel. Message and call lines are now read only by the notifier's anchored parser and by none of those checks.
- **Privacy**: the text of a Parlons message is no longer written to the Logs view or to `node.log` (the line keeps the sender's key and says "(text not logged)").
- **Also**: the node's output is read as whole lines (a line cut across two chunks was read as two); the macOS camera and microphone descriptions no longer speak of Parlons calls.
- **Tests**: `npm run test:parlons` 12 (`parlons-view-test.cjs` rewritten, `parlons-notify-test.cjs` new; `parlons-calls-test.cjs` removed with its file).

## [0.19.2] — Chat: files both ways in the phone's protocol, voice notes recorded, the failure dialog, Delete chat apart from Delete contact, saved contacts

- **Why**: the desktop Chat (Keets chat; not Parlons, which stays its own app) could play a voice note and show a photo or a payment, but could not record a note, send or receive a file, or tell "delete the chat" from "delete the contact", and a failed send was a passing toast. This brings what Pandamonium's chat has (`apks/base` `chat/`).
- **Files, both ways, in the phone's protocol** (`main/chatfiles.js`, a port of `ChatFiles`, `ChatFileDelivery` and maxima-core's `ChatFile`, `FileCrypto`, `KeetsFiles`, `FileDelivery`): Attach, "File (up to 100 MB)" encrypts the file (AES-256-GCM, 1 MiB records), publishes it to this node's keets drive in 112 KiB blocks with a manifest, and sends the offer (`state:"file"`, the `pf1:` reference). An offer received is a row with the file's name and "Open file transfer": nothing is fetched until you press "Download / resume". The sheet is the phone's: progress in MB, Pause, "Save verified file" (only a file whose digest matched), "Keep sharing", "Share again", "Remove local copy", and for a file you sent whether the other side downloaded and verified it. "File transfers" lists the chat's stored transfers. The limits are the phone's: 100 MB a file, 32 stored transfers, 2 GB reserved, free disk space checked, contacts only.
- **A received file is saved only where you choose** (a save dialog, its default name the sender's name with path separators, control characters and leading dots removed) and is never opened by the app. The screen never names a path.
- **Voice notes can be recorded**: Attach, "Voice note" shows the phone's dialog (live waveform, blinking dot, m:ss, 60 seconds at most, Send / Cancel). The message is the phone's: the recording published under `/chat/vn-<time><ext>`, `state:"audio"`, body `M:SS|waveform`. The phone records Opus in OGG; Chromium cannot write OGG, so the desktop records AAC in MP4 where the system can and Opus in WebM otherwise (mono, 16 kbps asked for). A `.webm` note is now fetched as audio.
- **The microphone is asked for only when you choose "Voice note"**, and the permission handler now grants camera or microphone only to this app's own window (its top frame), after the macOS question for that device. It used to grant `media` to any `file://` page of the session.
- **Failures are a dialog** (the phone's `ChatFailureDialog`): the whole error, selectable, "Copy details" (stays open), "Close". Send, photo, payment, voice and file failures use it.
- **Delete chat and Delete contact are two things**, with the phone's questions: "Delete chat" clears the conversation and keeps the contact; "Delete contact" removes both. The menu also says "Rename contact". Right-click on a message: "Copy text", "Delete (this device)".
- **New chat lists your saved contacts** under the paste field (a contact whose chat was deleted could not be reached before). No camera scan on the desktop.
- **Attach menu** in the phone's order: Photo, Voice note, Send payment, Request their pay address, File (up to 100 MB), File transfers.
- **Already there, checked against the phone**: payments in Minima and MxUSD, the identity sheet (QR, whole address, name), new chat by paste. The Chat tab lists Keets chats only (Parlons is separate, owner's decision).
- **Store**: `chat.json` gains rows with `state:"file"` and a `rev: 2` marker, nothing else; the file as the older version left it is copied once to `chat.json.before-0.19.2` before this version writes. Transfers live in `chat-files/<id>/` beside it.
- **Tests**: `scripts/chatfiles-test.cjs` (30: the Java tests' cases of `FileDeliveryTest`, `KeetsFilesTest`, `KeetsMediaClientTest`, `KeetsRefGuardTest`, `ChatFilesTest` ported; ciphertext, reference and manifest bytes derived from the Java rules, since the Java tests use random keys) and `scripts/chat-test.cjs` (10, 5 new).
- **Left out**: no "retry" button on a failed text message (the phone has none either; a file resumes from its sheet); no camera scan for a new chat; the phone's wake lock has no desktop counterpart (a transfer pauses when the node stops and resumes from its sheet).
- **Not proven without a node and a phone**: everything on the wire. In particular whether the phone's player reads the desktop's MP4 or WebM note, and a file sent in each direction.

## [0.19.1] — Mail as the phone: folders in a side menu, Mail's own Paper / Dark, the Send funds sheet with the currency picker, selectable text

- **Why**: after the merge of the released Mail (0.19.0) the desktop had the phone's features up to 0.5.26 but not its navigation, its own theme, or the phone's Send funds sheet. An audit against `apks/mail` 0.5.28 and Pandamonium's integrated copy (2.2.53) left these.
- **Folders are in a side menu, as on the phone.** The strip of tabs and the "MINIMA MAIL" bar are gone. Each folder has the phone's header (menu button, folder name) under the shell's host bar; the menu button opens the side menu: "MINIMA MAIL", "you · your six words", Inbox, Outbox, Sent, Archive, a rule, Contacts, Your key, Settings, and the version on the last line, with the unread and waiting counts. Escape or a click outside closes it. The freshness line and the compose button belong to the Inbox only.
- **Mail's theme is its own.** Paper unless you choose Dark in Settings, Appearance (the phone's "Paper by day, dark by night — your call"), kept in Mail's store. It no longer follows the shell's light or dark, and the theme button in Mail's bar (which changed the whole app) is gone. Ink on orange is the phone's `#1A1000` in both themes.
- **Send funds is the phone's sheet**: "Send funds to NAME", the currency picker (Minima and MxUSD, each with the node's sendable balance), the amount, their receiving address (filled from their signed messages, also while the sheet is open), "You'll receive at", a note, then Review. The amount is compared with the balance exactly (no floating point) before the review: "Insufficient balance." The question, the progress notice and the result ("Payment sent", the transaction id whole, Copy tx id) follow the phone. An unanswered send still says the payment may have gone out. The payment itself is unchanged.
- **Text in a conversation can be selected and copied** (phone 0.5.25).
- **Wording**: "33 minutes ago" / clock time / "3 days ago" / "7 Mar" on rows, and "Checked block 2428089 · now" (the block printed plainly), as the phone.
- **Fixed on the way**: two-line labels (a conversation's title and address, a setting's title and hint, a payment's amount and note) were inline and ran together.
- **Dev preview**: Mail fixtures (invented people, keys that are plainly not real, list words) and `?mail=menu|outbox|sent|archive|contacts|key|settings|thread|compose|compose-ready|funds`, `&mm=dark`; captures `scripts/dev-preview/shots/*-mail-*.png` at 932 x 704 and 1440 x 900, with the system in light and in dark.
- **Still behind the phone**: the recipient boxes have no suggestion row and no "N of six words" line; Contacts has "Add contact" as a line, not a + in the header; a photo is sent as JPEG (an animated GIF is not kept); "Share" copies to the clipboard. The sender's own declared name is still shown for an unsaved sender (the released desktop behaviour; the phone shows the six words).
- **Tests**: `scripts/mail-test.cjs` 23 (3 new: the side menu and its order, the theme, the exact amount comparison).

## [0.19.0] — Merge of the released Mail (v0.18.69) into the overhaul branch; the branch's version line becomes 0.19.x

- **What this is**: a merge of the released tag `v0.18.69` (the main line's Mail work, built in another session while this branch was open) into the overhaul branch, and a new version line. The main line owns 0.18.65 to 0.18.69; this branch had used those numbers (and up to 0.18.148) for its own unpushed commits. From here the branch is 0.19.x. No old commit is renumbered.
- **Brought in from the main line** (five released commits, no changelog entries of their own): 0.18.65 complete Mail recipients and resolve conversation keys before sending; 0.18.66 open the selected Mail message with a compact sender address; 0.18.67 publish Mail addresses by default and verify live beacons; 0.18.68 collapse completed Mail addresses before lookup; 0.18.69 open Mail threads directly, show contact words and add MxUSD payments. Files: `main/mail.js`, `main/mailaddress.js`, `main/mail-store.js`, `main/main.js`, `main/preload.js`, `renderer/mail.js`, `renderer/mail.css`, `scripts/mail-test.cjs`.
- **The released Mail code is the Mail code.** This branch's own 0.18.148 had built some of the same features a second time in the main process. In the three files where the two met (`main/mail.js`, `main/mailaddress.js`, `main/mail-store.js`) the released version was taken and 0.18.148's duplicate parts were dropped: its `beaconStatus` / `inspect` (the released `checkOwnBeacon` and `find` with a block are used), its `resolveRecipient`, `ensureOwn`, `phrases`, the kept `card:<key>` and the recorded renewal status. The opt-out key `mail-address-off:<key>` is the same in both, so nothing stored differs.
- **Kept from this branch** in those files: everything older than 0.18.148 (the store set aside on a wallet restore, the reservation-aware send pin, the reset chains), and from 0.18.148 only what the released code does not have: the one-time copy `mail.json.before-0.18.148` and the `rev: 2` marker, Mail's own Paper / Dark choice (`setTheme`), and payments limited to Minima and MxUSD by token id with the node's sendable balance (`payOptions`; the phone's 0.5.27). The released `pay` already carries the thread's subject.
- **IPC**: `mcd:mailBeaconStatus` and `mcd:mailPhrases` (0.18.148) are removed again; `mcd:mailCheckOwnBeacon` and `mcd:mailReadMessage` come from the main line; `mcd:mailSetTheme` and `mcd:mailPayOptions` stay.
- **Tests**: every suite run after the merge. `scripts/mail-test.cjs` is the released file (20 tests). `scripts/mail-address-test.cjs` is rewritten against the merged code (10 tests: the phone's identity and card vectors, default publication and the remembered stop, a renewal posts once, the beacon's block, six-word resolution, the currency picker vector, a payment's command and receipt).
- Nothing here was run on a node.

## The unreleased overhaul branch: local versions 0.18.65 to 0.18.148

The entries from here down to 0.18.65 belong to the Pandamonium overhaul branch and were never released. The released main line used the numbers 0.18.65 to 0.18.69 for other changes (its Mail work, merged in 0.19.0 above). Entries 0.18.64 and older are the released history both lines share.

## [0.18.148] — Mail, the main process up to the phone's 0.5.28: your address published by default, the live beacon check, six words resolved the phone's way, payments in Minima and MxUSD only

- **Why**: the desktop Mail was at parity with the phone's Minima Mail 0.5.8. The phone is at 0.5.28 (and in Pandamonium 2.2.53). This is the main-process half of catching up; the screens follow.
- **Your address is published by default** (phone 0.5.22). The first time Mail opens, the signed card of your own six-word address is put on the renewal list, as the phone does. "Stop publishing address" is remembered and never undone by the app. Each post is one nano-Minima and one signing operation, at most once every four hours and only when the node no longer shows the beacon.
- **The live beacon check gives its block** (0.5.23): `mcd:mailBeaconStatus` reads the address's beacons from the node (the 1,500-block discovery window) and answers "Beacon live · block N", "Beacon live on this node", "Beacon not yet visible · …", "Address conflict", "Publication off · no visible beacon" or "Could not verify beacon: …", in the phone's words. A renewal now records its outcome (visible, submitted, rejected, no suitable signing coin, awaiting approval).
- **Six words resolve as on the phone** (0.5.20, 0.5.24): a Mail key (with or without 0x, with an optional `|address`), a contact link, or six words. Words are matched against every key this store knows (contacts, both parties of every message, your own) before the node is asked; a verified card found on the node is kept so the contact can be relayed later. The refusals are the phone's sentences.
- **Send funds: Minima and MxUSD only** (0.5.26, 0.5.27). `mcd:mailPayOptions` answers the two currencies with the node's sendable balance; MxUSD is the token id `0x7D39745FBD29049BE29850B55A18BF550E4D442F930F86266E34193D89042A90` and nothing else (a token merely named MxUSD is ignored). A payment in any other token is refused before the node is asked. The `send` command is built exactly as before. The receipt message now goes into the thread the payment was made from (its subject), as on the phone; it used to land in the subject-less thread.
- **Mail's theme is its own** (Paper unless you choose Dark), kept in the store as on the phone; `mcd:mailSetTheme`. The screens use it from the next version.
- **Store**: `mail.json` gains meta keys only (`theme`, `card:<key>`, `mail-address-off:<key>`, a `status` on each publication) and a `rev: 2` marker. Before this version writes anything, the file as the older version left it is copied to `mail.json.before-0.18.148`, once. No message, contact or existing key is changed.
- **Tests**: `scripts/mail-address-test.cjs` (10, in `npm run test:mail`) runs the main process on a store in a temp folder with a recorded node: the identity and words from the phone's `PublicAddressTest` vault vector, the signed card, the link rules, the currency picker vector, and `MailBeaconTest`'s rule that a renewal posts only after a successful, empty lookup with a signable coin (and its creation block).
- Not proven here: anything on a node. See the checklist in `docs/PANDAMONIUM_DESKTOP_PLAN.md`.

## [0.18.147] — Coin reservation, review of 0.18.143: a posted coin survives its owner's own retry, and the file is opened again when the node was not ready

- **Found in review**: when an engine named a coin it had already POSTED in one transaction for a second one (its own retry) and then deleted the second, the coin was released although the posted transaction could still spend it. The posted record now stays; only a coin still being built moves to the new transaction.
- **Found in review**: if the node did not answer, or was locked, at the moment the app first asked for the wallet id, the reservations were kept in memory only for the whole session. The open is now tried again with every sweep (once a minute); what memory held is kept and written. The failure is logged once, not every minute.

## [0.18.146] — Coin reservation: the Wallet shows what is held, and by what

- **Found**: the node's "sendable" counts every plain coin of the wallet, the ones backing a live Dexicon order or named for an AtomiX or PandaPools transaction included. The Wallet offered them, and since 0.18.144 a send of them is refused: the figure and the refusal disagreed.
- **"Sendable" in the Wallet is what the wallet can send**: the headline, each token row, the token page and the Send screen show the node's figure less what the engines hold, exact (never rounded, never below zero), with "held 400.5 for a live Dexicon order" beside it. The held amount is the one a send would meet: a coin at the same address as a held coin counts as held.
- **The coins dialog** marks a held coin with a HELD chip and what holds it, in words on the row; the coin's detail is titled "Coin, held" and has a "Held for" line. POOL, BEACON and LOCKED are as before.
- **Send** refuses an amount above the free funds before its review: "Not sendable now: held 400.5 for a live Dexicon order. Nothing was sent."
- **How**: `mcd:reserved` (read only) gives every coin held by AtomiX, PandaPools and Dexicon with what holds it (ids whole) and, per token, held and free (`reserve.funds`: one wallet read per token that has a held coin). A read that fails keeps the last answer.
- **Dexicon's rooms are not redrawn.** They keep their engines' own figures. Each engine's coin reads already leave out what the others hold (0.18.144), so no room builds on another's coins; a room's balance line from the node's `balance` still counts them, and the action is then refused with the reason. Stated in `docs/COIN_RESERVATION.md`.

## [0.18.145] — Coin reservation: the coins of a live Dexicon order are held, and settlement comes first

- **Found**: a FAST order locks nothing on the chain. It names its backing coins, and the Dexicon engine keeps them from its own other orders and from a second take, but nothing kept them from an AtomiX lock, a pool operation or a wallet send. One of those spent the coin; a take arriving before the maker's next refresh was then co-signed into a transaction that could not mine.
- **The engine's own records are mirrored into the shared reservation** by the desktop glue (`main/dexicon.js` `coinHolds`), with no engine file edited: the coins of every live, unexpired order (`myorders`), and this wallet's own coins in takes sent and not yet answered or being voided (`pending_takes`, `void_takes`: a signed take has no expiry). They are read whenever the engine or its page writes those records, when the engine starts, and at every sweep.
- **While the order is live** its coins are refused to an AtomiX lock (`txninput`, or the lock by `send`), to a pool operation and to a wallet Send or Split, each before signing and with the reason ("held for a live Dexicon order"). The maker itself still uses them. **They are free** as soon as the order is cancelled or filled (the engine rewrites its list), at the order's own expiry even if no engine is running, when the coin is spent, and at the latest a day after the engine last listed them.
- **Held across a restart** from the reservation file, before the Dexicon engine has started. A stopped engine answers "cannot say", never "none": its orders are still on the relays.
- **Settlement comes first.** An AtomiX claim or refund and a P2P Chance reveal spend their contract coin and nothing else, and a contract coin is never refused (0.18.143). In addition, a settlement transaction that already carries its contract coin takes a plain coin that is only collateral: the hold is dropped, the log says so, and the order's owner finds the coin gone by its normal path (the maker's pre-sign check refuses a take, its refresh re-backs or withdraws the order). A coin in another owner's transaction is never taken this way, and an AtomiX lock, which spends plain coins only, is refused like anyone else.

## [0.18.144] — Coin reservation: `send` is kept away from held coins (MINIMA and tokens), `consolidate` waits, and each engine is shown only coins it can use

- **A `send` that lets the node choose its coins** (an AtomiX lock, an order or OTC publish, a pool or Dexicon send, Mail, miniMall, Vestr, the wallet's Send and Split) is, while another part of the app holds a coin of that token, pinned with `fromaddress:` to an address none of whose coins is held and whose coins cover the amount. `main/sendpin.js` does it (`pinSend`), now for tokens too, in token units and exact 44-decimal arithmetic. With no coin of that token held the command goes out byte for byte as before, with no extra read.
- **If the free coins cannot cover it, nothing is signed**: "Not enough free funds: 100 MINIMA is held for a live Dexicon order (a send takes its coins from one address, so coins at an address with a held coin count as held). Free: 60 MINIMA, and no single address holds the 60 this send needs. Nothing was signed." `fromaddress:` is the only coin choice `send` has, and it names an address: a free coin at the same address as a held one cannot be sent until the held one is free. This is a stated limit.
- **A `send` its caller already pinned** (`fromaddress:`) is refused when a coin at that address is held. The existing MINIMA pin (`pinMinimaSend`, used by Mail, miniMall, Vestr, the Web Wallet, the wallet and AtomiX's publish) leaves held addresses out as well.
- **`consolidate` is refused while another part of the app holds a coin of that token**, saying what holds it: core's `consolidate` picks its own coins (the largest, by address) and has no way to leave one out. `dryrun:true` passes. AtomiX's own tidy-up consolidation is refused the same way and tries again later, as it does after any failure.
- **Each engine chooses among coins it can use**: an engine's read of its own wallet coins (`coins relevant:true … tokenid:`) leaves out the coins another owner holds. The wallet's reads are not changed, and a look at one coin or one address never is.
- **A `;` chain** that holds a `send` or a `consolidate` is refused while coins are held (it cannot be pinned inside a chain).
- Still to come: the coins of live Dexicon orders (nothing holds a coin without a transaction yet), and the Wallet's display.

## [0.18.143] — One coin reservation under every signer: a coin named for one transaction is refused to the others

- **Found**: AtomiX, PandaPools, the Dexicon engine and the wallet's own commands each chose coins from the one wallet without knowing of each other. The signing queue (0.18.107) keeps their signatures apart in time, not their coins: one engine chose a coin and another chose the same coin before the first had posted. The loser got a rejected transaction, a fill reported "taken" that never mined, or a swap whose lock was dropped.
- **`main/reserve.js`, called from `rpcCall`** (`main/rpc.js`): the one function every node command of every caller passes, inside the signing queue. Each engine now names itself on its commands (`owner`: `atomix`, `pandapools`, `dexicon`, `casino`; everything else is `wallet`). Design: `docs/COIN_RESERVATION.md`.
- **A coin named by `txninput` belongs to that transaction's owner.** Another owner's `txninput` of the same coin is refused before the node sees it, in the node's own failure shape (`status:false`, "Coin 0x… is held for an AtomiX swap. Nothing was signed."), so each engine's own path deletes its transaction; that `txndelete` frees the transaction's other coins. Engines are not edited and sign exactly what they signed before.
- **Released, always**: by `txndelete` of a transaction that was not posted; when the node refuses the input; when the node rejects the `txnpost` (`status:false`, not pending); when the coin is spent; when a posted transaction is known dead (12 blocks and 10 minutes after the post the coin is still unspent and nothing in the mempool spends it); after 15 minutes for a build never posted or deleted; when the owner's engine stops (its builds); when the wallet is replaced (everything). A `txnpost` that answers ok is not a confirmation: the coin stays held until one of these.
- **A sweep once a minute** asks the node about the reserved coins only, one coin per read and at most 25 reads a turn, so a wallet of thousands of coins is never listed. A coin spent by a phone or a MiniDapp on the same node leaves the registry there.
- **Kept across a restart**: `coin-reservations-<wallet id>.json` in the app's data folder, written (temp file, fsync, rename) before the reserving command is forwarded. The wallet id is the SHA-256 of a seed the node derives for this alone, so another wallet opens another file. On a wallet replacement the file is moved aside under a dated name, never deleted.
- **A claim or a refund is never held up.** Only a plain wallet coin is ever refused: before a refusal the node is asked what the coin is, and an HTLC coin, a pool reserve or a bet is always let through, whoever named it first. If the node cannot say, AtomiX and P2P Chance are let through.
- Not yet in this version (next ones): `send`, `consolidate`, the coins of live Dexicon orders, and the Wallet's display.

## [0.18.142] — Quit warns when work is in flight, from the same source as Shutdown

- **Found**: Shutdown warned about swaps and trades in flight; Quit did not. Quit in the tray or the menu, Cmd-Q, or closing the window on Windows and Linux stopped the node with no question, whatever was running.
- **Quit now asks first when something is in flight**, each thing named in plain words with what quitting does: AtomiX swaps not finished ("cannot settle while the app is closed and can time out at 14:35", the nearest time limit of their locks; "at about" when it is worked out from a block number), a market on the book, OTC availability, open OTC deals, pool transactions not yet confirmed, Exchange trades sent and not yet answered, fills of your Exchange orders whose outcome is not known yet, Exchange orders waiting for a block, P2P Chance bets still open. Buttons: "Quit anyway" and "Stay open" (Enter and Esc stay open). With nothing in flight there is no question.
- **"Could not be checked" is said truthfully.** An engine that has not started, a store that could not be opened or a read that gave no answer in 5 seconds is named, and the question is asked: it is never read as "nothing in flight".
- **A quit the system asks for is never held behind a question** (logout, restart, power off): it goes on, and what was in flight is written to the log. The same for a quit with the node already shut down by the user (that question was asked then). This app has no updater that quits it: an update is downloaded and shown in the Downloads folder.
- **Every quit still lets the engines finish first**: the node is stopped through the same bounded drain as before (0.18.117; each engine's own bound, 30 s in all), so a transaction posted in the last second is recorded.
- **One source for both warnings** (`main/inflight.js`), fed by each engine's own `activeWork()`: Shutdown no longer builds its own from other reads, so the two cannot disagree. Shutdown gains what only Quit's source knew: OTC availability and deals, Exchange fills with an unknown outcome and orders waiting for a block, P2P Chance bets, the nearest time limit.
- Off the Mac, closing the window asks through the same quit while the window is still open; "Stay open" leaves it as it was. On the Mac closing the window still only hides it.

## [0.18.141] — The old AtomiX and Pools panels are removed, after a written parity audit

- **`docs/DEXICON_PARITY_AUDIT.md`**: every screen, button, dialog, setting, state and message of the old AtomiX panel (120 items) and the old Pools panel (117 items), with where each is in Dexicon rooms ① and ② or the shared screens. Both sides were read in full.
- **Put back before the removal** (the audit found them lost or changed): the OTC Withdraw button is always there again (it was drawn only while the screen believed the offer live); a failed "Check for reserves" keeps its message (it was erased in the same click); the Swap quote in room ② is asked again when the pools move; the K/KMIN number beside "healthy / migrate soon"; "Stop every other copy of this wallet that signs" on the paused-signing card; "Building the statement…" while it is built; the chart takes a theme change at once; a Daylight user of the old AtomiX panel opens Dexicon in its light theme.
- **The fonts.** The only declarations of Inter and JetBrains Mono were in the old AtomiX sheet. They are in `dexicon.css` now; without that, all three rooms would have fallen back to system fonts.
- **Not carried over**, each named in the audit with the reason: the "real funds" label, the app version in the panel header, Swap opening on Sell, the bid / ask / spread line on Swap, the PandaPools "Original" style, the progress spinners, and some explanatory sentences. Owed items are listed there too.
- **Removed**: `renderer/atomix-ui.js` (929 lines), `atomix-ui.css` (305), `pools.js` (875), `pools.css` (204), their tags and hidden holders in `index.html`, their wiring in `app.js`, three dead CSS rules. `poolcalc.js` stays: room ② uses it. No IPC or preload function was used only by the old panels.
- **The "same call, same arguments" tests keep their oracle.** The old panels were run one last time and their calls recorded (`scripts/fixtures/old-atomix-panel.json`, 51 cases; `old-pools-panel.json`, 67 cases); the room tests compare against the recordings.
- **Two tests that had failed for a long time.** `panels-test` "0.17.20 review fixes stay fixed" tested a removed file, and failed only because it counted dialogs written with double quotes (8 found, 9 stamped): it is replaced by tests of the same properties on the rooms (MxUSD naming, the quote race, the scrim); its style-stamp property has no counterpart. `keets-test` "sidecar bundle present" failed in any checkout that had not downloaded the sidecar's dependencies (`npm run fetch:keets`; they are never in git): the source and jar checks are now their own test, and the dependency check is required in CI and before a signed Mac build (`dist:mac:signed`), and says it was skipped elsewhere.
- The mismatch once reported between `main/atomix/lib/maker.js` and its donor: explained in the audit (the donor had moved to 0.1.60; the copy was renewed in 0.18.130; 40 files are byte-identical today).

## [0.18.140] — No script can touch a node by accident, and a TEST copy of the app

- **Found**: `scripts/atomix-unit.js` (the "unit" suite, also run by CI) started `docker exec` into a container of a fixed name whenever docker was reachable, and booted the whole AtomiX engine on it: `vault action:seed`, `seedrandom modifier:ethbridge`, `newscript`, `coinnotify`, `txnlist` / `txndelete`. On the owner's machine that name is a wallet with real funds. The two live gates did the same, and `scripts/cdp-eval.js` ran any expression inside whatever app listened on a fixed debugging port.
- **No name, address or port of any node is written in the repo any more.** A script reaches a node only through `scripts/test-node.cjs`, and only when the caller names it (`AX_TEST_NODE_RPC`, optionally `AX_TEST_NODE_CONTAINER`; no default) and sets `AX_TEST_NODE_I_OWN_THIS=yes`. It first prints in plain words what it will send, the seed read first. Named but not owned: it stops with exit code 2, nothing sent. `cdp-eval.js` takes its port from `AX_TEST_CDP_PORT` under the same rule.
- **The frozen-quote check is hermetic.** The engine boots on a mock node: a throwaway identity from fake values written in the test (the Ethereum key is `0x11…11`), a book of one order signed by a second throwaway identity. It no longer skips in CI. The same check against a real node remains, behind the variables.
- **The unit suite fails if anything leaves the process** (`scripts/no-reach.cjs`): a child process or a socket throws and is counted; the engine's network calls go to a stand-in. `scripts/hermetic-test.cjs` runs the suite and the gates with only stand-in `docker`, `curl`, `security` and `java` on PATH and fails if one is started.
- The live gates, with nothing named, say so and exit 1. The boot gate's expected Ethereum address is now `AX_TEST_NODE_EXPECT_ETH` (optional) instead of one node's address in the code.
- **`scripts/dev-run.sh`** (new, `docs/DEV_RUN.md`): starts the app from source as a TEST copy. Its own folder (refused if it is, is inside or contains the real `minimaCore` folder, or was not created by the script), its own ports (probed every run, refused if busy), jars never fetched (refused with the copy commands; `--jars <dir>` copies), the sha256 of each jar printed.
- **What was not kept by folder now has a separate name in a TEST copy** (`main/testprofile.js`): the login keychain service (it held the RPC secret, the mail and shop identities and the P2P Chance preimages under one name: a second copy would have overwritten the real install's), the safeStorage key (through the app name), no login item, no update feed at start. The window title, a tag on every screen and the Dock badge say TEST.

## [0.18.139] — Dexicon ② Pools lows, and an agreed OTC deal under a halt is ended (review)

- **Messages pointed at a menu entry that does not exist** ("Open Pool recovery and confirm…"). The button is Confirm wallet signing state, on the pool's own card in My LP. The glue's sentence now says so; the engine's own sentences ("confirm it in Pool recovery", vendored and not edited) are put into those words where the desktop shows them (room ② and the wallet's Consolidate refusal).
- **Esc or Close during "Create and save"** reported "The backup was not saved" while the save could still complete. A dialog closed while its action is still working now says what is true: it is not known yet whether the backup was saved, and how to tell. The archive setting's dialog follows the same rule.
- **Room ① residual: an agreed OTC deal while AtomiX is halted.** The halt gate (0.18.130) refused the engine's leg-1 lock and the deal went back to AGREED; the engine's reset rewrote the row, so the deal never expired and was tried again every 10 minutes for ever. Had the halt ever cleared, it would have locked at its old price. Now a deal refused for a halt is ended for good through the engine's own reject: REJECTED in the store, and a REJECT message so the counterparty knows the deal is off. If the message cannot be sent, the deal is still ended here. The engine is not edited.
- Every room ② dev-preview shot retaken.

## [0.18.138] — Dexicon ② Pools: a fresh price at Review, and a gate that cannot stay closed (review, mediums)

- **Found (a)**: the create form kept the opening price and the balances it was opened with, across tabs and rooms, with no age limit. Hours later the review still said "matches MEXC market", and the pool would open off the market (arbitraged at its owner's cost).
- **At Review the price and the balances are read again.** If the price is the same, the review follows from that fresh read. If it moved (or its source changed), the form stays, the screen says the old and the new price and how old the first read was, the MINIMA needed is worked out again, and Review must be pressed again. If the price can no longer be read, nothing is sent. If the manual form is open and a market price can now be read, the pool opens at that price and the form says so.
- **Found (b)**: four glue calls waited on one engine callback with no time limit (statement, check reserves, put a pool away, confirm signing). One dropped callback left Dexicon's one money gate closed in all three rooms until the app was restarted.
- **Those calls now end** after a set time with what is true: "PandaPools gave no answer in time, so it is not known whether this was done. Check Activity before trying again." The screen says those words as they are, never "failed". A reserve check that got no answer no longer blocks the next one as "already running".
- **Read-only actions no longer hold the money gate**: Export statement and Check for reserves.
- **The coins dialog** (with Split and Consolidate) no longer opens from room ② Wallet while a money action is running.
- The glue's shared `withTimeout` now clears its timer when the call ends.

## [0.18.137] — The wallet-restore gate: a store that cannot be opened, and this wallet's own pools (review, high)

- **Found (a)**: a damaged `pandapools.sqlite` fails its integrity check on every start. Room ② then said "starting" for ever without the error, and the restore gate said "wait a moment and try again" for ever. AtomiX had the same dead end with `atomix.sqlite`.
- **The error is carried and said.** `status()` of PandaPools and of AtomiX now gives why the last start failed. Rooms ② and ① show it in plain words with what to do: restart the app; if it stays, the file is damaged and can be set aside.
- **Set the store aside and start empty** (a button on that message, after a question): the file is moved aside under a dated name, never deleted, and the engine starts with an empty store. Refused unless the engine's last start failed. For pools the next step is said: restore them from the backup file.
- **The restore gate can be passed by a deliberate confirm**, as Dexicon's could since 0.18.110: when PandaPools or AtomiX could not open its store, or has still not started after two minutes, the gate names exactly what could not be checked and offers the way on. Known work (a swap in flight, a pool transaction still waiting, an order on the chain) still refuses. On a wallet replacement a pool store that could not be opened is moved aside too; AtomiX's store already was.
- **Found (b)**: a live pool was neither refused nor warned of when a different wallet was restored. Its reserves would have stayed where only the old wallet's owner keys can withdraw them.
- **The gate now lists this wallet's own pools that are not put away**, each with its whole address and its reserves, and the user must confirm: "These pools belong to the wallet you are replacing. Their funds can only be withdrawn by that wallet. Close them first, or make sure you have its 24 words and a pool backup."
- **The same wallet restored again**: the app cannot tell beforehand which wallet a phrase or a backup file holds, so the warning is shown once and says so: if it is the same wallet, nothing changes for the pools (the wallet is compared before and after, since 0.18.116). A chain resync without a phrase or a file does not pass the gate at all.
- Not done: the dated name still ends in "-legacy-keys-", the name used for a replaced wallet's stores, also for a store set aside because it is damaged.

## [0.18.136] — Dexicon ② Pools: Add liquidity reviews what is sent (review, high)

- **Found**: the engine takes one figure for an Add, the MINIMA, and works the token side out itself from the pool's ratio when it posts; it refuses a zero MINIMA amount. The screen had two fields. With 100 and nothing, the review and the result said "100 MINIMA and 0 MxUSD" while the pool took the matching MxUSD as well. With nothing and 5, the user confirmed and was then told "enter an amount to add".
- **Add liquidity has one field, MINIMA.** Under it, the MxUSD the pool will take at its current ratio, as an estimate that follows the typing.
- **The review states both sides**: "Add 500 MINIMA and about 1.61049999 MxUSD to the pool. MxUSD is taken at the pool's ratio when the transaction is posted, so this figure can change slightly.", with the pool pair and the pool address.
- **An empty or zero MINIMA amount is refused before the question**, in the form.
- **The result says what the pool took**, not what was typed. The engine answers with the transaction id only, so the glue works the token side out by the engine's own rule, with the engine's own arithmetic, from the reserves the engine had just read (`depositUsed` in `main/pandapools.js`); the same figures go into the Activity record ("Added 500 MINIMA and 1.61049999 MxUSD to a pool"). When that figure is not known the result says so and points to Activity.
- The call is unchanged: `ppDeposit(pool, MINIMA, token)`. The third argument is now the reviewed estimate; the engine does not use it.
- **Swap**: the review and the result print the amount the pools take (the route's `totalIn`); when it differs from what was typed, the review says both.

## [0.18.135] — Dexicon ② Pools: a token is its id, never its name (review, high)

- **Found**: a token is named by whoever made it. One called "MxUSD " (a trailing space), "MxUSD" with a zero-width character, or "МxUSD" with a Cyrillic М was shown unmarked on Swap; the pool line and the review showed no token id; the pair list followed the scan, so a deep fake pool could be the default pair; the selection was a position in that list, so a rescan could silently change the selected token. MINIMA could be paid for a worthless token.
- **The rule now**: the one MxUSD is token `0x7D39745FBD29049BE29850B55A18BF550E4D442F930F86266E34193D89042A90`. Only it is written "MxUSD"; it is listed first and is the default pair.
- **Every other token** is written by its own name marked "(not MxUSD)", with its whole token id in the pair name (the pool line, the pair list, the Pools and My LP cards) and in the swap review ("Pool pair"). Control, zero-width and direction characters are removed from a name before it is shown.
- **A name that imitates MxUSD or USDT** (compared without case, spaces, accents, zero-width characters and with Cyrillic and Greek look-alike letters mapped) adds a line of its own to the review: "This token is not MxUSD. Its name imitates it."
- **The selected pair is held by token id.** A rescan that reorders the pools changes nothing. If the selected token leaves the registry, nothing is selected and the screen says so with the id; no other pair is selected in its place.
- **Activity records** are labelled by token id in the glue (`labelById` in `main/pandapools.js`): a new swap's record, the pool transactions of All pools and the history rows. An older record that ends in a name is relabelled only where the node's own history of that transaction names one token; otherwise it is left exactly as recorded. The screen no longer rewrites the word "USDT" in a record ("Tether USDT" used to become "Tether MxUSD").
- Not covered: look-alike letters outside Cyrillic and Greek do not get the extra warning line (they are still marked "(not MxUSD)" with the id).

## [0.18.134] — Dexicon ① MxUSD: Market and OTC beside the wide captures

- Market and OTC were built from phone-size captures, because two Fold captures were mislabelled. The real wide captures (19 Nov) were read beside the regenerated dev-preview shots, and the differences fixed:
- **Market**: the line under the explainer reads as the phone's ("Tap a level to take it, use the Swap page, or publish your own offer."); the price line carries the bid, the ask and its age under parity ("Parity mid 1  (bid 1 / ask 1)  ·  16s ago"); an empty offer reads "Off — edit your order, then Publish."; in the book, a maker's key sits at the outer edge of its half (a bid's at the left, an offer's at the right) with size and price against the divider.
- **OTC**: the explainer is titled "OTC — A PRIVATE DEAL"; the two availability fields are a line, not a box.
- The explainer cards of Market and OTC are open until folded, as the phone shows them.
- Kept different on purpose: the explainers say "this app answers while it is running", where the phone says it answers in the background with Dexicon closed (true of the phone, not of a desktop app); for MEXC no bid or ask is written beside the mid (the engine keeps the mid only).

## [0.18.133] — Dexicon ① MxUSD: OTC rows by id, and the low findings (review M6 and lows)

- **OTC: a button can no longer send your offer to a different LP.** The Buy / Sell buttons of the LP list named a row by its position. The engine replaces the list while the screen is not redrawn under a field in use, so a click on row 2 could propose to another LP, whose ACCEPT then locks by itself. Each button now names the LP by its id and the sizes its card showed; the row is looked up again at the click and once more after the terms dialog, and if the LP left or changed its sizes nothing is sent and the list is redrawn.
- **A swap in ERROR is not called finished while its lock could still be refunded.** It stays under In progress, with Refund now, until its timelock window has closed (the engine's own rule). The MINIMA-mode banner and the stopped-node count include it.
- **The MINIMA-mode banner is computed.** "Nothing of yours is live there" was asserted without looking at OTC. It now says what was checked: your swaps, your levels on the book, and your OTC availability (reported by the engine); a live OTC availability keeps the room in MINIMA mode.
- **OTC Withdraw while halted** no longer says "Availability withdrawn": the engine posts nothing while halted, and the screen says so.
- **A failed Save & publish** no longer says "NOT published" as if nothing stood: the order is saved, and AtomiX publishes a saved order again by itself (at the next reprice, or within 30 minutes). A refusal (halted, wallet being replaced) says nothing was saved.
- **Rung sizes are written as typed.** The editor filled each rung's size through the price format: six significant figures, and an exponent from a million up (1234567.89 became "1.23457e+6"). The old panel did the same; that was a defect, and the two now differ in this one case on purpose.
- Closing the editor during a save no longer re-enables the buttons mid-action: the busy label is set and cleared in one place and every money action is behind Dexicon's one gate.
- "See the swap below" is no longer said where no card is drawn.
- A currency switch clears the OTC list and the "a market was live" flag (the switch withdraws the market and disarms OTC).

## [0.18.132] — Dexicon ① MxUSD: hidden swaps, the halt card and the node-stopped notice say what is true (review M3, M4, M5)

- **A hidden swap can always be found again.** Hide removed a swap's card from Swap and Activity and promised "It stays in History", but History listed finished swaps only, so a hidden swap still in flight was shown nowhere. History now lists every finished swap and every hidden one, with Check now, Refund now and a new **Unhide**.
- **The halt card says what is true.** It said "Swaps already in flight keep settling: that part is safe to leave running". That is false for the Minima side of a swap under a key the node no longer owns: it cannot be claimed or refunded until the node owns that key again, and a maker swap whose taker claims is forfeited at the timelock. The card now says which key no longer matches (Minima, Ethereum, or both), what still settles and what does not, lists each unfinished swap with its full hash, what cannot happen for it and its lock's deadline, and gives only instructions that have a button: Export the stale ETH key, Sweep, Open Activity. "Rescue what you need FIRST" with no tool under it is gone.
- **The node-stopped notices say what is at stake.** "Swaps already locked are refunded at their deadline once the node runs" was wrong for a swap waiting on this node's claim. Both notices now count the unfinished swaps (read from AtomiX's own store, which works with the node off), say that a claim is lost if the node stays off past its deadline, say to start the node now, and give the nearest deadline of your own locks.
- Each swap row from the glue now carries its own lock's deadline (`timelock`, `legIsMinima`).

## [0.18.131] — Dexicon ① MxUSD: a take's outcome is said truthfully (review M2)

- **"Nothing was locked" is said only when that is known.** After a take, the alert said it for any error. A Minima send can time out at 180 s and still post, and an Ethereum broadcast can fail after it went out. Now only a refusal known to come before any lock says it (the quote expired, the market was withdrawn, the maker left the reviewed book, AtomiX held or halted, the engine not ready). Every other outcome says **"No lock was confirmed. Look at In progress before trying again."**
- **A buy in several parts now says why a part did not start.** The reasons were dropped ("0 of N parts locked"); the glue now returns them and the alert shows each one.
- The alert no longer says "nothing was spent". When an approval of USDT was sent, it says that it costs gas.

## [0.18.130] — Dexicon ① MxUSD: the halt is enforced by the glue and the engine's own lock (review H1)

- **A halt is now enforced where the money moves, not only on the screen.** The identity halt exists for when the node's keys no longer match the ones AtomiX saved: anything locked under the saved key then cannot be refunded. Until now only the screen refused, from a copy of the halt state up to 60 s old. The glue (`main/atomix.js`) now reads the engine's verdict at the moment of every call that creates a new liability and refuses it in plain words: a take (and each further part of a sweep), Save & publish, Publish, OTC Go live, an OTC offer, an OTC accept and an OTC counter.
- **An agreed OTC deal no longer locks by itself while halted.** When an LP's ACCEPT of an earlier proposal arrived in the engine's own poll, the engine locked leg 1 with no screen involved, halted or not. The glue now refuses that lock at the engine's `executeOtc`; the engine takes it as a failed execute and puts the deal back to AGREED with no secret, no swap row and no lock.
- **Never refused by a halt** (a halt never freezes settlement): claims, refunds, Check now, Refund now, withdrawing the market, withdrawing OTC availability, rejecting a deal, and every read.
- A halt now redraws the screens at once (the engine's notice raises an update as well as a notification).
- **Engine copy brought level with atomix-mds 0.1.60 (03d5665).** `main/atomix/lib/maker.js` and `lib/peg.js` were two donor releases behind (0.1.59 spread setting, 0.1.60 randomised sizes, both 18 Nov; the desktop copy was 0.1.57). Both are file copies; a config with no spread and no rand, which is every config the desktop writes, gives exactly the ladder it gave before. The parity check passes again (40 files).
- Known limit, unchanged engine behaviour: an agreed deal refused this way is retried by the engine every 10 minutes and is refused each time while the halt lasts.

## [0.18.129] — Dexicon ② Pools, the comparison round (Phase 3d)

- Every screen and state of room ② was captured in Dexicon dark and light at 932x704 and 1440x900 and read beside the Fold and phone captures.
- **Collect withdrawn funds to my wallet** is now the first line of Back up / restore pools, where the phone has it, and no longer a button of its own on My LP. The same call after the same question.
- **Wallet**: each balance card has its token mark, as on the phone.
- Kept different from the phone on purpose: "click to copy" where the phone says "tap"; Add liquidity keeps the desktop's two amounts (the engine call takes both) and shows what the pool price pairs with the MINIMA typed; Close says the reserves pass through the pool's owner address, which is how the desktop engine pays out; a balance opens the wallet's own coins dialog; the old panel's second look ("Original") is gone, Dexicon's own light theme takes its place.
- The checks that need a node are listed in the plan, section 9.

## [0.18.128] — Dexicon ② Pools, Activity, and pool operations everywhere they are counted (Phase 3d)

- **Activity** in room ②: My activity (every swap, create, add, migrate and close you sent, newest first, with its state, the transaction id whole, when it was sent and when it was last checked; a failure in plain words with whether funds moved) and All pools (every pool's transactions this node holds, then the older unverified observations, marked as such). Show more loads the rest.
- **Pool operations are in the shared Activity and History** of rooms ① and ③, after the AtomiX swaps: waiting ones first in Activity, finished ones in History.
- **The Shutdown warning and the restore check no longer take "PandaPools has not started" for "nothing is waiting".** When there is a pool store and the engine has not started, they say the pool operations could not be checked. A data folder that never had a pool store still counts as nothing waiting. A wallet restore now waits for PandaPools to start, as it already did for AtomiX.
- Not run on a node.

## [0.18.127] — Dexicon ② Pools, Wallet (Phase 3d)

- **Wallet**: the phone's "Available to pool" card with your receive address whole (one click to copy), Consolidate MINIMA coins, Consolidate MxUSD coins and Check node connection, then one card per balance: the sendable amount in full, the coin count, and confirmed, locked, unconfirmed and when it was read.
- **Consolidate** is the wallet's own, with its question and the pool owner-key rule: it refuses, with the reason, while a pool owner key cannot safely sign. It passes the same one-at-a-time gate as every other money action.
- **A balance opens the wallet's own coins dialog**: every coin with its id whole, Split, Consolidate, and the token's details with Send. These are the existing flows, unchanged.
- The phone's Wallet screen has no Send or Split of its own (in Pandamonium they are the wallet's), so none was added here.

## [0.18.126] — Dexicon ② Pools, create a pool in four steps, and Migrate (Phase 3d)

- **Create a pool** on My LP, in four steps shown on the screen: 1 Before you start (what a pool needs from you: the app and node running, the backup; tick to go on), 2 Amounts (the MxUSD to provide; the MINIMA is worked out from the MEXC or live-pool price, with what you have and what is missing), 3 Review ("Create this pool?"), 4 Back up (the encrypted backup, asked for right after the pool is posted).
- **The forced backup is the engine's rule.** While a backup is owed, step 1 says so and offers Back up now. If the engine refuses a create because the backup is owed, the screen says no funds moved and goes back to that step.
- With no market price and no live pool to match, the manual form is shown with its warning: both amounts, the opening price they give, and a box to tick, as before.
- **Migrate** on each pool (the filled button once the pool is near its floor): the two new reserves, the question, then the backup again, because it must cover the new pool.
- The engine is called exactly as before: the same price anchor, the same MINIMA derived from the price (up to the 6-decimal grain, the engine's own create rule), the amount as typed.
- Not run on a node.

## [0.18.125] — Dexicon ② Pools, backup, restore and recovery (Phase 3d)

- **Back up / restore pools** on My LP opens the recovery tools: Back up my pools to a file (the encrypted pool and node backup, password typed twice, at least 12 characters), Restore pools from a file (Load file, Extract node backup, Restore), Archive connection, How recovery works. Each makes the old panel's call with the same arguments.
- **When the engine requires a backup**, My LP says so at the top with Back up now. This is the engine's own rule before another pool can be created or migrated; the screen reads it and never works around it.
- A refused backup or restore is said in plain words, without the internal code, and says that nothing was saved or restored. The saved file's whole path is shown.
- The typed password leaves the fields before the save starts and is never kept.
- Not on the desktop, because its engine has no such call: the phone's "Check a backup file", "Recover & withdraw (one-time exit)" and "Recover on existing wallet". Restore and Check for reserves cover the reserve check; nothing was removed that the old panel had.

## [0.18.124] — Dexicon ② Pools, My LP (Phase 3d)

- **My LP**: the phone's summary card (your MINIMA reserves, or "No pools yet", or that saved pools need a reserve check), then each of your pools with its liquidity, value, pool price, fees earned, price since open, impermanent loss, age and floor headroom. Pool address and owner key are shown whole, labelled, one click to copy.
- **Add** and **Close** on each pool, **Collect withdrawn funds to my wallet**, **Check for reserves**, **put a closed pool away** and **bring it back**, **Confirm wallet signing state** when owner signing is paused, the withdrawn-funds cards (still moving, or cannot be moved from this wallet), **Export statement** and the **Pool calculator**. Each makes the call the old panel made, with the same arguments, after the same question.
- Fees and percentages are cut to 4 decimals, not rounded.
- Saved pools that cannot be read are said to be unreadable, never shown as "No pools yet".
- Create a pool, Migrate and the backup and recovery tools follow in the next versions.
- Not run on a node. Tests run each action through the old panel and the new screen and compare the calls.

## [0.18.123] — Dexicon ② Pools, the pool list (Phase 3d)

- **Pools**: every pool of the shared registry as the phone lists it: reserves, spot price, fees accrued and the pool address, whole, one click to copy. The line above says how many pools there are and their MINIMA depth, added exactly. SCAN reads the registry again now.
- **Individual / Combined**: Combined shows one card per token with the pools combined, total reserves, aggregate spot price and tradeable depth, from the engine's own figures.
- A pool of another token shows that token's id whole.
- Reads only: nothing on this screen signs or sends.

## [0.18.122] — Dexicon ② Pools, the room and Swap (Phase 3d)

- **Room ② is PandaPools as the phone draws it inside Dexicon**, over the engine the desktop already runs, the same pool store (`pandapools.sqlite`) and the same calls. Its own bottom bar: Swap, Pools, My LP, Wallet, Activity. Each remembers where you were.
- **Swap**: the pool line (pools and depth), your balance, the MEXC line for the MxUSD pair, You pay ⇅ You receive (est.), the rate, price impact, route and fee, then SWAP and "Confirm swap". The click asks for a fresh quote and posts exactly that quote, as before. There is no slippage setting, as on the phone: if a pool moves first, the swap is rejected and you keep your funds. Recent activity is listed under the button.
- **One money action at a time in all three rooms.** Rooms ① and ② now pass the same gate as the Exchange: while a swap, a publish, a send or a pool action is being reviewed, signed or sent, a second click, or a button in another room, does nothing.
- **A failed pool action says what happened, whether funds moved and what to do.** The engine reports fixed sentences ("Could not identify the input signing key", "could not mint an owner key"); the screen says them in plain words and adds what the node itself answered. The glue keeps the node's last refusal by command name only, never its arguments.
- The dollar token is named MxUSD by its token id. A token that only calls itself USDT is said to be another token.
- The old Pools panel is no longer drawn. Its file stays in the tree until the parity audit. The other four screens of the room follow in the next versions; until then they say so.
- Not run on a node. Tests: `scripts/dexicon-pp.test.cjs` runs the old panel's swap beside the new one and compares every call and argument.

## [0.18.121] — Dexicon Exchange, the low findings of the review

- **"You pay at most"** is now the sum of what each take can cost. Every take is its own transaction and the engine rounds each one (MxUSD paid up, MxUSD received down, 8 decimals); the screen rounded the total once, which could be less than the sum. The funds check uses the same figure, and "receive at least" is the sum of the round-downs.
- **The order book's Total column is cut**, not rounded up (4 decimals).
- **Changing an on-chain order's price** takes at most 6 decimals, as placing one does; the page would have rounded a longer price without asking.
- **Number entry.** "1,234.5" was turned into 1.2345 as it was typed (the comma became a point and the second point was dropped). A number with a comma or with more than one point is now left as typed, the ticket says "Type the number without a comma: digits and one decimal point, for example 1234.5", and nothing can be sent. The same for the new-price dialog. In room ① a comma was stripped, so "1,5" was fifteen: such an entry is now no amount. Enter in a ticket field never trades.
- **Bridge calls are bound to their engine.** The hidden page's calls carry the epoch of the engine the frame was made for; the main process refuses one that is for an engine no longer running (also for the identity delivery).
- **Orders shows what the page said.** The market maker's dialogs are the page's own; their answers (a ladder that was not saved, a refused cancel or price change) appeared only in the Trade log. The last lines are now shown on Orders too.
- Tests: `scripts/dexicon-ui.test.cjs` (three), `scripts/dexicon-host.test.cjs` (one).

## [0.18.120] — One Dexicon store per wallet, and the page's signatures are checked in the main process (review, M3 + M4)

- **M3, the fault.** There was one `dexicon.sqlite`. A wallet that was not its owner had it moved aside, so switching the data folder A → B → A set A's store aside for good: A's takes that were sent and not yet answered were never voided.
- **One store per wallet.** The store is named by the wallet's public trading identity, whole (`dexicon-0x…128 hex….sqlite`); the host derives the identity before it opens anything. B opens its own store, A's stays where it is, and A finds it again with its orders and its unanswered takes. An existing `dexicon.sqlite` is renamed once to the name of the identity recorded in it (to this wallet's when it records none), is never written under the old name, is kept aside if that name already exists, and is left alone with no engine started when it cannot be read.
- **M4, the fault.** The hidden page can ask for `txncreate … txnsign … txnpost` through the bridge, because it builds and signs its own takes. A script injected into that page (one unescaped relay string would do) could have had any spend signed.
- **The main process now checks what the node holds** (`txnlist id:`) before it forwards a signature or a post from the page (`main/dexicon-signgate.js`). A transaction that pays only this wallet, with nothing burned, is signed and posted (the page's void). A transaction that pays anyone else is never posted, and is signed only inside the FAST trade the user has just reviewed on the native screen (side, limit price, amount: told to main by the app's own window, which the frame cannot reach), against one live order the service itself has read from the relays, paying only that order's addresses, at that order's price or better. Only the page's own command shapes are accepted, only on transactions the page created; `txnimport` left the bridge. Refusals are logged and shown.
- **What M4 does not cover** (docs/DEXICON_ENGINE_NOTES.md has the full list): which of the orders inside the reviewed limit is taken; the engine's own record of the take (the page writes it only after signing, so it cannot be read at the check; a donor-side change is proposed in the notes); an order a relay invents (the reviewed limit and amount bound the loss). Not proven on a node: the exact shape of `txnlist`.
- Tests: `scripts/dexicon-host.test.cjs` (M3: A, B, A again; the one-time rename in four cases. M4: the engine's own take and relock built through the bridge; eight kinds of forged spend refused; the review's side, limit, amount and age; a change between check and signature), `scripts/dexicon-ui.test.cjs` (the frame cannot send the review).

## [0.18.119] — Large takes reach the relay, and a quiet relay is not "down" (review, H4 + M1)

- **H4, the fault.** `main/netfetch.js` refused any POST body over 64K characters. A sealed take is about eight times its transaction (hex inside JSON, hex again in the signed payload, hex again as the sealed blob), so a take of more than about 7.8 KB of transaction never left the app: "no relay accepted the take", after the key uses were spent. The protocol's own limit is 256K (`lib/envelope.js`).
- **Fix.** `postText` takes `opts.maxBody`, at most 256K; only the Dexicon host passes it, and only for a URL that has passed its relay allow-list. Every other caller keeps 64K. Test: a 12,000 byte take built with the engine's own `seal` and `envelope.msg` is over the old cap, inside the protocol's limit, accepted by the engine's own verifier, and sent with the 256K limit on both paths (the page's take, the service's reply); a non-relay URL is still refused.
- **M1, the fault.** The hidden page asks a relay to hold a quiet poll for 25 s; the app ends an outbound request after 15 s without a byte. Every quiet poll failed, the relay was marked down, and the Trade screen and the status line said "no relay answers" on a quiet book.
- **Fix.** The page's bridge (`renderer/dexicon/mds.js`, the one desktop-owned file of the page) cuts a GET's `hold` to 10 s, so a quiet relay answers "nothing new" inside the limit and the page polls again at once. The page files stay verbatim and no connection limit was raised. The "no relay answers" wording now appears only when polls really fail.

## [0.18.118] — The FAST Trade click signs only what was reviewed, once (review, H1 + H2)

- **H1, the fault.** The review on the Trade screen was planned on the service's relay book (one relay every 10 s); the hidden page signs from its own long-polled book. "BUY 100 from 1 FAST ask, no order is placed" could publish a RESTING order when that ask had left the page's book, and "nothing is traded now" could be an immediate take.
- **One book.** The page now sends its own FAST book with every report and the ticket plans on that (the ladder is still the read model). A crossing order that cannot be taken in the amount (minimum remainder) is said as such, and the button is off, instead of promising a resting order the page would not place.
- **The click carries the review.** `place` sends `expect` (take or rest) and, for a take, the amount and the worst price. The page's own `crossing()` is asked again at the click; unless it finds the same, nothing is signed: "The book changed since you reviewed this. Review it again. Nothing was signed." A reviewed take can no longer turn into a resting order inside the page (`AX.maker.create` is refused while it runs).
- **H2, the fault.** `submit()` had no in-flight guard and kept the amount: a double click or a held Enter published the FAST order twice, and after a sweep the button was armed again with the full amount.
- **One money action at a time.** Trade (both buttons), cancel, change price and Stop the market maker share one gate on the screens, and the page refuses a second one itself while a trade or a publish is running. After a FAST action the amount field holds the page's own remainder (only what is surely untraded), or nothing.
- **Room ①** already guarded Start swap, Publish, Save, Withdraw, OTC and the ⇄ switch. Refund now and the wallet actions (Send, Export key, Consolidate) had none: they now run one at a time.
- Tests: `scripts/dexicon-ui.test.cjs` (four new: the page refuses what was not reviewed; a take cannot rest, one action at a time, the report; the ticket's book and `expectOf`; the native double click and the amount), `scripts/dexicon-ax.test.cjs` (one).

## [0.18.117] — A fill co-signed while the node stops is no longer lost (review, H3)

- **The fault.** The Dexicon engine was dropped the moment the node said "stopping". A maker's co-signed take writes its fill, tells the taker and reduces the order only after `txnpost` answers; an answer that arrived after the drop was refused, so the fill was on the chain and unrecorded, the taker was never told, and after the restart the order was backed again at full size.
- **Stop drains.** `dexicon.stop()` ends the timer and every new event at once and drops signers that are only queued, but drops the engine only when nothing of it is at the node or on its way to a relay (three quiet looks), or after 15 s; an unanswered `txnpost` is waited for beyond that. The node is told to quit only after that (`node-manager.stop()` → `onStopping`, 30 s at most).
- **An unanswered txnpost is recorded, not guessed.** When the node goes away without answering (anything but a refused connection), the service writes a fill with status `unknown` to disk at once, reduces the order as if it had been posted, and tells the taker neither "taken" nor "refused". The chain decides: an output coin of the trade exists = DONE; this node's own coin in it is unspent and out of the mempool ten minutes later, or nothing after two hours = LOST and the amount is put back on the order. History shows UNKNOWN with what it means.
- **AtomiX and PandaPools** have the same shape (a record written after the reply) but their engines are not dropped at a node stop; they now also finish what they have at the node before `quit` (`quiet()`, 10 s at most). Not changed, and not re-examined: what each of those engines does with a `txnpost` of its own that is never answered.
- Not done: telling the taker "taken" once an unknown fill turns out to be on the chain (the reply needs the txpowid, which the node never gave).
- Tests: `scripts/dexicon-host.test.cjs` (four: the drain, its bound, unknown → done and unknown → lost across a restart, a refused connection), `scripts/keymode-test.cjs` (the drain comes before `quit`), `scripts/dexicon-ui.test.cjs` (the UNKNOWN chip).

## [0.18.116] — A failed or repeated wallet restore no longer sets the swap store aside (review, M2)

- **The fault.** `runResync` set every engine store aside whenever a wallet-replacing `megammrsync` (phrase or file) did not answer, also when the node was still running on the unchanged wallet. `atomix.sqlite` holds the claim secrets of swaps in flight; AtomiX then started again on an empty store. On the success path the three engines kept ticking between the node's wallet swap and its shutdown.
- **Held first.** Before such a command is sent, AtomiX, PandaPools and Dexicon are stopped and stay stopped (`hold()` in each host: no timer, no start by a node status, no action from a screen, and no signing command reaches the node; reads and `txndelete` still do, so a chain in flight ends cleanly; `hold()` waits up to 15 s for what is at the node). If they cannot be stopped the command is not sent.
- **Moved only on evidence.** The wallet is identified before and after (`node-manager.walletMark`: a digest of the node's one-way `seedrandom` derivation; the value is never kept or logged). Another wallet: the stores are set aside (never deleted) before the restarted node is called running, then the engines start on new ones. The same wallet (the same phrase again, a refused command, a timeout before any swap): every store stays where it is and the engines go on. Not readable: nothing is concluded, the engines stay stopped, asked again every 10 s.
- **A timeout with the node still running** leaves the check owed: megammrsync can outlast the 180 s RPC limit and swap the wallet afterwards. It is asked again on every health tick and at the next node start; when the wallet has changed the engines are held again and the stores set aside.
- **Still decided without a comparison:** a wallet that could not be identified before the command (a locked node). The stores are then set aside after a success and after an unknown result, as since 0.18.106.
- Tests: `scripts/keymode-test.cjs` (six, every branch: same wallet, another wallet, timed out and unchanged then swapped later, failed after the swap, node died with each outcome and unreadable, refused, engines that cannot be stopped), `scripts/engine-abort.test.cjs` (AtomiX and PandaPools held), `scripts/dexicon-host.test.cjs` (Dexicon held).

## [0.18.115] — Dexicon ① MxUSD: the halt card, the rescue tools and Market offline (Phase 3c)

- **The halt card** (`DoorsTab.paintRescue`). The engine's identity watch has always stopped new liabilities when the node no longer owns AtomiX's keys; the old desktop panel never showed it. `main/atomix.js` `status()` now carries the watch's verdict (`halt`, read only, null unless halted; every key and address whole), and room ① shows the card above Swap, Market and OTC and above the wallet in Assets: what stopped, that swaps in flight keep settling, the orphaned Minima key, the stale and the current Ethereum address, and the rescue before anything else.
- **Rescue tools**, when the Ethereum wallet is the stale one: Export the stale ETH key (the ordinary warning, then the key) and Sweep funds to the node wallet (the ordinary Send dialog with the node's current address filled in; the engine's checks and the review apply). The card says the stale key lives only while the app runs and must be exported before the app is quit or restarted.
- **While halted** Review swap, Publish, Save & publish, Go live, a proposal, Accept and Counter are refused on the screen with the reason. Refund now, Check now, Withdraw and Reject are never gated: settlement is not frozen.
- **Not built: a reset.** The phone offers "Start fresh"; the desktop has no call that forgets an orphaned identity without setting the swap store (and its claim secrets) aside, so the card offers none and says to ask for help after the rescue. `mcd:axInvalidate` is not used by any screen.
